# 7

->**Salutations to the glorious Ganesha** <note>original: Śrī-Gaṇeśāya namaḥ; Ganesha is the elephant-headed deity traditionally invoked at the start of any venture to remove obstacles</note><-

->**Now begins the Determination of the Victory of Kings** <note>original: Rājavijaya-nirṇaya</note><-

Having bowed to Him, the Creator Brahma joyfully creates the entire world; having bowed to Him, Vishnu protects it—I bow to that all-pervading Lord Shiva <note>original: Śaṅkara</note>. || 1 ||

Having bowed to Shiva, the Lord of the Universe and the cause of the world’s origin, I shall compose the work named **The Victory of Kings** according to the prescribed rules. || 2 ||

Here, indeed, with the desire for the welfare of all the world, a certain learned scholar, wishing to compose the book named **The Victory of Kings**, first describes the purification of the Five Limbs of the calendar for the successful accomplishment of desired goals.

### First, the Purification of the Five Limbs <term>Pancha-anga; the five components of the traditional Indian calendar (Almanac) used to determine auspicious timing</term>

The lunar day, the day of the week, the lunar mansion, the solar-lunar junction, and the "half-day" or division—these are declared to be the **Five Limbs** <note>original: tithi, vāra, nakṣatra, yoga, and karaṇa</note> to be considered for all undertakings. || 3 ||

<summary>This opening page of the manuscript invokes the deities Ganesha and Shiva before introducing a treatise on "The Victory of Kings," focusing on the importance of the five-fold astrological calendar (Panchanga) for ensuring successful outcomes.</summary>
<keywords>Ganesha, Shiva, Rajavijaya, Panchanga, Astrology, Auspicious timing, Tithi, Nakshatra</keywords>

--- Page 9 ---
<meta>This page follows a heavily damaged introductory folio that contained institutional seals. We are now at the beginning of the actual literary work, opening with the traditional Sanskrit "Mangalacharana" or auspicious invocation.</meta>

# Om! Prosperity! Salutations to the illustrious Ganesha. Now, the **Victory of the King** is written by the grace of the Giver of Boons.

<margin>
Ra.
Vi.
1
</margin>

May the elephant-faced god <note>original: "ibhananah," a name for Ganesha</note>—whose face is a blooming lotus, who acts as a sun to the hearts of the virtuous, and whose crown is marked by the crescent moon—grant us our heart's desire. (1) I bow to that Mother of the World, the beloved of the Lord of the Mountains <note>original: "Girishadayita," referring to the goddess Parvati</note>; by the grace of bowing to her pair of lotus feet, even the son of a mute man may surpass the eloquence of Brihaspati <note>The preceptor of the gods and the master of speech</note>. (2) [I bow to] that Lord who is the singular path to the knowledge of the three Vedas <note>original: "Trayi," the triad of the Rig, Yajur, and Sama Vedas</note>. (2) There was a King named Simha <note>original: "Simhabhupa," literally "Lion-King"</note>, whose lotus feet were painted by the mass of radiance from the jewels in the millions of crowns of all the kings who bowed before him. He was a forest-fire to the trees that were his rival kings, and a brilliant ray of light from the ocean of compassion. (3) ...a lion who destroyed the temples of the fearful elephants that were his enemies. (5) Victorious is that noble protector of the earth, the illustrious Lord Madana, a ruler who follows the path of the wise and is the unique banner for the lineage of King Pratipa. (6) By him, the one named Ranahastin <note>The author’s name, meaning "War-Elephant"</note>—who is a banner for the fortress of scholars—has been honored with many ornaments, attractive umbrellas, and fine horses; he now triumphs upon the earth. (7)

Having extracted the essence from the vastness of the oceans [of scripture] and following the doctrines of the Daughter of the Mountain <note>original: "Girija," another name for Parvati</note>, this **Victory of King Hara** <note>original: "Hararajavijaya"</note> is composed by **Ranahastin** for the success of those with simple minds. (8) In the history of this Lord of the Earth, whose world-conquest <note>term: Digvijaya—the ritual conquest of the four quarters of the earth</note> is well-known... (9) Endowed with the powers of energy, lordship, and counsel <note>term: Shaktis—the three traditional powers of a king: Utsaha (drive), Prabhu (majesty), and Mantra (counsel)</note>, the illustrious one is a lover of ethics <note>term: Niti—political science and moral conduct</note>, wise, and a delighter of his subjects. Observing the state of the world daily through the light of his steady conduct, this "tilaka" <note>The ornamental mark on the forehead, signifying the best of his kind</note> among the lords of the earth is the cause of prosperity. (10) ...he who serves the gods, the twice-born <note>term: Dvija—members of the upper three social classes, typically Brahmins in this context</note>, his gurus, and virtuous people... a universal emperor <note>term: Chakravartin—a ruler whose chariot wheels roll everywhere without obstruction</note> who has destroyed all calamities...

<summary>This is the opening page of the Hararajavijaya, a Sanskrit courtly epic (Kavya). It begins with invocations to Ganesha and Parvati, introduces the patron King Simhabhupa (and his successor Madana), and identifies the author as Ranahastin.</summary>
<keywords>Sanskrit Kavya, Ranahastin, King Simhabhupa, Hararajavijaya, Ganesha, Parvati, Niti, Digvijaya</keywords>

--- Page 10 ---
<meta>This page continues from the introductory verses where the author, Ranahastin, praised his patron, King Madana. We now move from the poetic invocation into the core philosophical and practical justification for the "Science of Breath" (Svarodaya) in statecraft and warfare. The numbering in the original manuscript is inconsistent, with the number 18 appearing twice and 19 being omitted.</meta>

Just as a lamp provides **light in the darkness**, so too is the knowledge of *Svara* <note>original: "svara," referring here to the "science of breath" or the flow of vital air through the nostrils</note> for kings; without a knower of breath-flow, the **Glory of Victory** <note>original: "Jayashri," victory personified as a goddess</note> cannot be attained in battle. A king who sets out on a journey without considering the alignment of his breath, even if he is filled with fury, cannot succeed. Yet, a single man, empowered by the strength of his breath, can conquer a host of enemies, just as **Partha** <note>A title for the hero Arjuna from the *Mahabharata*</note> defeated the army of the **Kauravas**. (15)

The lord of the earth who wishes to forcibly conquer enemy warriors, but disregards the words of those who know the breath, immediately sinks—along with his terrified army—into a deep ocean of grief, as if consumed by fire; his mountains of pride are utterly shattered. (16)

If there is even one expert in the **Science of Breath** <term>Svarodaya: a system of divination based on the rhythm and flow of breath through the left and right nostrils</term> among the soldiers, he protects the kingdom by his wisdom. Without a knower of breath-flow, victory for a king is difficult to obtain in this world, even when his army possesses great strength. (18) <note>The manuscript skips verse 17 and jumps to 18.</note>

This science was spoken by the **Supreme Lord** <note>original: "Parameshvara," usually referring to Shiva</note> in many scriptures on Yoga and other disciplines. For those who practice this with controlled senses and speak the truth, the calculations found here will prove entirely accurate. Therefore, for a king who follows these instructions, the **Glory of Victory** shall remain stable. Even the most courageous men, if they rely only on the strength of their two arms in battle, are like those falling into a forest fire when they enter the great "Chakra" <note>An astrological or military wheel-diagram</note> without knowing their breath. (18) <note>Number 18 is repeated in the original text.</note>

If a king proceeds without knowing the breath, as if he were ignorant, his kingdom quickly moves toward ruin. Victory does not belong to those who do not respect gods or **Brahmins** <note>The priestly and scholarly class</note>, regardless of the strength of their location or timing. (20)

The foolish man who takes for himself the property of the gods or the wealth of a Brahmin, desiring his own growth, shall instead meet his destruction. A king should remain on the path of justice and rely upon the **Seer** <term>Daivajña: an astrologer or one who knows the divine will</term>; thus he attains fame in this life and the righteous worlds in the next.

### The Opening of the Book

**Thus ends the Preface of the Book.** 

Now, I shall state the essence of the preface and the sequence of the work. First, one should know the "Hundred-Step Diagram." Then follows the sequence of the **Chakras** <note>Mystical diagrams or charts used for divination</note>: 
*   The method of the **Tortoise** (*Kurma-chakra*)
*   The diagram of the **Snake of Breath-Knowledge**
*   The diagram for the **Lion-Throne**
*   The instrument of the **Seven Hills**
*   The **Fly-whisk** diagram
*   The **Auspicious-in-all-Directions** (*Sarvatobhadra*) diagram
*   The **Horse** diagram and the categories of strength
*   The **Array** (*Prastara*) at the end 
*   The **Piercing** (*Vedha*) and the classes of power
*   The **Wheel of Power** and the diagram named after **Brahma** (*Viranchi*)
*   The revered pair of **Mother-matrices**
*   The **Victory** diagram and the **Elephant** diagram
*   The **Chariot** diagram and the diagram of the **Sun-limbs** 
*   The **Unconquerable** diagram and its two-fold nature. (1)

<summary>This page establishes the vital importance of "Svarodaya" (the science of breath) for military success, warning that even a strong army will fail without it. It concludes the introduction and provides a table of contents listing various mystical diagrams (chakras) to be discussed.</summary>

<keywords>Svarodaya, Science of Breath, Jayashri, military astrology, Partha, Kauravas, Chakras, Sarvatobhadra, kingship, divination</keywords>

--- Page 11 ---
<meta>This page continues from the introductory section where the importance of the Science of Breath (Svarodaya) was established. It now transitions into an "Anukramani" or a systematic index of the various divinatory diagrams (Chakras) and chapters that will be detailed in the manuscript. The numbering suggests a structured guide for a king's practitioners to reference specific ritual or astrological tools.</meta>

<margin>
Ra.
Vi.
2
<note>Likely an abbreviation for the title "Ranahastivijaya" and the page number 2.</note>
</margin>

The **diagram** <note>original: "chakram," a complex circular chart or symbolic wheel used for divination</note> is filled with beautiful families of symbols, accompanied by all the spirits of the dead. It proceeds through the positions of the Sun and the Moon <note>original: "Arka-shashanka," the sun and moon are central to breath-science</note> in a fierce sequence, beginning with the signs of the zodiac and their active spirits. In the first section, the arrangement of the "Vilocaka" <note>An obscure technical term, likely a specific type of viewing or alignment</note> points is described at the start of the work. (3)

One should know the **Pancogaraja** <term>Pancogaraja: literally "King of the Five Limbs," likely referring to a diagram involving the five elements or five specific astrological factors</term>; then comes the knowledge of the divisions of the sun's rays within it. Following that, the "Mallattura" <note>original: "mallattura," possibly a local or technical name for a specific diagrammatic arrangement</note> is explained. In the third section, the preferred **Triple-Vein Diagram** <note>original: "Trainadya-chakra," relating to the three main channels of vital energy: Ida, Pingala, and Sushumna</note> is described, as well as the **Twelve-Spoked Diagram** <note>original: "Dvadashara-chakra"</note>. Following that, the diagram of the lunar mansions <note>original: "Nakshatra-chakra"</note> is known for its many results, as if awakened from sleep.

From the expansion of these, the "Madanavahira" <note>likely a diagram named after a specific deity or king</note> and others are described for the purpose of the march. Then I shall speak of the "Mrigana," the "Yamaya," and others beyond those. (5) Next, I shall describe the **Diagram of Star-Houses** <note>original: "Nakshatra-bhava-chakra"</note> and the internal divisions within it. Then the "Cakravaracena" and the "Hripanta" are detailed, followed by the "Vadrasharasatta." (6)

I shall then describe the diagrams for **weapons, banners, and hearts** <note>original: "Shastra-dhvaja-hridaya-chakrani"</note>, and the characteristics of the "Yamallattana." Through the "Pancogaka" method, one arrives at the excellent fourth chapter titled **The Root** <note>original: "Mula-adhyaya"</note>. (7) In the fifth chapter, I shall speak of the spirits <note>original: "Bhutas"</note> using the **Arya meter** <note>A specific rhythmic structure in Sanskrit poetry</note>, providing results consistent with their names; here, the **Great Destroyer** <note>original: "Mahamari," a fierce goddess often associated with protection against or the cause of plagues and defeat</note> should be understood. (8)

Thereafter, the "Ttrapalika" and the "Bhaurau-dra-parattasta" are mentioned, followed by the description of the "Kalagewa." Next, the "Mantra-prayoga" <note>the practical application of sacred chants</note> and the "Yogoshi" are detailed, alongside the expansion of **Chandika** <note>The fierce, protective form of the Goddess</note>. Then comes the "Bhadra-chaturanga" <note>a "four-limbed" or auspicious diagram often related to army formations</note> for deception, followed by the "Kamakhya." After that is the "Shahuli," and then the **Victory over the Three Worlds** <note>original: "Trailokyavijaya," a common name for highly potent diagrams or mantras intended to ensure total dominance</note>. (10)

I shall also speak of the "Kalapasa" <note>The Noose of Time/Death</note>, to be known through its categories, followed by the diagram named **The Fire of Rahu’s Time** <note>original: "Rahukalanala-chakra," referring to the destructive energy of the eclipse demon Rahu</note>. Then comes the "Svaramyarga" and the "Ajna" <note>Command</note>. (11) Further on, the "Mahute" and the "Sheva" should be known, as well as the "Kulikarahopra." Then follow the diagrams of the Sun’s orb and the Earth. Beyond those are the **Yoginis** <term>Yoginis: here, sixty-four semi-divine female spirits whose presence dictates auspicious or inauspicious directions for travel and battle</term> of three types, categorized by the lunar day, the fire, the planets, and the immortals.

Beyond that, the "Nariri" is to be known, and then the "Jnathi" which leads to victory. This sequence has been stated in the fifth section. Further, I shall speak of the "Raga-vako-ragga" and the "Shastradi-jnana-kankaga" in the chapter concerning the heart, ending with the "Vaijayakhya." I shall speak next of the "Patagramonjika." (15) Beyond that is the "Samudra" <note>The Ocean diagram</note>.

<summary>This page provides a detailed table of contents or index (Anukramani) for the manuscript, listing various mystical diagrams (chakras) and chapters dedicated to specific divinatory tools, deities like Chandika and Mahamari, and astrological influences used to ensure victory.</summary>
<keywords>Table of Contents, Chakras, Divination Diagrams, Trailokyavijaya, Rahu, Yoginis, Astrology, Military Rituals</keywords>

--- Page 12 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the general index of diagrams (Chakras) into specific ritual procedures and the foundational theory of "Svara" (vital breath/vowels). The text concludes a technical list of battlefield tools—such as banners and ointments—before shifting into a philosophical justification for these practices, citing the duty of kings and the legendary King Janamejaya. It then begins a detailed explanation of phonetics as the basis for divination.</meta>

The **system of other traditions** <note>original: "parantantrātmakaṃ"</note> and the **Mark of Distinction** <note>original: "tilakaṃ"</note> should be known as the **Hundred-fold Stream** <note>original: "śatadruḥ"</note>. Beyond that, one finds the **ritual pills** <term>gutika: small consecrated pellets used for medicinal or magical protection</term>, the elaboration of the teachings, and the **scientific applications** <note>original: "śāstra-parikriyā"</note>. These include the characteristics of weapons, techniques for crushing and breaking, the application of medicinal ointments, and the arrangement of **incantations** <note>original: "mantra"</note>. This also encompasses the ritual of the **Great Horn** <note>original: "kāhalāñcana-vidhiṃ"</note>, the protective breastplate, and the rules for the ritual meal. (18)

In that sequence, I shall arrange the **Banner** <note>original: "patākāṃ"</note>, the **Peacock-feather Bundle** <note>original: "piñchicā"</note>, and other ritual tools such as the **Pacification Rite** <note>original: "śāntika"</note>, the **Gateway** <note>original: "pratoraṇa"</note>, and the one named the **Standard of Victory** <note>original: "dhvaja"</note>.

In this sixth section concerning the **Victory of the Elephant King** <note>original: "Gaja-hastirāja-vijaya"</note>, this sequence of diagrams <note>original: "yantra-kramo"</note> has been arranged by the wise. It is highly effective for those submerged in the power of the mantras, even in complex or confused situations. Thus, the application of this work is revealed for the benefit of the practitioner. (18) This tradition is carried forward through the **instruction of the Guru** <note>original: "gurupadeśa"</note> by those who know the secrets of the breath-sounds. It is intended to turn back misfortune in battle. It is known as the **Elephant-King of the Mountain** <note>original: "sādra-gajahasti" system</note>. (19) <insert>Thus ends the Index of Diagrams.</insert>

For kings, the highest duty is the protection of their subjects; to punish those who deserve it is their righteousness <note>original: "dharma"</note>. Just as **King Janamejaya** <note>A legendary king from the Mahabharata known for his powerful rituals</note> achieved fame through his own strength, so too does one find the description of the **vowels** <term>svara: refers both to grammatical vowels and the flow of breath through the nostrils</term> in this "Victory of the King." (1)

Therefore, I shall now describe the **natural vowels** in brief, through which one may know what is auspicious or inauspicious across all time. The five vowels beginning with **'A'** are short; with the long vowels, they become six. These sixteen are said to be the **Mothers of the Vowels** <note>original: "svara-mātṛkā"</note>. I shall now speak of their specific measures. (2)

Among them, the four vowels are paired, and the vowels are joined in tens. Among these, five are short; these measures of the vowels should be employed through the "staff" of the breath-flow. (3) The measure, the letter, the category, the **life-force** <note>original: "jīva"</note>, the mantra, the success, and the "mass" <note>original: "piṇḍaka"</note> of the Elephant—these eight are the markers of distinction. This is what is said regarding the success of the vowels. (4)

One who knows this arrives quickly even from a distance. Therefore, one should protect the world of mortals. In the first category, there is the **measure** <note>original: "mātrā"</note>, known as the **Jewel of Weapons**. <unclear>ro vā svatrāvaḍahagā vasmitā stiryākiṃ</unclear> One should establish these in the first cells of the diagram. One should write the five vowels and letters in sequence, starting with the letter 'A' within the supreme **Wheel** <note>original: "cakra-varaṃ"</note>...

<summary>This page concludes the table of contents for ritual diagrams and military preparations, transitioning into the "Svarodaya" system which uses phonetic vowels as a divinatory tool for kings to ensure victory and protect their realm.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of phonetic divination (Svara Śāstra), moving from the general theory of sound-grids into specific planetary assignments, the classification of vowels and consonants, and the transition into the calculation of astronomical time.</meta>

Rā.

3
</margin>

In whichever **cell** <term>koṣṭha: a square or compartment in a divination grid</term> the first letter of a name is placed, its **similar-sounding letters** <note>original: "savarṇa"</note> are also considered to belong there. This includes conjunct characters and those that are repeated; at the end of the cell, one should reflect upon them in this manner. (7)

In the first five cells, one should place **Mars** <note>original: "Dvitija"</note>, **Mercury** <note>original: "Vid"</note>, **Jupiter** <note>original: "Ijya"</note>, **Saturn** <note>original: "Arkaputra"</note>, and **Saturn** <note>original: "Arkaja"</note> again. Then, the **Sun** <note>original: "Dineśa"</note> and the **Moon** <note>original: "Śītakiraṇa"</note> follow. Again, in two more cells, the **Zodiac signs** <note>original: "rāśīnyathākramenā"</note> should be known in their proper sequence. This system, known for its auspicious results, was spoken by **Shri Shubhasha** <note>likely the author or an authority cited: Śrī Śubhaśāsa</note>. The first group is said to consist of sixteen vowels. The groups beginning with 'Ka' are five-fold, each containing five letters, as declared by the ancient sages. <margin>3</margin>

Having determined the value of the name's letters, one should divide by five. Based on the **remainder** <note>original: "aṅkaśeṣato"</note>, the wise one should announce the results. (10) By the vowels of the zodiac signs, the first **nine-fold divisions** <term>navāṃśa: a one-ninth division of a zodiac sign used in Vedic astrology</term> are determined; for the remaining parts, the Moon, the Sun, and others are used. In this way, by the division of the name into nine parts, the five vowels become the lords. The stars beginning with **Revati** <note>the 27th Nakshatra</note> are grouped in fives, following the order of **Punarvasu** <note>the 7th Nakshatra</note>. The **Sound of the Star** <note>original: "uḍusvara"</note> is declared by the greatest of sages through the vowels. By the combination of the **vital breaths** <term>prāṇa: in phonetics, this refers to the aspiration or 'breath-force' of a letter</term>, the weight of the syllables, and the vowels, the "Ready Sound" <note>original: "sajjasvara"</note> is formed in the sixth group. Thus, the total perfection of sounds is achieved. The union of these sounds must be accomplished through natural reasoning. (13)

One should determine the vowels sequentially according to their duration. These natural sounds, which are well-known, are like the signs of the birth-constellation. In the combination of vowels, one should achieve success through the power of the first letter. These verses <note>original: "gāthā"</note> apply even in other lands. (14)

**Thus ends the Calculation of Natural Vowels**

For the delight of one's own people and the King's wife, and to illuminate auspicious results, I now state the **Measurement of Time** <note>original: "kālamānam"</note> for the sake of the virtuous. (15) Having understood the **mean motion** <term>madhyama-mata: the calculation of planetary positions based on average speed rather than true observation</term>, consisting of years, months, days, and minutes, one should calculate the "corrected" year with precise instruments. (16)

Subtracting the years by the three-fold actions, and multiplying by the "eyes of the kings" <note>cryptic numerical notation, possibly 216</note>, then multiplying by 163 and dividing by 877... the resulting remainder is 49 and the divisor is 80.

<meta>This page continues from the planetary assignments of the sound-grid, transitioning into the calculation of time-lords (years, solstices, months, and lunar days) that govern the Five Vowels (Pañcasvara) system.</meta>

Through the influence of the established expansion (1), the combination proceeds from the twelve-year cycle. From the origin, the **aggregate** <term>piṇḍa: the total or "body" of the calculation</term> is formed by the years, the **lunar mansions** <term>nakṣatra: the 27 star clusters of the zodiac</term>, the **solstice** <term>ayana: the six-month northern or southern path of the sun</term>, the zodiac signs, the lords of the seven vowels, and the signs of the months.

The **breath** <term>prāṇa: used here as a subtle unit of time or vital energy</term>, the five lunar days, the day-lord, the **vowel duration** <term>mātrā svara: the temporal length of a vowel</term>, and the sequence of **time-units** <term>nāḍikā: a period of 24 minutes</term> are declared as the seventh in the sequence of time. (18) 

Having divided the sixty years into five groups, the **vowels** <note>original: "svara"</note> are applied to these five-fold combinations. In the same way, the **Lord of the Total** <note>original: "piṇḍanātha"</note> for the five-year periods was spoken by those who possess knowledge of time for the sake of clarifying the intended meaning. (19)

Whether in the **Southern** <note>original: "yāmya"</note> or **Northern** <note>original: "saumya"</note> solstice, one must recognize the faults or strengths according to the tradition. The lordship over the **fortnight** <term>pakṣa: the two-week periods of the waxing or waning moon</term> is determined in this manner. (20)

Furthermore, in the bright fortnight and others, the vowel is determined by the **fifth part** <note>original: "pañcamabhāga"</note> of the duration. One should understand the vowel lords in the divisions of the signs. (21)

In the months of **Mārgaśīrṣa** <note>roughly November-December</note> and **Bhādrapada** <note>roughly August-September</note>, or when the vowel lords are without measure in the **Śravaṇa** <term>nakṣatra</term>, they are proclaimed by the masters as being situated within the heat. (22)

The sound 'U' is the cause, and 'M' is the silent designation. The sound 'R' pervades, and the 'U' sound is seen as the lotus. Here, the lords of the fifth are the vowels which become the masters of the month in their proper sequence. (23)

Wherever the first vowel occurs, that is the power for the sequence of the lunar days starting with **Nanda** <note>the 1st, 6th, and 11th lunar days</note>. This was declared by the sages regarding the dates and the days of the week. (24)

By these measured **time-units** <note>nāḍikā</note>, five in sequence, the 'A' and 'I' sounds arise. This is the daily vowel on this day, alternating with the measured vowel. (25)

By five **nāḍikās** <note>approx. 2 hours total</note> in sequence, the vowel shines in the five **ancestral divisions** <note>original: "pitṛda"</note>. That is called the **ghatikā-vowel** <term>ghatikā: a unit of time equal to 24 minutes</term> by the wise. (26)

In this way, these vowels, joined with their predecessors, provide the increase or decrease of the fruits of one's own actions. I have described this according to the opinion of the King; it is the **science of phonetic dawning** <term>svarodaya: the study of breath and sound for divination</term> and nothing else. (27)

Thus, the wise one who knows the occurrences born of their own time through proper calculation, even in the passing of **ages** <term>yuga: a vast cosmic epoch</term>, his speech shall never be false, just as his measurement remains true. (28) 

**The Five Vowels** <note>original: "Pañcasvara"</note>

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of the Swarodaya system, a form of divination based on the sounds of the breath and vowels. Specifically, it details the five *Avasthas* (states or ages) that a vowel or sound can inhabit—Childhood, Youth, Adulthood, Old Age, and Death—and how these states determine the success or failure of actions like battle, travel, or the chanting of mantras.</meta>

In this way, through the distinctions of the spoken vowels, their sequential order arises. They are known as **Childhood**, **Youth**, **Adulthood**, **Old Age**, and **Death**. (29) Regarding the state of **Death** <note>original: "mṛtyu"</note>, it is spoken of as the first; the second is **Old Age**; the third is **Adulthood**; the fourth is **Youth**; and the fifth is designated as **Childhood**. These are calculated by those learned in the science using a reverse counting method. (30)

When these vowels are applied, they yield results according to their sequence. A vowel in the **Childhood** state <note>original: "bala"</note> brings wealth, while others may lead to destruction or even death. (31) Through one's own actions, the fruits of success or great effort are realized here according to the prescribed path. (32)

The **Child** state is stronger than the **Death** state; the **Old** state is stronger than the **Child**; the **Youth** state is stronger than the **Old**; and the **Adult** state is the strongest of all. (33)

This hierarchy determines gain and loss. In battle, the **Death** and **Old** states are considered weak and inauspicious. The **Adult** state <term>taruṇa: meaning "vigorous" or "peak maturity"</term> among its own letters acts like a king over enemies; even when a person has lost their strength, they regain their prowess through this state, just as the moon regains its light. (34) In the fire of conflict, victory and defeat are decided; the **Adult** state brings victory, while the **Youth** state may face opposition, though it is sometimes auspicious. (35)

One should examine the four vowels that have arisen and establish their positions. Through the combination of these vowels, the twelve houses and their results are determined. At various times, one should observe these natural states to see if they are praiseworthy or not. (36-37)

In matters of **travel** <term>yātrā: a formal journey or military expedition</term>, **war** <term>yuddha</term>, or performing **harsh deeds** <note>original: "krūra karma"</note>, the **Child** state is generally considered favorable for all. The **Youth** state <note>original: "kaumāra"</note> is best for auspicious desires, as it leads to the successful completion of one's goals. (38) On firm ground, the **Adult** state is powerful for harsh tasks, ensuring success in travel and war. However, the **Old** state is inauspicious for travel and war; if one attempts to master **mantras** <term>mantra: sacred chants or formulas</term> during this time, the results will be fruitless. (39)

One should not perform any desired auspicious or beneficial work when a vowel is in the **Death** state. Teachers say that this state produces only death and ruin. (40) By joining the right combinations, one attains the body of success without effort. Whether in travel, in the village, or in the city, the **Total Body** <term>piṇḍa: the mathematical aggregate used in these calculations</term> should be considered. Success or obstacles are determined by the stars and the influence of these states.

...the performance of coral-related work <note>original: "saṃpravāḍā-" referring to coral or similar trade</note>, the commencement of travel, and the auspicious entry into a new house or village. It is also for the worship of deities, for achieving success in installations <note>original: "sthāpane," likely referring to the installation of idols</note>, and for ceremonies of initiation, asceticism, and consecration. It applies further to the construction of residences and palaces. (42)

The wearing of auspicious ornaments, the timing of coronations, and the beginning of various name-giving ceremonies or strengthening rituals should also be undertaken when the vowel is in a "living" state. For those who seek success, the hand of the wise practitioner avoids confusion and heat <note>original: "vibhrahastambhamohā-," likely meaning avoiding the paralysis of doubt or failure</note>, ensuring that the work is successfully completed.

In the science of the **Arising of Sounds and Letters** <term>Svarodaya: the ancient practice of using breath and phonemes for divination and timing</term>, one should perform all actions—whether they lead to good or bad results—according to the rising of the vowels. This is especially true for travel and for battle, as declared by the Lord himself. (44)

Whatever has been spoken by the ancient texts for the purpose of purification, and whatever worldly customs have been established, all should be performed in accordance with the measures of the vowels and their sounds. Whether it be the lunar day, the rising sign, or the mathematical combinations of days and periods <note>original: "tithau vādhilagna-gaṇita-dinayoga-karaṇaiḥ"</note>, these remembered auspicious times yield their fruits only when the vowels of the person's name or lineage are in the correct state.

Because of this, the mathematical method for the vowels has been given. By knowing the clear instruments for calculating the points of influence <note>original: "vedha," a technical term for the intersection or piercing of astrological points</note>, one discovers the results. In this calculation, one should divide the units of time. 

One should take the duration of the lunar day, converted into **Ghatikas** and **Palas** <term>Ghatika and Pala: traditional Indian units of time; a Ghatika is 24 minutes, and a Pala is 24 seconds</term>. Using the mathematical factors of the Sun, one should divide the remaining portions to find the specific vowel sound. From this, the results—for sacrifices and other rites—should be known. The wise practitioner should purify these elements; thus, a king or leader is not defeated but rather achieves victory over others.

As previously stated, the mastery of the strength of the letters is achieved through the **Diagrams** <note>original: "chakra"</note>. In the **Bright Fortnight** <term>Shukla Paksha: the period of the waxing moon</term>, these are particularly powerful during the day. In this diagram, the vowels and their respective powers are united. When these powers are unified in the bright fortnight, the diagram becomes a giver of victory. 

Regarding the naming of a person, the first letter is paramount. When combined with the calculated day and lunar day, it reveals the time of potential destruction. Through the divisions of the **Hour** <note>original: "hora"</note> and the **Ninth-parts** <note>original: "navabhāga," or Navamsha</note>, the "Child" state of the vowel <note>original: "bala-svara"</note> and others are determined. 

From the moment of sunrise, according to the duration of each vowel’s influence, this applies to the fortnight, the month, and the passage of years. Even in a cycle of twelve years, the transformation of the vowel state occurs. The timing of the rising sign and the units of the **Ghati** are thus essential for calculation...

By this method, within the two cycles of the vowels <note>original: "dārhidat-dvaye," possibly referring to the solar and lunar cycles</note>, the sequence of the six-day periods and the course of the year are consumed in order. The duration of the vowel's influence and the portion of the "lump" <term>Piṇḍa: here referring to the total collective units of time or life-force</term> are divided to find the current state. From the remaining time, the specific state is determined. (55)

The twelve states for **Death** <note>mṛtā-avasthā</note> are: 1. Dead, 2. Power, 3. Infant, 4. [Missing/Unclear], 5. Maiden, 6. Young Woman, 7. King, 8. Distress, 9. Liberation, 10. Fever, 11. Giver, 12. Dead. (56)

The twelve states for the **Child** <note>bālaka-avasthā</note> are: 1. Healthy, 2. Auspicious, 3. Deluded, 4. Angry, 5. Intelligent, 6. Great Success, 7. Peaceful, 8. Proud, 9. Intoxicated, 10. Equanimity, 11. Youthful, 12. Auspicious. (57)

For the **Prince** <note>kumāra-avasthā</note>, the states involve: 1. Killer, 2. Patient, 3. Resonant <note>original: "guñjā"</note>, 4. Yellowish/Robust, 5. Determined, 6. Engaged, 7. Lustful, 8. Content, 9. Successful/Prosperous, 10. [Missing], 11. Wealthy, 12. Fearless/Peaceful. 

When the twelfth falls on a Thursday: (58) 1. Indecisive, 2. Wasting, 3. Middle, 4. [Missing], 5. Blessed with sons, 6. Residing/Staying, 7. Sleeping, 8. Wise, 9. Radiant, 10. Afflicted, 11. Feverish, 12. Dead. (59)

The states born of **Old Age** <note>vṛddhaja-avasthā</note> are: 1. Severed, 2. Wanton, 3. Knowing the enemy, 4. Remaining, 5. Great, 6. [Missing], 7. Giving pain, 8. Scarred, 9. Creating division, 10. Skillful, 11. Dead, 12. Forgiving. (60)

The sixth state is the "steady" state... <note>The text here describes the internal variations of these states</note> In this way, one should understand the results of all these states through the sound of the name. (61)

**The Results of the Birth-Vowel:**
The vowel active at the moment of birth determines the "pulse" <note>nāḍī-svara</note> of one's nature. 

If the **Child Vowel** <term>Bāla-svara: the vowel 'A' in its earliest stage</term> is rising at birth: the person will be fickle, timid, a liar, miserly, lacking self-control, untruthful, and very talkative.

If the **Prince Vowel** <term>Kumāra-svara: the vowel 'I'</term> is rising: the person is a professional, a connoisseur of the arts, fond of women, always fortunate, long-limbed, and brave. 

He who is born under this rising becomes a King, possessing all auspicious marks, and is always victorious in battle. 

If the **Young Man Vowel** <term>Yuva-svara: the vowel 'U'</term> is rising: the person is joined with women, religious, desirous, wise, steady in courage, truthful, and of good conduct. (66)

If the **Death Vowel** <term>Mṛta-svara: the vowel 'O'</term> is rising at birth: the person is cruel throughout the year, impaired in body or senses, lazy in all tasks, and dull-witted. (67)

This has been explained from the *Union of the Six Suns* <note>original: "Ṣaḍarka-yāmala," a lost or rare Tantric work on divination</note> through the grace of the Guru. I shall now speak of the results of the "Arising of the Vowel," which illuminates one's karma. The "Union of the Lump" <note>yoga-piṇḍa</note> and the life force... by the power of penance, the measure of the syllables should be applied in sequence.

<meta>This page continues the technical mathematical calculations for determining the strength and effects of vowels over time-cycles. It transitions from the Svarachakra (Vowel Circle) system to the Svarodaya (Rising of Breath/Sound) system, introducing the physiological and yogic aspects of breath and energy.</meta>

One should write out the eight-year and twelve-year cycles, combined with one's own period of experience <term>bhoga: the duration of a vowel's active influence</term>. The calculation involves dividing by eleven parts to find the strength of the vowel. From the experience, the name of the vowel and the "Time-Rising" vowel <note>original: "kālodayī-svara"</note> are identified. This is known through the four Vedas, with the vowel 'A' serving as the basis. By following the reverse order, the twenty-fold calculation <term>viṃśopaka: a unit of measure representing 1/20th, used here to quantify auspiciousness</term> results in seventy-two units. The eyes of the Vedas and their limbs <note>a reference to the Vedangas or auxiliary sciences</note> are then counted in the sequence of the Vedas. One brings forth the vowels starting with 'A' and calculates from the "Child" stage onward once more. (72–74)

In the twelve-year cycles, the vowels starting with 'A' are situated; among them, five states are inauspicious and seven are auspicious. (74) In the first half of the time, there is growth and strength; the remaining half is to be divided to distinguish between the good and the bad. The twelve-year cycles are marked by their internal intervals. In these, the auspicious ones should be placed on one side and the inauspicious on the other. The nature of the auspicious and inauspicious results is known through the signs <note>zodiacal signs</note> and planets. Subtracting from the total, the remainder reveals the fruit of that particular subject. When multiplied by twenty and divided by sixty, the remaining *vimsopakas* act as indicators of the auspicious or inauspicious outcome. (78)

The "Lump" <term>Piṇḍa: the physical body or the total sum of elements</term> is born of one's own karma; within this body, one should understand the influence of the planets. Within the vowels of the zodiac signs, the "Soul" <note>jīva</note> is active. The letters <note>varṇāḥ</note> should be understood as the measurement of that soul. The result for women is determined through the "measurements" <note>mātrā</note>. Knowing this, O Goddess, one can perceive the auspicious and inauspicious nature of the day. These vowels are said to provide results equal to the passage of years. Likewise, their fruit should be grasped at the time of inquiry based on one's own "rising" breath. (82)

In the village or in any situation, including death, through the fifty variations of one's own group <note>the vargas or categorized letters of the alphabet</note>, the different results are spoken. There, the different vowels shine forth like water. (82) This has been described in many ways by those with varied intelligence. Within the doctrine of the "Branch of Names," the sages have determined this: just as a small boat can cross the ocean, so too does this knowledge help one cross the difficulties of life. (83)

Thus ends the **Circle of Vowels** <note>Svarachakra</note>.

### The Awakening of the Breath within the Body

Now I shall clearly explain the *Svarodaya* <term>Svarodaya: the science of the rising breath or sound</term> as it exists within the physical body. By understanding the movement of the "Swan" <term>Haṃsa-cāra: the rhythmic movement of the breath (inhale/exhale)</term>, one gains knowledge of the three times <note>past, present, and future</note>. (1)

The Kundalini power is situated at the navel, having the form of a coiled serpent. Her mouth is pointed upward, and she resides within. Through the various channels, she moves in twenty-one thousand <note>referring to the 21,600 breaths a human takes in a day</note> ways. This great power, the Kundalini, starts from the root path...

# The Subtle Channels and the Ten Vital Airs

From these main centers, subtle channels <term>nadis: conduits of life-force or prana</term> emerge, which exist throughout the body to provide it with deep nourishment. It is taught that there are seven hundred <note>The text here mentions 700 main channels, though it later references the traditional 72,000</note> primary ones. Among these, ten channels are the most significant as they act as the pathways for the ten vital airs. I shall now name these channels and the winds that flow through them.

The ten major channels are:
1. **Ida**
2. **Pingala**
3. **Sushumna**
4. **Gandhari**
5. **Hastijivhika**
6. **Pusha**
7. **Yashasvini** <note>OCR reads "ayasa," likely a corruption of Yashasvini</note>
8. **Alambusha** <note>OCR reads "paleka"</note>
9. **Kuhu**
10. **Shankhini**

From these primary conduits, seventy thousand <note>original: "saptati-sahasrakam," usually interpreted as the 72,000 nadis of Hatha Yoga tradition</note> subtle channels branch out. 

### The Ten Breaths
The vital air manifests in ten forms within the body:
*   **Prana** <note>the forward-moving breath</note>
*   **Apana** <note>the downward-moving breath</note>
*   **Samana** <note>the balancing or digestive breath</note>
*   **Udana** <note>the upward-moving breath</note>
*   **Vyana** <note>the pervasive breath</note>
*   **Naga** <note>responsible for belching</note>
*   **Kurma** <note>responsible for blinking</note>
*   **Krikara** <note>responsible for sneezing/hunger</note>
*   **Devadatta** <note>responsible for yawning</note>
*   **Dhananjaya** <note>which remains even after death</note>

The movement of the **Prana** is clearly perceived within the center of the body. Among the channels, **Ida**, **Pingala**, and **Sushumna** are the third and most vital trio. **Ida** and **Pingala** carry the solar and lunar energies. **Sushumna** is the nature of the "Hamsa" <term>Hamsa: literally "Swan," a mantra representing the sound of the breath</term>, indicating the very origin of the breath's rise. With the sound *Ham*, the breath exits; this is the masculine/solar principle. With the sound *Sah*, the energy enters; this is the feminine/Shakti principle. (9)

For the practitioner, the **Moon** (lunar energy) flows through the left nostril <note>original: "vama-nadi"</note>, while the **Sun** (solar energy) flows through the right nostril. Each of these flows possesses five "digits" or divisions <term>kala: a unit of time or measurement</term>, which correspond to the five elements: **Earth**, **Water**, **Fire**, **Air**, and **Ether**.

### The Flow of the Elements
The movement of the breath reveals which element is currently dominant:
*   **Earth** <note>Prithvi</note> flows in the middle.
*   **Fire** <note>Tejas</note> moves upward.
*   **Air** <note>Vayu</note> moves transversely (sideways).
*   **Ether** <note>Akasha</note> moves in the transition or crossover between the others.

Each element rules for a specific duration <term>ghatika: a period of 24 minutes</term>. Throughout a full day and night (24 hours), there are twelve such transitions of the breath. 

### Characteristics of the Elements
One can recognize the elements by their color, length, and shape:
*   **Air** is perceived as blue or smoky in color <note>original: "nila-varna"</note>.
*   **Fire** is red in color and transformative.
*   **Earth** is superior and yellow in color.
*   **Water** is white in color.

Regarding their forms: **Air** is said to be triangular; **Earth** is quadrangular (square); and **Water** is circular. In terms of taste, **Air** is pungent; **Fire** is bitter; **Earth** is astringent; and **Water** is considered sweet. 

By placing the fingers over the face—the thumbs on the ears and the fingers over the eyes and nostrils—one can contemplate these six movements of the breath and nostril transitions. Through this, the knowledge of the elements <term>tattva-jnana</term> arises in sequence: the yellow, the white, the red, and the dark...

<meta>This page transitions from the anatomical description of the subtle channels (nadis) to the practical application of the Swarodaya system. It focuses on how the flow of breath through the left (Moon) or right (Sun) nostril dictates the success of specific worldly actions, conflict outcomes, and the results of divination queries (Prashna).</meta>

### The Rhythm of the Lunar and Solar Breath

For the unconditioned practice, from the first day of the waxing moon <note>original: "shukla-pratipada," the first day after the new moon</note>, the breath should first flow through the Moon channel <term>Chandra-nadi: the left nostril, associated with Ida</term>. At the end of the fortnight, this order reverses. A wise practitioner who understands these transitions should direct the breath through the Moon at night and the Sun <term>Bhaskara: the right nostril, associated with Pingala</term> during the day. One who is constantly devoted to this practice is undoubtedly a true Yogi. **23**

### Auspicious Activities for the Moon and Sun

When the breath flows through the **Moon (Left) channel**, the following actions are successful:
*   Starting a journey or giving gifts
*   Marriage ceremonies
*   Donning new clothes or jewelry
*   Performing auspicious deeds or "entering" a new home <note>Vastu-pravesha</note>

In contrast, the **Sun (Right) channel** is praised for:
*   Disputes, gambling, and warfare
*   Bathing, eating, and sexual union
*   General business dealings or moments of fear

Through the proper application of these channels, all success is attained.

### Divination and Conflict

When a question <term>Prashna: a divinatory query</term> is asked, if the breath is in the "life-position" <note>the active nostril</note>, the first person <note>the inquirer</note> will be victorious. However, if the messenger <note>the person bringing the question</note> stands on the side of the "empty" channel <note>the inactive nostril</note>, the second party will prevail.

In times of war, if the breath is steady in the **Moon**, one definitely wins. If the breath is in the **Sun**, a great debate or struggle ensues, but victory follows.

### The Influence of the Five Elements

The outcome of a conflict can also be determined by the dominant element <term>Tattva: the five fundamental states of energy in the breath</term> during the query:
*   **Earth Element:** A treaty or peace is formed <note>original: "sandhi"</note>.
*   **Water Element:** Victory is assured.
*   **Fire Element:** Results in burning or total loss.
*   **Air Element:** Leads to "breaking" or defeat.

If the messenger arrives while the breath is in the "Full Channel" <note>the active nostril</note>, the task will be accomplished. If the enemy is situated in the place of the active channel, they will undoubtedly conquer.

### Omens of Position and Breath

The direction of the messenger is critical. One should observe if they stand to the front, left, or right. If the breath flows through the Sun channel and the person is on the right or behind, success is indicated. If the breath flows through the **Left (Moon)** channel and the person is in front or to the left, victory is certain. **24**

Regarding health queries: if the name of the sick person is spoken while the breath is "full," they will live; otherwise, they will not. In matters of sleep or romantic union with a young woman, if the breath flows through the Sun and then the Moon, one becomes like the **God of Love** <note>original: "Makardhwaja," a title for Kamadeva</note>. One should always provide life to the "Lord of the House" <note>the primary focus of the query</note>... <unclear>broken text</unclear> in the night or at the time of the query.

# The Royal Victory through the Breath

<margin>Raja-vijaya 7</margin>

...that man is truly a master of the Self <note>original: "shivam," referring here to the auspicious or the divine state of a liberated person</note> through his own power. He should proceed according to the circumstances and the situation. The flow of the Moon <term>Indu-chara: the breath through the left nostril</term> is like a charming illusionist <note>The text likens the Moon's shifting nature to "Maya"</note>. There is one God who resides in three states. **42**

### Guidelines for Daily Living

Immediately after eating, when engaged in work with women, and during sleep, the wise should always ensure the breath flows through the **Sun channel** <term>Surya-chara: the right nostril</term>. 

When bathing, when in mourning, when afflicted by poison, when angry, or when weary during the day, the practitioner should consciously direct the breath to flow through the Sun channel to gain clarity and strength. 

### Regarding Progeny and Pregnancy

On the matter of the womb and the secrets of the Lord of Progeny <term>Prajapati: a creator deity associated here with reproduction</term>, if the breath is in the Sun channel during the "even" days of the cycle, it leads to a son. If the breath is in the Moon channel <term>Nishakara</term> during the "odd" days, it results in a daughter. However, a misalignment of these flows may lead to the loss of the pregnancy. **45**

### Divination by Names and Breath

When a question is asked, if the syllables of the name correspond to the "uneven" category, they belong to the Sun <term>Bhaskara</term>. If they are "even," they belong to the Moon. If the flow of breath matches these categories during the inquiry, there is gain; otherwise, there is loss. **46**

### Categorization of Magical and Spiritual Acts

The following intense actions should be performed when the **Sun breath** is active:
*   **Killing** <term>Marana</term>
*   **Delusion** <term>Mohana</term>
*   **Paralyzing** <term>Stambha</term>
*   **Creating Enmity** <term>Vidvesha</term>
*   **Uprooting** <term>Ucchatana</term>
*   **Subjugation** <term>Vasha</term>
*   Acts of summoning, attraction, and breaking through obstacles.

Conversely, the following should be performed when the **Moon breath** rises:
*   **Peace-making** <term>Shantikam</term>
*   **Nourishing rituals** <term>Paushtikam</term>
*   Gathering divine medicinal herbs
*   The advanced practice of **Yoga**

When the breath rises in the morning, knowledge is born. If the Sun rises as the breath, it is one way; if the Moon, another. A rising breath that is balanced is auspicious; that which is uneven is not.

### The System of the Five Sounds

This concludes the section on the body’s breath. Now, we begin the division of the **Five Sounds** <term>Panchasvara: a system using the vowels A, I, U, E, O</term>, categorized by time, the stages of life <note>from childhood to death</note>, and the different states of the soul. These are used to determine what is achievable. According to the school of the **Royal Victory** <note>original: "Raja-vijaya," the title of this manuscript</note>, which is favored by hundreds of masters, we now examine the different measures and movements of the first sound.

### The Divination Grid

To achieve success in understanding the life force <note>original: "Jiva"</note> and birth, one must master the second and third sounds. One must look with a steady and focused gaze.

Through the sequence of the elements and the presiding deities, the practitioner reaches the state of the "deathless" letter <note>a reference to spiritual liberation or the perfection of the mantra</note>.

### The Tortoise Chart

I shall now speak of the **Kurma Chakra** <term>Tortoise Chart: a geographical-astrological diagram where the land is mapped onto the body of a cosmic tortoise</term> according to the school of Koshala. By the mere knowledge of this, one can understand the coming upheavals of a country <term>Deshaviplava: political unrest, invasion, or natural disaster</term>. 

The mountains, the single peak where the gods reside, and the millions of women—all of this, along with Mount Meru <note>The sacred center of the universe in Hindu cosmology</note>, is perceived as being well-contained within the earth. The eight mountain ranges and the oceans are all held by the Earth; but who holds the Earth herself? She is held by the **Boar** <term>Varaha: the third avatar of Vishnu who lifted the earth from the cosmic ocean</term>. That Great Boar, the upholder of the world, shines as if he were a garland. 

Even the great-bodied Indra <note>King of the Gods</note> stands upon the head of that Boar. Located at the Boar’s crest-jewel is my own head <note>This likely refers to the "head" or northern point of the diagram being discussed</note>. **5** In this manner, the serpent **Shesha** <note>The thousand-headed cosmic serpent</note> exists, coiled and supporting the foundation. The Tortoise is situated in a specific portion of the view, positioned beneath the surface. 

I shall now describe with great care the measurements of this cosmic Tortoise. It extends for hundreds of thousands, even millions of **Yojanas** <term>Yojana: a traditional measure of distance, roughly 8 to 9 miles</term>. Half of that is its width, and its height is half of that again. **8** 

The head of the tortoise, measured at ten million units, is known by sages such as Sanaka. Within that space, the interval is measured as one million units. **9** The span of its torso is twice that, as are its feet. The number of its fingers and claws is reckoned in the tens of thousands of Yojanas. This measurement of the Tortoise is also declared in the **Yamala** texts <note>A class of ancient Tantric scriptures</note>. On its upper...

This Earth, which is composed of the seven islands <note>original: "sapta-dvipa," referring to the seven continents of traditional Indian cosmology</note>, is situated here. The regions of Bhrigu, Kutch, and the Konkan fortress, as well as the vegetation of the western lands, are noted. 

Under the constellations Jyeshtha, Mula, and Ashadha, which are situated in the western part of the tortoise, the regions of the West and South are located. Here, the Malaya mountains and all the islands of the ocean, including Saurashtra <note>modern Gujarat</note> and the Indus region <note>Saindhava</note>, are found. In these water-land stations, the vegetation is stationary, yet the people suffer distress. Here also are Jalandhara and the region of the sands, stretching toward the direction of Mount Meru.

### The Application of the Tortoise Diagram

This is known as the **Earth-Tortoise** <note>original: "Prithvi-Kurma"</note>, which begins with the star Krittika <note>the Pleiades</note>. In the four sections of this Tortoise, one should first draw the Eastern Diagram, placing the names of the countries accordingly. One should carefully observe the Country-Tortoise where Saturn resides. 

One should also draw the **City-Tortoise** <note>original: "Nagara-Kurma"</note> in the same manner as before. In the place where Saturn stays, there will be sorrow, and the same is true for the point it pierces. Following this, one should proceed to the **House-Tortoise** <note>original: "Griha-Kurma"</note> as it has been established. Draw the House-Tortoise at the entrance of the home, beginning with the star Magha <note>the star Regulus, associated with ancestors and power</note>, following the rotation of the house from the East. This determines the good or bad fortune of the building. ४

Saturn staying in the house is a bringer of grief and intense heat/affliction. Known to be at the door, it provides obstacles; at the feet, it is like a consuming fire. ४ It is known that in the South, there is fear from "demons" <note>original: "rakshasas," referring here to great calamities or malignant forces</note>. In the Northwest, it is known to be auspicious, yet Saturn provides its influence there as well. In the North, it provides the gain of wealth, and in the "mixture" <note>original: "sankare," likely the junctions or corners</note>, it grants all success. 

When Saturn is strong, it is auspicious even with little power. But if it afflicts a place at a time when the "ripening of karma" <note>original: "karma-pacake"</note> occurs, then a great obstacle arises in that place—of this, there is no doubt. When Saturn moves into a desired or favorable station, one should perform the rituals described in the *Yamala* <note>a class of Tantric scriptures</note> to destroy all those obstacles. This Tortoise Diagram is a Great Diagram, also spoken of in the *Yamala* texts. For the knowledge of the "three times" <note>past, present, and future</note>, it is as if it sits right in the palm of one's hand.

I shall explain the various results of the Base and the others. Through the "piercing" of the houses, one should know the good or bad effects of the gentle and cruel planets.

If the "Base" is struck while the king is being consecrated on the lotus, the nation will come under the control of another; of this, there is no doubt. If the king remains in the "Support" <note>original: "Ashrite"</note>, he will be ignorant of political policy. The King will take on the form of a lion, with a mind as bold as a lion. He will always be fond of battle and will constantly destroy hosts of enemies.

Following the Serpent Diagram (Phani-svara), the text transitions into the **Throne Diagram** (Simhasana-chakra), a complex predictive tool used to determine the stability of a monarch's reign and the welfare of the people based on planetary transits across specific points of a symbolic throne.</meta>

# The Throne Diagram

When a planet enters the **Throne Diagram** <note>original: "Simhasana-chakra"</note>, if it is afflicted, the King <note>original: "Nripa"</note> becomes fearsome in appearance despite his glory; he becomes fickle-minded, extremely wrathful, and a tormentor of his subjects. If at that time there are ill-omened songs, cruelty, or harsh imprisonments, but the planet is established in an auspicious state or an **auspicious rising sign** <term>Lagna: the zodiac sign ascending on the eastern horizon at a specific moment</term> on the **Lotus Seat** <note>original: "Padmasana"</note>—

Sitting like Indra <note>the King of the Gods</note> in conjunction, when the planet is in its own sign or exalted, the King achieves high fame and destroys the assembly of enemies all at once. However, when **malefic planets** <note>original: "krura-graha"</note> arrive and occupy the seat, there is suffering, loss of territory, and even death occurs. If Saturn <note>original: "Sauri," literally "Son of the Sun"</note> is poorly placed, he causes famine and hellish conditions where thieves thrive and the subjects perish. **(15)**

When Saturn is on the Seat, it brings defeat in battle, or perhaps the suffering of disease and the sorrow of being conquered. When Saturn is at the "foot" of the diagram, the infantry is destroyed, or the King's beloved, the prince, or his circle of friends meet their ruin. If the **Banner** <note>original: "Dhvaja," a specific point in the diagram</note> stands in the **Throne Diagram**, death is certain, unless Venus <note>original: "Shukra"</note> is in a balancing or favorable position. 

When Saturn, Rahu <note>the North Node of the Moon</note>, the Sun, or Mars <note>original: "Maheya," literally "Son of the Earth"</note> are joined with the Moon or situated in the seat, they cause intense distress and destruction; they are particularly harmful when **retrograde** <note>original: "vakra"</note> or situated in a **malefic channel** <term>Nadi: subtle energy lines within the astrological diagram used to calculate influence</term>.

If the Moon is in conjunction with the seat, the King acts as Time <note>meaning he becomes a bringer of death or finality</term>. Saturn only gives auspicious results through divine favor or friendship, without a doubt. Where Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note> is situated on the seat, there is a vast and prosperous kingdom. 

### Instructions for Drawing the Diagram

Regarding the **Throne Diagram**: first, one should draw the diagram so that it is beautiful with three-by-three divisions. In this arrangement, one must place the **twenty-seven constellations** <note>original: "bhani," referring to the Nakshatras</note>. Starting from Ashvini <note>the first constellation</note> in the middle, and Mula <note>the nineteenth constellation</note>... in the North, East, and South, three fields of seven are considered. 

The constellations Ashvini, Magha, and Mula cause the **piercing of the channels** <term>Vedha: an astrological "strike" or interference where a planet's position affects another point</term>. I shall now state the results for each, starting with the **fly-whisk** <note>original: "Chamara," a traditional symbol of royal authority</note>. On the western central line is the Lord of Horses; in the Southeast is the Lord of Men; and in the very center is the Lord of Elephants. 

The auspicious and inauspicious effects should be known through the six divisions. For others, the three **Umbrellas** <note>original: "Chatra"</note> are situated there to provide results for the King. The fly-whisk, the water-pot, the lute, the umbrella, the staff, and the pin on the seat—the nine types of "piercings" are thus proclaimed.

In whatever constellation <term>Nakshatra: one of the 27 lunar mansions of the zodiac</term> Saturn <note>original: "Sauri," a name for Saturn meaning 'Son of the Sun'</note> is situated, one should predict destruction <note>original: "bhanga"</note> for that area. I shall now describe the specific results for each part, beginning with the left side. **(8)** If Saturn is positioned in the **Fly-whisk** <note>original: "Chamara," a traditional symbol of royalty</note>, fierce winds and drought <note>original: "anavrishti"</note> will occur. Famine will arise there, along with theft and the suffering of the subjects; of this, there is no doubt. If it should be in the **Water-pot** <note>original: "Kalasha"</note> behind, there will be defeat in battle and great fear. Whatever direction the wind blows, everything in its path is ruined; of this, there is no doubt.

If it is in the **Lute** <note>original: "Vina"</note>, the Chief Queen <note>original: "Pata-rajni"</note> is afflicted and perishes. The King becomes fickle-minded, and the earth <note>the kingdom</note> becomes filled with fear. When Saturn, the enemy, is in the area of the **Scepter** <note>original: "Danda"</note> on a day of judgment, then the destruction of that territory <note>original: "kshetra"</note> and its constellation occurs without a doubt. Destruction comes to the **Seat** <note>original: "Asana"</note> when the cruel Saturn occupies it. During the time of the crown prince's succession, there is execution or slaughter while the King is established there. Benefic planets like Mercury and Venus <note>original: "Saumya" and "Shukra"</note> may influence the day, but the results of Saturn are as spoken. When Saturn is retrograde <note>original: "vakra"</note> or cruel, it makes everything cruel. If Rahu <note>the north node of the moon</note>, the Sun <note>original: "Aditya"</note>, and Jupiter <note>original: "Jiva"</note> are partially situated there, for those born in the north, it brings destruction and downfall. When a trio of cruel planets is joined with Mercury and the Moon, destruction occurs there just as described by the ancient sages. **(18)**

In this way, the malefic planets in that diagram, when joined with Venus, signify the destruction of the constellations on the southern path, as stated by the wise. Thus, in other kingdoms, wherever such a conjunction of planets occurs as described, one should predict destruction. Malefic planets give their [worst] results in the eighth position for Saturn, while Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note> gives the results for the benefics. Mars, Mercury, Venus, Rahu, the Sun, and the Moon are equal in strength. Just as the cruel planets cause harm, the gentle <note>benefic</note> planets bring auspiciousness. That which is occupied by a cruel planet becomes cruel; that joined with a mild and gentle planet is auspicious. When a gentle planet is overcome by a cruel one, the constellation's formation is broken by the arrival of an enemy army <note>original: "para-chakra," literally 'another's wheel' or 'foreign invasion'</note>. For a King where Rahu or Ketu <note>the descending lunar node</note> are situated, there is a breach of his territory and the King may suffer from poisoning. **(21)**

The King should abandon hunting, daring expeditions, travels, and war <note>original: "vigraha"</note> when a cruel planet <note>original: "krura khechara"</note> is located in the **Parasol Diagram** <note>original: "Chatra-stha"</note>, especially if he sees his elephants falling ill. Knowing this, if the King performs rituals for domestic peace <note>original: "shanti"</note> as described in the **Yamala** <note>a class of Tantric scriptures</note> texts, then peace will prevail for him. This concludes the **Parasol Diagram** <note>original: "Chatra Chakra"</note>. In the case of gentle and mild influences... there is the heat of fire or even slight imprisonment... from the Sun's constellation, two cruel ones are to be known...

Let it be remembered that the obstruction <note>original: "Khokara"</note> of various kinds is recorded everywhere in the rules of destiny. Listen now to the effects of the planets' positions. When a cruel planet is present, there is a bad omen; when a benefic is present, there is loss. If a planet is weakened, there is doubt. A cruel planet staying in a house causes fear and death. Through the balance of good and bad planets, one finds either fear or the suffering of disease. 

In marriage, this leads to widowhood. During the time of travel <note>original: "Yatrakale"</note>, it brings great fear. In battle, it brings disease, death, and defeat. When the Sun is situated in the first quarter of the Southeast direction <note>Agneya</note> or in the portion of the Shravana constellation, it creates a "kick."

The Sun is said to reside in the twelfth position. If it stays in the third house, it is remembered as song <note>likely an error in transcription for a specific planetary result</note>. The Moon affects the ninth, and Venus affects the fifth. 

Through these, destruction occurs. When Mars, Rahu, and Saturn are remembered, they cause death. In the Impact of Jupiter <note>Jiva</note>, the destruction of one's piercing-point <note>Vedha</note> occurs. Through Venus, work is frustrated. The result of this planetary "Impact" is remembered as a great name/omen <note>unclear reading</note>. **(27)**

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for horary astrology (Prashna), specifically detailing the calculation of planetary influences and the "Vargatari Chakra." It follows the previous discussion on destructive aspects (Vedha) by offering a method to determine auspiciousness based on the Moon's position and planetary "gazes" or aspects.</meta>

The combinations of the lunar mansions should be understood in the sequence beginning with Ashvini <note>the first of the 27 lunar mansions or Nakshatras</note>, along with the divisions of the nine-fold chart <term>Navamsha: a one-ninth division of a zodiac sign used to determine a planet's hidden strength</term> and their respective colors. Starting with Aries, the lords of the signs are Mars and Venus, then Mercury, the Moon and Sun, then Mercury and Venus again, followed by Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn.

By calculating the divisions within the chart and observing the movement through the three types of variations, the timing of events is determined. The results of this—gain or loss, victory or defeat—depend on that timing. Multiplying the desired time <note>original: "Ishta-nadi," the specific time elapsed since sunrise</note> by the appropriate factors and dividing by sixty, the remainder indicates the Moon's position in the sequence starting from Ashvini.

At the **time of a question**, or in matters of marriage, or at the moment of birth, the fruit of this calculation should be known according to the hidden wisdom of all the scriptures.

**Thus, the expansion of the Vargatari Chakra in twelve spokes**

The chart is drawn following the sequence of the signs, moving in a counter-clockwise direction <note>original: "vama-rasi-margena"</note>. Within this twelve-spoked diagram, the positions of the planets and the Moon are established at the moment of the inquiry. One must judge all auspicious and inauspicious outcomes based on the power of the planetary aspects <term>Graha-drishti: the 'glance' or influence a planet exerts on other houses in a chart</term>.

Planets cast their gaze upon the third, tenth, fifth, ninth, fourth, eighth, and seventh houses. Malefic planets casting such a gaze grant their respective fruits. Mars and the Sun are said to have an "upward gaze" <note>original: "Urdhva-drishti"</note>, while Mercury and Venus have their own specific influences. Jupiter and Saturn are associated with a "level gaze" when joined together.

The signs of Aries, Taurus, Capricorn, Virgo, Cancer, Pisces, and Libra are the locations where the Sun and other planets reach their points of debilitation <term>Nicha: the point in the zodiac where a planet's influence is at its weakest</term> when they are in the seventh sign from their point of highest exaltation. The highest exaltation points for the Sun and others are at twenty-eight, twenty-seven, and twenty degrees respectively. 

The Moon’s influence is determined by where it wanders—whether in its point of exaltation, debilitation, or in a neutral state. When the Moon is born into <note>situated in</note> these high, low, or middling positions, the astrologer must declare the results. If the Moon is in its place of exaltation, the Sun and Moon together provide great strength. If in a neutral place, the influence is moderate. If the Moon is in the sign of its debilitation, it is influenced by Saturn, while Mercury and Venus provide strength when in their triangular positions <term>Trikona: the highly auspicious 1st, 5th, and 9th houses or specific degree ranges</term>.

When the Moon is aspected by a benefic planet, everything becomes peaceful and auspicious. If it falls under a "cruel gaze" <note>original: "Krura-drishti," an aspect from a malefic planet like Mars or Saturn</note>, there will be loss of wealth and great fear. Thus, when the Moon is joined by a benefic, auspiciousness arises for all people. If joined by a malefic, the result is cruelty. If the influences are mixed, the result is undoubtedly mixed.

Based on whether the Moon is in its bright or dark phase <note>original: "Shukla" or "Krishna" paksha</note>, the four colors/classes <note>this likely refers to the Varna classification used to describe the nature of a person or a query</note> must be known with great effort by the wise at the **time of a question**. The lords of these divisions are the Sun, Mars, Venus, Mercury, Jupiter, Saturn, and Rahu.

Those who are wise should understand the planets starting from the "Ka" phonetic group <note>the group of consonants k, kh, g, gh, ṅ</note>. The Sun, Mercury, Jupiter, and Venus are known by their colors. Jupiter and Mercury are yellow. The Moon and Venus are white. One should know that yellow indicates an auspicious result, while white indicates an even more auspicious result. Following this, the Red **Wheel of Sounds** <term>Svarachakra: a mystical diagram mapping Sanskrit phonetics to planetary influences</term> is described.

One should draw a wheel with twelve spokes, where each <term>Nadi: a channel of energy or a specific time unit of 24 minutes</term> shines with equal radiance. At every fifth position, a horizontal line should be made. Thus, a total of 108 **Nadis** are produced. Within this calculated wheel, one should place the **Nadis** in their proper positions. If a cruel planet "pierces" or obstructs <note>original: "Vedha," an astronomical aspect where one planet's position blocks or influences another</note> a point, the result occurring at that time should be spoken. One must describe the many auspicious and inauspicious effects based on these obstructions. If there is an obstruction by a cruel planet, and even if there are twenty beneficial aspects, there will be loss during a journey, and in a great battle, there may be death. Cruel planets cause total loss, while gentle planets give half the fruit. If there is an obstruction at the time of birth or an inquiry, there is no doubt that the person faces death. All of this knowledge regarding the timing of results, which was hidden in the secret Tantras and scriptures, is now revealed by me here. 

This concludes the third type of obstruction.

# The Wheel of Sounds (Svarachakra)

The vowels are said to represent the Gods (*Devas*). The "Ka" group of consonants are the Demons (*Daityas*). The "Cha" group are the Serpent Spirits (*Nagas*). The "Ya" and "Ta" groups are also defined. The "Tha" group represents the Sages (*Munis*). The "Pa" group represents the Ogres (*Rakshasas*). From the Ogres come the Ghosts (*Pishachas*), from them the Demons, and then the Gods. Among all these, Humans are the most excellent, for they are capable of accomplishing all goals. In times of war, one should determine the strength of the sides using this specific sequence. This is the primary consideration regarding the first letter of a name.

Such is the strength described in this system, known as the **Brahmayamala** <term>Brahmayamala: an ancient and highly influential Sanskrit text of the Bhairava Tantra tradition</term>. This concludes the **Wheel of Phonetic Groups** <term>Vargachakra: a chart used to determine the compatibility or strength of individuals based on the first letter of their names</term>. Even in times of war, victory can be achieved through this wheel. Whether one person can kill another is seen through the observation of the **Svarodaya** <term>Svarodaya: the science of breath and sound used for divination</term> to examine victory or defeat.

In this context, whether a situation is pleasant or difficult, or whether it relates to the Moon's influence, the results follow in order. Auspiciousness is found at the corners. According to other authorities, strength is derived from the initial letter of the name. If the two names belong to the same category, the one with the "stronger" sound becomes powerful. The "A" group is associated with the Snake, the "Ka" group with the Cat, the "Cha" group with the Lion, the "Pa" group with the Dog... <note>the text here maps phonetic groups to animal symbols to determine natural enemies and allies</note>. The "Ta" group is associated with the Enemy-Slayer. The wise say the "Pa" group is liberated...

It also details the 108 Nadis and the impact of malefic planetary "obstructions" (Vedha) on a person's fate.</summary>
