<meta>This page continues the technical classification of phonetic "wheels" (chakras), transitioning from the astrological Wheel of Sounds to the Wheel of Categories (Varga-Chakra) specifically applied to the science of omens (Shakuna Shastra). It details how bird omens are calculated based on directional deities, lunar days, and the phonetic groups of a person's name.</meta>

One should also understand the **Wheel of Categories** <term>Varga-Chakra: a system that groups the Sanskrit alphabet into eight phonetic families, each assigned to a specific bird or omen</term> for determining the outcome of battles or journeys. In the various directions, the rulers are placed as follows: in the East is the Sun <note>original: "Prabhakara"</note>; in the Southeast are the Vasus; in the South is Yama; in the Southwest is the Rakshasa; in the West is Varuna; in the Northwest is Vayu; in the North is Soma <note>the Moon</note>; and in the Northeast is Ishana <note>Shiva</note>. 

Then, one must consider the Eight Birds/Omens in their proper order:
1. The Vulture <note>original: "Gridhra"</note>
2. The Hawk <note>original: "Sena/Syena"</note>
3. The Owl <note>original: "Kaushika"</note>
4. The Jackal <note>original: "Shrigala"; though a mammal, it is traditionally included in bird omen texts</note>
5. The Peacock <note>original: "Mayura"</note>
6. The Great Owl <note>original: "Uluka"</note>
7. The Calf <note>original: "Govatsa"; likely referring to a specific bird behavior or a minor deity's omen</note>
8. The Crane <note>original: "Sarasa"</note>

These correspond to the phonetic groups: the "A" group, the "Ka" group, the "Ca" group, the "Ta" group <note>retroflex consonants</note>, the "Ta" group <note>dental consonants</note>, the "Pa" group, the "Ya" group, and the "Sha" group. One must determine the strength of these birds through the grid of eight squares <note>original: "kosthakastaka"</note>.

### The Strength of Omens by Time and Month

The Peacock and the Crane are powerful in the morning. The Calf and the Owl <note>Kaushika</note> are strong during the middle of the day. The Owlet <note>original: "Pingala"</note> and the Great Owl <note>Uluka</note> are known to be powerful at night. 

In the specific months, their power shifts:
- The Great Owl and the Owlet are powerful during the month of Magha <note>approximately January/February</note>. 
- The Peacock and the Crane are strong in the month of Ashvin <note>September/October</note>. 
- The Owl and the Moon-bird are powerful in the month of Nabhasya <note>August/September</note>.
- The Vulture and the Hawk are generally weakened <note>original: "balaihatau"</note> at all times compared to these.

### The Strength by Lunar Days (Tithis)

The strength of the birds is also determined by the lunar calendar <term>Tithi: a lunar day in the Vedic calendar</term>:
- The Vulture is powerful on the 6th and 9th lunar days.
- On the 2nd and 10th days, it remains steady.
- The Owl <note>Kaushika</note> is strong on the 11th lunar day.
- The Owlet <note>Pingala</note> is strong on the 4th and 12th lunar days.
- The Crane is powerful on the 5th and 13th lunar days.
- The Peacock is strong on the 6th and 14th lunar days.
- On the 7th and 15th <note>Full/New Moon</note> days, the energies are balanced.
- On the 8th lunar day, the Calf possesses great valor and power.

In this way, the wise man should understand the strength of these omens. One should consider the person’s house, their name-group <note>Varga</note>, and the relative strength of the day, night, lunar day, and month. 

<margin>The hierarchy of strength: The Owlet is stronger than the Great Owl; the Great Owl is stronger than the Crane; and the Crane is the most significant <note>original: "guru"</note> among them.</margin>

<summary>This page outlines the Varga-Chakra, a system that links Sanskrit phonetic groups to eight specific animal omens (mostly birds). it provides a technical guide for determining the "strength" (balam) of an omen based on the time of day, the lunar date, and the specific month.</summary>

...powerful and auspicious. Thus is the **Wheel of Rows** <term>Panti-chakra: a grid-based system where planetary aspects are aligned in rows to determine omens</term> described, based on the observations of the Calf bird and the Crane.
<margin>Ra. Vi. 13</margin>

### The Wheel of Viranchi

Now, I shall describe the **Wheel of Viranchi** <term>Viranchi-chakra: named after Viranchi, a title for Brahma the Creator; this wheel is used to calculate the "piercing" of birth stars</term>, which grants a clear understanding of success. This knowledge is rare, even among the gods. One should write the three groups of stars starting from the Southeast <note>original: "Agneya"</note> in a sequential row. O Parvati! <note>The text is framed as a divine revelation from Shiva to his consort, Parvati.</note> Know that the "piercing" <term>Vedha: an astrological concept where planets in certain positions "pierce" or strike the influence of a lunar mansion</term> occurs through the corners and the direct sight of the planets.

One must calculate the nine stages of the stars:
1. Birth <note>original: "Janma"</note>
2. Wealth <note>original: "Sampat"</note>
3. Calamity <note>original: "Vipat"</note>
4. Well-being <note>original: "Kshema"</note>
5. Obstruction <note>original: "Pratyari"</note>
6. Success <note>original: "Sadhaka"</note>
7. Death <note>original: "Naidhana"</note>
8. Friend <note>original: "Maitra"</note>
9. Great Friend <note>original: "Ati-maitra"</note>

If the "piercing" occurs in the three critical stars, death is certain—there is no doubt. In the three sets of birth stars, if cruel <note>malefic</note> influences like the Sun <note>original: "Ravi"</note> are present, they are known to provide unfavorable results. O Blessed One! If joined by benefic planets, the result is auspicious; if by malefic planets, it is inauspicious. If the planets are mixed, the result is mixed, especially depending on which planet holds the most strength.

The fruit of this sight <note>planetary aspect</note> should be given according to the power of the planet. The strongest influence prevails, whether it be the Sun, Mars <note>original: "Bhauma"</note>, or Saturn <note>original: "Sauri"</note>. If the three stars are defeated, life itself is at risk.

### The Wheel of Mother-Syllables

The Wheel of Viranchi is also calculated using numerical values assigned to the days and elements: the Vedas <note>4</note>, Fire <note>3</note>, the Twins <note>2</note>, the Vasu deities <note>8</note>, and so on. Below these, one should write the **Mother-Syllables** <term>Matrika: the phonetic sounds of the Sanskrit alphabet viewed as divine "mothers"</term>. By totaling the values of the letters and vowels in a person's name, if the remainder shows a higher value for one person, they will be the victor in battle. If the value is lower, they will face defeat. This is the **Wheel of Mother-Syllables**.

### The Wheel of Victory

Now I shall speak of the even more essential teachings spoken by the teacher **Lapatacharya**. This system clearly shows victory and defeat through the letters of a name, distinguished by:
* The division of voiced and unvoiced sounds <note>original: "Ghosha-Aghosha"</note>
* The "entry" and "exit" of breath or movement within the wheel.

This is the **Wheel of Victory** <note>original: "Vijaya-chakra"</note>.

### The Wheel of the Elephant

Next, I shall describe the wheel named **Matanga** <note>The Elephant Wheel</note>. Merely by knowing this, one understands the phonetic weight <note>original: "matra"</note> required for victory in war...

<meta>This page transitions from general phonetic and astrological wheels to specific veterinary and military divination. It focuses on the "Gaja-chakra" (Elephant Wheel) and "Turanga-chakra" (Horse Wheel), which were essential for kings to determine the auspiciousness of their cavalry and war elephants based on the transit of Saturn (Sauri).</meta>

A King who desires success should draw this wheel <term>Chakra: a symbolic diagram used here for astrological mapping</term>, which is composed of all the body parts of the animal. Twenty-eight lunar mansions <term>Nakshatra: the 28 sectors of the sky used in Vedic astrology, including the interstitial star Abhijit</term> are to be assigned to the figure following the "Path of Creation."

Two stars should be placed at the mouth, two at the tips of the tusks, two in the eyes, and others in the ears and the head. Four stars each should be placed on the tail, the back, and the belly. This layout is known by the wise as the "Face of the Two-Tusked One" <note>original: "Dvairadana-vadana," a poetic name for an elephant</note>.

One must discern the good and bad fortune of the animal based on the specific star in which Saturn <term>Sauri: another name for Shani or the planet Saturn, considered a heavy and often malefic influence</term> is currently situated. If Saturn resides in the stars of the face or the eyes, or in the stars of the belly at a significant time, there is no doubt the elephant will fall. If it resides in the stars of the feet, the back, the tail, or within the ears, there will be destruction caused by wind or in the heat of battle <note>This implies the elephant may stampede or suffer a physical breakdown during a conflict</note>.

During the time when Saturn is positioned in these unfavorable stars, one must make every effort to avoid the "Binding of the Frontlet" <term>Pattabandha: a ceremonial investiture where a decorative head-plate is tied to a royal elephant or horse</term>.

->**The Ornaments of the World**<-

The sacred mountain Meru is the ornament of the earth; the moon is the ornament of the all-knowing; knowledge is the ornament of men; and the elephant is the ornament of armies. 

There is no equal to the elephant in war; there is no equal to the elephant in strength; there is no kinsman like the elephant; and there is no enemy as formidable as the elephant. This "Immortal Wheel" is highly praised. Those men who are slain while positioned upon the shoulders of an elephant <note>referring to warriors or drivers (mahouts)</note> instantly attain heaven; therefore, elephants are considered equal to the path to heaven itself.

### The Wheel of the Horse

Thus ends the **Elephant Wheel**. Now, O Goddess <note>original: "Sureshvari," addressing Parvati in this tantric dialogue style</note>, I shall describe the **Horse Wheel** <term>Turanga-chakra</term>, having mastered the essence of the horse, the true carrier of kings.

One should draw the wheel of the horse and assign the stars starting from the star representing the horse's intake <note>referring to the star active at the horse's acquisition or birth</note>. The twenty-eight stars should be assigned as follows: two to the mouth, and four each to the ears and the head. Five stars are placed on the belly and five on the back.

Wherever Saturn is located, the fruits of that placement follow:
If Saturn is in the stars of the mouth, the back, the belly, or the head, the horse will falter in the water or become confused and erratic <note>original: "vibhramam"</note>, causing a total loss of strength and defeat in the great battle.

When Saturn is in these positions, the King must avoid using the horse for ceremonial bindings, for departures, or for war. Horses belonging to a King that are stationed in distant lands will be shaken and filled with fear by the power of the enemy if the stars are ill-aligned. The horses of a King that wander the earth under favorable stars ensure the world remains under his command. Without them, nothing in the world shines. Just as a cloud gives rain or the sun gives light, even a horse with few good qualities can grant the desired fruits of victory to a King.

...[this diagram] destroys all faults generated by the planets and other celestial bodies. For one who moves with it, wisdom and wealth are gained through virtue, just as victory is achieved through the resonance of one's own fame.

->**The Chariot Wheel**<-
<note>original: "Ratha-chakram"</note>

First, one should draw a wheel in the shape of a beautiful chariot. At the front of the chariot, and at its central pillars, the stars should be placed. Starting from the star occupied by the Sun, one should write the stars three by three until all twenty-seven <term>Nakshatras: the 27 lunar mansions or star-sectors of the zodiac</term> are accounted for. In this great forest of stars, one learns of the auspicious and inauspicious outcomes. In the limbs of the chariot, victory is found. Thus is the **Chariot Wheel**, which provides great curiosity and insight for the four-fold army <term>Chaturanga: the traditional Indian army divisions of chariots, elephants, cavalry, and infantry</term> regarding their intelligence in battle.

Because the signs of the zodiac are spread throughout, the **Joy Wheel** <note>original: "Harsha-chakra"</note> is now described. For this, a human figure is drawn using all its body parts, including the star Abhijit <term>Abhijit: an extra "intercalary" star often used in divination, making 28 stars in total</term>. In the various parts of this human form, the fate of the hero <note>original: "Veera-bhagyam"</note> is stationed.

One star is placed on the face, one on the head, three on the left hand, and six on the right foot. This completes the layout of the wheel. Furthermore, in that wheel, one must observe where the lords of the signs—Mars, Saturn, and the nodes <note>original: "Vibhauma-sauri-tamah," referring to Mars, Saturn, and Rahu/Ketu</note>—are situated. If a malefic planet is located in the house of the enemy, or if it aspects <term>Drishti: the astrological "glance" or influence one planet casts upon another house</term> the house of the enemy, then at that moment of the two hours <term>Hora: an astrological hour, roughly two modern hours</term>, a great battle or upheaval will occur.

Even a single planet, if positioned in a way that exhausts its strength, can cause destruction. When many such planets are positioned together, even powerful heroes may be overcome by their enemies. Wherever the strike of the thunderbolt <note>original: "Vajra-ghata," a metaphor for a devastating or fatal blow</note> is possible, one must consider the specific influence of Mars <term>Bhumija-phalam: the "results of the Earth-born," a name for the planet Mars, the god of war</term>. To speak clearly: when "cruel" planets <term>Krura-graha: malefic planets like Mars and Saturn</term> are situated on the right side or the back, they cause injury and defeat in battle. If they move to the left, the strike of the thunderbolt will surely bring inauspicious results.

->**The Human Figure Wheel**<-

In this second diagram, the human form <term>Thilla-mandala: a technical term, possibly a regional name for this specific circular diagram</term> is utilized. The wheel should be known as being three times the width of the body's thumb-measurement. By knowing the position of the stars and the zodiac signs on the body, one can predict if death will occur by the fall of a weapon <note>original: "shalya," often meaning an arrow or surgical tool</term>. **॥ Thus ends the Second Human Wheel.**

When the count is endowed with strength, it creates a union for those in the joints of the army. The twelve stars are counted together with the planets to determine the flow. The **Akula** groups <note>original: "Akula" translates to "non-group" or "scattered," while "Kula" refers to a family or organized group</term> are said to provide victory for the townspeople/citizens, while the remaining **Kula** groups are auspicious for others. This wheel should be viewed with the three-fold conjunction. One who knows this is truly wise in that matter. **Thus ends the Akula-Akula Wheel.**

...bringing successful results for the king. <note>original: "mahibhuja"</note> **Thus ends the Wool Wheel.**

A wise man should draw a square wheel with four gates. One should place the <term>Tithis: lunar days</term> beginning with Nanda and the rest, starting from the eastern gate in sequence. The <term>Nakshatras: lunar mansions or star clusters</term> starting with Krittika should be inscribed. In the middle, the twenty-eight stars <note>This includes Abhijit, the "extra" star often used in divination</note> are placed. If situated in Jupiter’s position, the stability of the place is broken. In Venus’s position, <unclear>one arrives</unclear>. In Saturn’s position, there are weapons and war. By the remembrance of Rahu, there is much destruction on the earth; the position is quickly broken and one must flee. When the Seven Wandering Planets <note>The sun, moon, and five visible planets</note> are in their steady positions, one is victorious. 

Victory in battle occurs when the cruel planets <term>Krura: "hard" or malefic planets like Mars and Saturn</term> are in their proper places. Defeat occurs when the gentle planets <term>Saumya: "mild" or benefic planets like Jupiter and Venus</term> are poorly placed. If there is a conflict between the cruel and gentle influences, the warrior must fight; if both are cruel, it causes death. Gentle influences provide a treaty in war, while mixed influences provide mixed results.

**Thus the Strength Wheel.**

One should draw three lines along with a trident <note>original: "trishula"</note> standing upright. A sacred spot <note>original: "tirtham"</note> is placed alone in all the corners of the pairs. Two peaks are joined at each corner of the trident. Having placed the fifth vertical rod in the middle, one should calculate from the Sun’s star. Based on the tradition of the five elements that have passed or are to come, the results for the Great-Armed King <note>original: "mahabhuja"</note> are declared from the two halves of the wheel. 

If the count reaches five, there is victory over the enemy. If the enemy lords are in a state of "breaking," there is fear. This is proclaimed as the result for the king. As far as the trident's point reaches, even a leader with a very small army, having obtained the power of the **Strength Wheel**, can properly engage and overcome the enemy's forces in battle.

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for astrological divination diagrams used for military success. ### The Sun-Time-Fire Wheel

This is the **Sun-Time-Fire Wheel** <note>original: "Suryakalanala-chakram"</note>, known as the destroyer of the lotus of sins. In this wheel of the sky, one should place tridents <term>Trishula: the three-pronged spear of Shiva, used here as a directional marker in the diagram</term> in the eastern and middle sections of the star-wheel <note>original: "tarachakra"</note>. 

In the progression of a battle, one must count the positions on the trident. If the factors are situated outside, in the middle, or moving between them, the result changes. One should establish the circle and the stations <note>original: "dhishnyan"</note> of the stars. The predicted fruits of these three stations are as follows:

Even if touched by malefic planets <note>original: "krura"</note>, if a factor is in the center—the "womb"—success is attained through the unity of the sounds. In the procedure of touching the stations of the name and birth star, seven specific applications are known. 

In a great war, a king <note>original: "bhumibhuja"</note> may be slain, or there may be victory at the forefront of the trident’s womb. Defeat <note>original: "bhanga"</note> occurs if the planet is situated on the outside of the name-station. Even if the desired outcome is imagined, one must look to the four, five, and twelve-fold measures to determine victory. 

Consider the eighth house from birth and the five-fold effort; the twenty-second star indicates the "Planetary Staff Combination" <term>Graha-danda-yoga: a specific celestial alignment signifying punishment or severe force</term>. In that very battle, the full power of Time <note>original: "Kala"</note> is felt. Through the fullness of time, in the sixth house or the rising sign <note>original: "lagne"</note>, the strength of the astrological hour <term>Hora: a one-hour duration ruled by a specific planet</term> and the strength of the warrior’s class <note>original: "varna-viryam"</note> must be weighed. 

If a man is lacking in land or strength, he gains no power or profit. But if this wheel, named **Time-Fire**, is used in battle, the king's enemies will be consumed like water by a great fire born of wrath. 

O Lord of the Ganas <note>An invocation to the god Ganesha, the remover of obstacles</note>, the Sharp-Rayed One [the Sun] reveals this knowledge. From the stations of the Moon and the movements of the stars, the internal path of the journey is known. If the Sun touches these points, then horses, arrows, chariots, and weapons are all engaged by the decree of fate. 

When the sound of the kettledrums <note>original: "bheri-rava"</note> rises in battle, the stated result will manifest; otherwise, the planetary positions may be reversed. This wheel, which protects kings, should be consulted regarding the fall of a city or the victory signaled by the Moon's trident.

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of astrological "wheels" or diagrams used for military and political forecasting. The previous page concluded with a warning about the "Planetary Staff Combination" and the twenty-second star's influence on a king in battle. This page transitions from the general Sun-Fire Wheel to the more specific Thief-Time-Fire Wheel, used to predict insurgencies, raids, and the outcomes of direct military engagement between a ruler and an aggressor.</meta>

If situated in the very middle of the trident-threefold structure <note>original: "trishrishtake," referring to the three-pronged layout mentioned previously</note>, the position is known to bring gain. There is no doubt that when the Moon <note>original: "Chandrama"</note> reaches this point, success is attained. One must make every effort to avoid the first, eighth, third, and fifth [lunar days] during the "empty" periods <note>original: "rikta," referring to the 4th, 9th, and 14th lunar days of a fortnight, considered auspicious for certain tasks but generally avoided for new beginnings</note>. The twenty-second [star] is undoubtedly "Time" <term>Kala: here meaning the personification of Death or the end of a cycle</term> for the king. In the Time-Fire Wheel <note>original: "Kalanala Chakra"</note>, seven specific limbs or divisions are described. 

In the path known as the "Thief's Path," the nine planets should be analyzed. Within the clarified Mantra Wheel of the Time-Fire, three stars are also considered here. In this system, the rivers and the wheels proceed in a sequence, but the fruits are determined by the planets alone. This is how it should be spoken, as seen in the Science of Sound <term>Svara-shastra: a branch of divination based on the sounds of a person's name, the breath, and the phonetics of the alphabet</term>. 

Saturn <note>original: "Sauri"</note> being in the name is beneficial; the Moon brings profit; Mars <note>original: "Lauhita"</note> brings... [results related to blood or heat]; and Mercury <note>original: "Bauddha"</note> should be considered for the dignity of the subjects. In the "Dry" station, one should perform the "Siddhi of Jewels" <note>an attainment or ritual for wealth</note>. In the "Mixed" station, the intellect is sharpened. In the wheel called the Thief-Time-Fire, the gain of garments and wealth arises in that specific cell <note>original: "koshthe," a square in the diagram</note>. This is the wheel of the stars. 

From this, one should remember "Time." This is not for common folk. It is for the start of wars and the attainment of success through the *Siddhi Yoga* <note>a combination for perfection/success</note>. This Thief-Time-Fire wheel is shaped according to the love of the people. For those to whom this "Thief’s Footprint" of the Time-Fire is in a favorable path, their fame travels across the earth, shining like the autumn moon. // Thus [the section ends].

## The Thief-Time-Fire Wheel
<note>original: "Chora-kalanala-chakram"</note>

One should draw the wheel by writing the twenty-seven stars <term>Nakshatra: the lunar mansions of Hindu astrology</term> starting from Ashwini. In the corners, one must perform the "piercing" <term>Vedha: an astrological aspect where a planet's position 'pierces' or affects another point in a diagram</term> in a transverse or slanted shape. 

The piercing of Ashwini is with Revati; the piercing of Magha is with Jyeshtha and Mula; the piercing of Ashlesha is with Agneya <note>Krittika</note>. In this manner, one should place the planets that have entered the wheel. If they strike the name of the King <note>original: "bhupa-namardi"</note>, a battle will surely occur; it cannot be otherwise. 

If there is no piercing, or if there is a "gentle" piercing <note>original: "saumya vedha," caused by benefic planets like Venus or Jupiter</note>, there is no war for the ruler of men. If there is a "cruel" piercing <note>original: "krura vedha," caused by malefic planets like Mars or Saturn</note>, a war will occur. At that time, the King will face a thief <note>original: "chora," often implying a rebel, raider, or invading enemy</note> seeking battle. If the ruler is well-informed and the piercing is gentle/benefic, he will be averse to war. According to the division of gentle and cruel planets, and the sequence of friend and enemy, the outcome of the thief's path should be spoken.

<summary>This page describes the construction and interpretation of the Thief-Time-Fire Wheel (Chora-kalanala-chakra), a divinatory tool used to predict outbreaks of war and the success of kings against insurgents.</summary>
<keywords>Kalanala Chakra, Svara-shastra, Nakshatra, Vedha, military astrology, King's fortune, planetary aspects, Chora (Thief/Invader)</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of astrological "wheels" (Chakras) used for military forecasting. The previous page ended with a discussion of planetary influences, specifically Mars and the Moon, on the success of a campaign. This page moves into more complex geometric diagrams used to predict the survival or defeat of a king in battle.</meta>

Whether there will be battle or not, and the presence or absence of a "couch" <note>original: "talpa," likely a metaphor for a position of rest or a funeral bier depending on context</note>, is determined by the **Enclosed Fire-of-Time Wheel** <note>original: "Samputa Kalanala Chakra"</note>. In this wheel, battle is analyzed through three constellations. Through the "Enclosed" method, [one determines] if there is a "Scissor" <term>Kartari: an astrological configuration where a point is hemmed in by two celestial bodies</term> among the three. Within the "Staff-Skull" <note>original: "Danda-Kapala"</note> formation, if it is situated... it destroys the three signs. In sequence, victory occurs. In the "Enclosed" and "Skull" positions, there is fear of death. If the "Thunderbolt" <note>original: "Vajra"</note> configuration is reached, battle occurs. If it stays in the "Scissor" or "Staff" positions, there is a breaking or defeat in war.

### The Twelve-Spoked Thunderbolt Wheel

Thus ends the discourse on the **Prickle Fire-of-Time Wheel** <note>original: "Gula Kalanala Chakra"</note>. Now begins the **Twelve-Spoked Thunderbolt Wheel** <note>original: "Dvadashara Vajra Chakra"</note>. It is composed of the twelve signs starting with Aries <note>original: "Mesha"</note>. One must know the two distinct fields named after the Sun <note>original: "Bhaskara"</note>.

The **Field of the Sun** is declared to be the signs from Leo to Capricorn.
The **Field of the Moon** <note>original: "Chandra-kshetra"</note> is, without a doubt, from Aquarius to Cancer.

1. When the Sun enters the Field of the Moon, and the Moon is also situated within the Moon's field, one should predict victory for the **Aggressor** <term>Yayina: the king who is marching or invading</term> and defeat for the **Defender** <term>Sthayini: the king who remains stationary or is being besieged</term>.

2. When the Moon enters the Field of the Sun, and the Sun is situated within the Sun's field, at that time success is achieved; otherwise, the result of the battle is reversed. In the *Brahmayamala* <note>a sacred text of the Tantric tradition</note>, it is said that the conjunction of these planets brings either death or victory.

When the Sun is joined with the Sun's influence and the Moon stays in the Moon's influence, then success is achieved; otherwise, the battle's outcome is reversed. If the Sun and Moon are both in a state of "Destruction" <note>original: "Samhara"</note>, it affects both parties. One must observe this wheel both at the time of a journey and at the time of war.

### The Power of the King’s Victory

Thus ends the **Power of the Sun Fire-of-Time Wheel**. Here, for the victory of kings, this knowledge is held in the hand <note>original: "haste," meaning it is readily available or mastered</note>. This "Thunderbolt Wheel" was composed by the elephant-headed Lord <note>original: "Hastina," likely referring to Ganesha or a sage of that name</note> in this chapter. The second chapter, which is pleasing and excellent, has been explained as it was found in the *Brahmayamala*—the "Fire-of-Time for Kings" <note>original: "Raja Kalanala"</note>—to create confidence in the outcomes of journeys.

1. The planetary conjunctions have been spoken of in these three chapters. In all auspicious works and journeys, they grant good fortune in battle.
2. If one desires to set out on a journey, one must look to the Moon and the Sun; one should speak of the influence and the results of actions based on their power.
3. Some say that this is the primary cause for the success of kings: just as the year is ruled by the Lord of Aries <note>Mars</note>, so too is Jupiter influential.
4. Those who hold lordship over the year, the month, the day, and the constellation—they are known as the authorities; they are the "Kings of Kings" ordained by fate.
5. This "Royal Inquiry" is well-known in the Vedas, the Agamas, and the Puranas. Its great, blazing tongue of flame speaks to the world.
6. In all matters of fortune, misfortune, and the casting of birth charts, this is considered direct evidence...

<meta>This page continues from the previous discussion on planetary influences and divinatory outcomes, transitioning into a specific system of grid-based divination called the Petha-cakra. It explores how the movement of the Sun and Moon through the four goals of life—Dharma, Artha, Kama, and Moksha—predicts success in travel, battle, and wealth.</meta>

<margin>
Ra.
Vi.
22
</margin>

Having understood the procedure for determining potency <note>original: "vīrya-pāna-vidhi," likely referring to the strength or efficacy of the divination</note> and the results of the planetary deities for those who are distressed, one should always seek the fruits of those who travel. Through the speech of the sages, one gains a connection with those who have attained yoga. By the increase of that attained wisdom, the fear of birth is removed for the lowly; one obtains the fruits of the previous deeds. Thus, the **Petha-cakra** <note>A divinatory diagram or grid used for predictions</note> is described. It is a diagram consisting of **thirty-two squares** <note>original: "dvātriṃśat-koṣṭaka"</note>. In this grid, the four aims of life are placed: **Dharma** <note>Duty/Righteousness</note>, **Artha** <note>Prosperity/Wealth</note>, **Kama** <note>Desire/Pleasure</note>, and **Moksha** <note>Liberation</note>. These four squares represent the foundation.

### Predictions based on the Grid

1. If **Dharma** is the path and the **Moon** is situated in **Artha**, what will happen in a fair battle? One should know that it will result in **victory**.

2. When **Dharma** grants **Artha**, it becomes a giver of victory. If the **Sun** is in **Dharma** and moves toward **Kama**, it grants results despite the presence of opposition.

3. If the **Sun** moves from **Dharma** to **Moksha**, it creates profit through an auspicious union. If it moves from **Artha** to **Dharma**, it creates profit rooted in auspicious deeds.

4. When both the **Moon and the Sun** are in **Artha**, or if they are in the position of **Rahu** <note>The North Node of the moon, associated with eclipses</note>, if the **Sun** moves from **Artha** to **Kama**, there is no certainty in travel or in one's work.

5. If the **Sun** moves from **Artha** to **Moksha**, it is half-auspicious and results in profit. If it moves from **Kama** to **Dharma**, and is joined with an auspicious planet, it results in profit.

6. If the **Sun** moves from **Kama** to **Artha**, it is said to grant wealth and wisdom if the auspicious **attendant spirits** <note>original: "bhaṭ-gaṇo," likely technical units of calculation or guardian spirits</note> have arrived together.

7. Where the **Sun and Moon** both move into **Kama**, it results in a favorable union. If the **Sun** moves from **Kama** to **Moksha**, and is joined with an auspicious planet, it results in the gain of wealth.

8. If the **Sun** is in **Moksha** and knows **Dharma**, it leads to profit and auspiciousness. However, if **Moksha** leads to **Artha** and back to **Artha**, those results are **fruitless**.

9. If **Moksha**, **Artha**, and **Kama** are all givers of results, one should set out for travel or for battle. When the **Moon and Sun** are in the path of **Moksha**, it brings the yoga of conflict <note>original: "vigraha," meaning war or separation</note>. This grid is to be consulted for travel and all auspicious times.

### Calculation for Travel

To calculate the success of a journey: Take the months starting from **Chaitra** <note>The first month of the Hindu lunar calendar, roughly March-April</note> and multiply them by three. Add the current **lunar date** <note>term: tithi<note>A lunar day in the Vedic calendar</note></term> and divide the total by nine. This determines the movement of the journey, whether it will be swift or otherwise.

1. The specific categories of outcomes are: **Narapatika**, **Radhasika**, **Sadhimanika**, **Yasahari**, **Vara**, **Mohavala**, **Indriyavati**, **Kavaka**, and **Patraca** <note>These appear to be technical names for the nine possible remainders of the calculation, indicating specific qualities of the journey</note>.

2. Thus ends the section on **Travel** <note>original: "Yātrā"</note> in the book named **The Elephant of the King’s Splendid Victory** <note>original: "Rājavirājavijeragahasti"</note>.

<meta>This page transitions from the thirty-two-square Petha-cakra to the Saptaghaṭa-cakra (Seven-Pot Diagram). # The Seven-Pot Diagram and the Results of Planetary Conjunctions

Following the words of the great elephants among sages <note>A metaphor for great or powerful teachers</note>, the chapter on the **Seven-Pot Diagram** <note>original: "saptaghaṭacakra," a divinatory chart likely shaped like seven ritual pots or involving seven containers of time/space</note> is beautifully described. In the third section, the results of the planetary conjunctions in the **Trines** <note>original: "trikoṇa," referring to the 1st, 5th, and 9th houses in a birth chart</note> are revealed through various "strike" events.

### The Omens of Conflict and Disaster

When **Iron** <note>original: "loha," here referring to "loha-pāta" or the falling of iron, a technical term for warfare, injury by weapons, or violent conflict</note> appears on a single line of the grid, involving the Sun, Saturn, and Rahu, it signifies destruction. If Saturn and the Sun act as enemies, or if they are situated among "iron trees" <note>likely a metaphor for a forest of spears or weapons</note>, one should predict the falling of iron upon others. If the Sun, the Son of the Sun <note>Saturn</note>, and Rahu are situated in that diagram, one should clearly point toward the outbreak of war.

Through the anger of the Lord of Creatures <note>Prajapati</note>, if the Moon is situated in the fourth house with steady intelligence, and the **Rain** rises, there will be water everywhere. If the Moon is in an auspicious sign, and Jupiter or Venus are in the trines, then a **Whirlwind** <note>original: "caṇḍa-vāta"</note> is born from the sign of Aquarius. If thundering is heard during the constellation of Shravana, the sixth or eighth houses are affected.

### The Descent of the Thunderbolt

If **Rahu and Ketu** are both situated in the **Ascendant** <note>original: "lagna," the sign rising on the eastern horizon</note> and the Moon and Mars are agitated, a **Thunderbolt Strike** <note>original: "vajra-pāta," meaning either a literal lightning strike or a sudden, total catastrophe</note> is proclaimed. 

When the Sun, Mars, and Jupiter are positioned in the center of the Ascendant, a thunderbolt falls in the middle of the path. If the lines are marked with red or yellow <note>referring to the colors used to draw the divinatory grid</note>, and a single line is touched by these "red-eyed" planets, then a falling of iron <note>warfare</note> will surely occur. Thus say the lords of the sages.

### Omens of Wind and Weather

Saturn's presence in the chart, alongside the Sun, destroys the strength of the Trines.
*   If the Sun is in Leo within the diagram's lines, and both are in the Ascendant, **Iron** <note>war</note> is strengthened.
*   If a line starts from the sky and reaches the seventh house where the Moon is situated, a **Wind Strike** <note>original: "vāyu-pāta," a destructive storm or gale</note> should be predicted.

In the nineteenth line, if Jupiter, the Sun, and Mars <note>original: "mahisuta," the 'son of the earth'</note> are in the Ascendant, then Iron <note>war</note> is favorable; however, in the second house, it results in a **Body of Diamond** <note>original: "vajra-kaya," perhaps implying a hard, unyielding disaster or a protective state</note>. Wherever the Sun sits on a line, and the Moon follows that same line, and Rahu also goes there, a **Vehicle of Iron** <note>military expedition</note> should be predicted. 

If the Sun and Saturn are situated together in the wind-lines of the chart, and the Moon is also on that line in a water-conjunction, then rain will fall in that place. When the Trines are occupied by malefic planets on a single line, or if the Moon comes into that same line, it signifies a coming fire or great heat.

<keywords>Saptaghaṭa-cakra, planetary conjunctions, omens, warfare, lightning strike, weather prediction, Rahu, Ketu, Saturn, Ascendant</keywords>

# The Falling of Iron and Planetary Battles

In those signs with retrograde or crooked motion <note>original: "apavaktra"</note>, first the servant-form of the **Falling of Iron** <note>original: "lohapaata"; an astrological configuration signifying war, the striking of weapons, or violent calamity</note> is described. In the middle portion of the moving signs, the result is water; in other signs, the "vajra" <note>the thunderbolt or diamond-strike</note> is well-known. In the **Trines** <note>original: "trikoṇa"; the 1st, 5th, and 9th houses</note>, the remaining signs are described as fiery and fixed, where water and the "Vara" sound appear. At the beginning of these signs, the destruction caused by the strike of iron is foretold.

### The Words of Uddisha on Grid Lines

The teaching of **Uddisha** <note>a name for Lord Shiva, often cited as the divine source of tantric and astrological knowledge</note> declares the following: when planets are situated on a single **Line** <note>original: "rekhā"; referring to the alignment on the astrological grid</note>, kings and the various directions are remembered as being scorched. If the planet **Saturn** <note>original: "shani"</note> is situated there, intense heat or drought will occur for a year. 

If a **Planetary Battle** <note>original: "graha-yuddha"</note> occurs between "poets" <note>likely referring to Mercury or Venus</note>, knowledge is tested. If the **Moon** <note>original: "chandra"</note> falls on a line with a **Malefic** planet <note>original: "krūra"; such as Mars or Saturn</note>, one should predict death or great destruction. When such malefic planets are united on a single line, an army approaches, and the signs of a battle-field are present. 

### Configurations of Conflict

Whether joined by a malefic or a benefic planet, the indicators of war are numerous. If **Saturn** and the **Sun** are combined with **Mars**, it creates a "mire of blood" <note>a poetic way of describing a massacre</note>. If joined with **Jupiter** <note>original: "guru"</note>, a "Stambha" <note>original: "sandhi-stambha"; a paralyzing of forces or a strategic stalemate</note> occurs. 

The strength of these planets—whether malefic or auspicious—must be understood to determine the outcome:
- **Valor and Wisdom:** 3rd House
- **Policy/Loss:** 12th House
- **Partnership/Desire:** 7th House
- **Duty/Religion:** 8th <note>The text lists 8, though Dharma is usually 9th</note> House
- **Enemies:** 6th House

By knowing the relative strength and weakness of these planets, the procedure for the **War-Strike** <note>original: "yuddha-pata"</note> is explained here. 

### The Pulse Diagram and Lunar Mansions

Through the movement of the fiery planets, the results are doubled. This concludes the description of the "Strikes" in terms of minutes <note>original: "vināḍika"</note>. If, through the combination of the day and the **Lunar Mansion** <note>original: "nakṣatra"</note>, the Falling of Iron occurs, it is known via the **Pulse Diagram** <note>original: "nāḍī-cakra"</note>. 

In the three corners of the diagram, the planetary "strikes" should be placed. On the path of the **Royal Banner** <note>original: "rāja-ketu"</note>, any planet that wanders like a king from the "mouth of destruction" <note>original: "samhāra-vadana"</note> and is joined by a malefic planet, leads to ruin...

# The Specifics of the Iron Strike and the Poet’s Diagram

In the rising sign, the **Falling of Iron** <note>original: "lohaṃ"; an omen of war or violent calamity</note> is specified. When the "Vajra-strike" <note>a severe astrological affliction</note> exceeds its own measure, and the planet **Saturn** <note>original: "shani"</note> enters into conjunction with a benefic or an enemy, the twelve divisions <note>original: "dvādasa-varga"</note> must be calculated.

When the planets reach the end of the six-fold path, starting from the constellation **Chitra** <note>the 'Brilliant' star, Spica</note>, water is indicated. The "four-fold iron" manifests in the paths at the end of the rainy season. Under the constellation **Purva Bhadrapada**, when the fifth lunar day <note>original: "tithi"</note> is combined with the specific day of action, and three parts remain, the **Falling of Iron** is declared. 

### The Manifestation of Rain and Ruin

When the creation of clouds occurs in the treasury of these deities, one should observe the lunar day. If the heat rises during the first half of the **Ghatikas** <note>original: "ghatikā"; a unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note>, and ten parts are on the left and the directions are severed, then **Prajapati** <note>the Creator deity, associated here with the constellation Rohini</note> resides in that lunar day. If there is abundant rain there, the "Iron" is drawn forth with great force. All of this must be known by those who understand the positions of the stars.

### Times of Prohibition

When this "Iron" is known to be active through these measures, one should not engage in the following activities:
*   Going out for **Hunting** <note>original: "mṛgayā"</note>
*   Entering a village or a home
*   The marching of an **Army** <note>original: "sainya-yāta"</note> (1)
*   **Weddings** <note>original: "vivāha"</note> or the meeting of friends
*   Treating wounds or performing surgeries
*   Visiting pilgrimage sites or divine lineages (2)

When the **Thunderbolt Strike** <note>original: "vajrapāta"</note> is present, the weak are destroyed. Knowing this, the supreme king should avoid these actions to remain auspicious. (3)

## Construction of the Poet’s Diagram

The **Poet’s Diagram** <note>original: "kavi-cakram"; 'Kavi' (Poet) is a name for the planet Venus (Shukra). This diagram is used for military and political divination</note> should be constructed as a square divided by three lines. The wise practitioner should place the fire-dwelling constellations <note>original: "agnibhā"</note> starting from the **East**. With four pillars, this Poet's Diagram is known. (5)

### Omens of War and Movement

The "Crest of the Poet" <note>likely referring to a specific position of Venus or a comet-like omen</note> and the "Reddish-Brown One" <note>original: "pingalā"; a reference to a specific bird omen or planetary color</note> indicate the entry into battle. There will be loss of horses or vehicles in the theater of war. (6)

When the **Moon** is situated with **Jupiter** <note>original: "jīva"</note>, one should calculate the purity of the Poet’s Diagram at the interval of entry or exit. (7) If the Poet’s Diagram is pure, the strike is averted; if it is malefic, then there will be death on the path or a great plague. (8)

### Specific Combinations for Victory and Defeat

*   **Thursday:** If a battle occurs on a day ruled by **Jupiter** <note>original: "jīva-vāra"</note> under a successful constellation, victory is achieved.
*   **The Red-Brown Omen:** If the "Red-Brown" omen appears, there is a fear of fire. (9)
*   **Punarvasu and Tuesday:** If the constellation **Punarvasu** falls on a Tuesday <note>original: "bhauma-dine"</note>, it is a "hundred-fold strike," signifying death in battle or the loss of cattle and trade. (10)
*   **Friday and Pushya:** If the constellation **Pushya** falls on a Friday <note>original: "bhṛgu-dine"</note>, even a person in bondage may attain freedom; a faulty configuration becomes faultless. (11)

Those who know the "Sound of the Setting Water" <note>a technical term for a specific astrological calculation</note> understand the birth-stars of the combatants. On that day, all those who are auspicious will succeed in the theater of war. (13) The entry of **Cruel Planets** <note>original: "krūra"; such as Mars or Saturn</note> brings fear, while the exit of **Benefic Planets** <note>original: "saumya"; such as Mercury or Jupiter</note> is auspicious for departure. (14)

### The Wheel of Knowledge and Victory

One should write the signs in a square with corners. Place one sign in the head, one in the throat, two in the ears, two in the hands, and four in the feet. Counting from the Sun's constellation to the birth constellation, wherever the Moon is situated, know the result for that position. If in the head: flight of the enemy. If in the ear: the enemy gains knowledge of your arts. In the left foot and the hands: ruin and loss of wealth. **(1)** In the right hand: one goes with bold and persistent women. In this Wheel of Knowledge, if a "strike" <note>original: "pāta"</note> occurs, success is difficult to find in the three worlds. **(3)** 

**Thus concludes the Wheel of Knowledge.** <note>original: "iti jñāyavatha-cakraṃ"</note>

Born **16** as a small hill fort, it is known that if water is present at that time, it is considered superior. For those with horses and infantry, the intermediate path is the one with sufficient water. **19**

Similarly, <term>Land-born Forts</term> <note>original: "Sthalaja-durga"</note> are of three types. If the water source is found by digging or is situated at a certain depth, **20** and if the water level corresponds to the <term>Orbit of Saturn</term> <note>original: "Shani-kaksha"; likely an astrological calculation for the depth or timing of water availability</note>, it is considered a Middling fort. In the context of the <term>Kota</term> <note>original: "Kota"; the fort or the fortress-diagram</note>, it is accompanied by the planets. **21** Among the Land-born types, if it is joined with the power of the "Vastra-yamala" <note>an obscure technical term, likely referring to a specific structural pairing or astrological alignment</note>, the <term>Protection of the Fort</term> <note>original: "Durga Raksha"</note> is accomplished even when the fort is under pressure. **22**

I shall now explain the remedy through the <term>Planetary Combinations</term> <note>original: "Graha-yoga"</note> in detail. Then, the capture of the fort is seen to happen when the planets are unfavorably aligned. **24** At the time when the food offerings <note>original: "naivedya"</note> are being presented, it is then that the divine masters should begin the <term>Capture of the Fort</term> <note>original: "Kota-grahana"</note>. **25**

The East, South, and the other directions—starting from the first half of the universe to the middle of the womb—the West, and the North; this <term>Arrangement of the Diagram</term> <note>original: "Chakra Vinyasa"</note> involving these four sections of stars is auspicious for the fort. **30** By examining the corners and the triangles, and observing the pillars and the exits, this constitutes the Arrangement of the Diagram. **31** It can be a square, an elongated triangle, a circle, or a long rectangle.

It should be known as a **half-moon** <note>original: "ardha-candra"</note> shape; the sphere there is like a vessel. **32** As for the field of movement, in the sequence of equal parts, the entry and exit <note>referring to the rising and setting of celestial bodies</note> occur similarly in the seven cycles. **33**

There are three ways to understand the <term>Planets</term> <note>original: "Graha"</note>: through their motion in the high, low, or middle paths. Along with these three, they are also associated with <term>Retrograde Motion</term> <note>original: "vakra"; literally "crooked" or "backward" motion</note>. **34**

The experts in the circle of the heavens distinguish the types of <term>Aspects</term> <note>original: "drishti"; the "gaze" or influence a planet casts on other signs</note> as upward-looking, even, or transverse. When the **Sun** <note>original: "Surya"</note> is in its fast-moving cycle, it is said to be "rising." In the second stage, it completes its course; in the third, it moves toward the end; and in the fourth, it reaches a state of stationary weight. **36**

The retrograde motion occurs in the fifth and sixth houses; by the seventh house, this "crookedness" has passed. When the Sun is in certain positions, a distorted or <unclear>difficult</unclear> movement is born. **37**

The power of the planets arises in the twelfth and eleventh houses. In the planetary cycle, it should be known that the **Sun** and the **Moon** are always in high motion. **38**

In "Great Motion" <note>original: "Atichara"</note>, the planet moves with high speed; in "Slow Motion" <note>original: "Manda"</note>, it appears sluggish. These are proclaimed as the states of retrograde, crookedness, and turning. **39**

A **Cruel Planet** <note>original: "Krura"; a malefic such as Mars or Saturn</note> becomes even more intense in its effects, while a **Gentle Planet** <note>original: "Saumya"; a benefic such as Jupiter or Venus</note> increases in gentleness. In the movement of the cycle, they remain balanced unless reversed by fast motion. **40**

**Aries, Virgo, Capricorn, Pisces, and Libra** are the signs in sequence. The Sun and other planets reach their <term>Exaltation</term> <note>original: "Ucca"; the point of highest strength</term> in these signs, while their <term>Debilitation</term> <note>original: "Nica"</note> occurs in the seventh sign from that point. **41**

The highest point of exaltation for the **Sun** is twenty-eight degrees, while for the **Moon**, it is three degrees... <note>the text continues listing the specific degrees of planetary strength</note>. **42**

The high and low positions of the planets are explained through these placements. The **Sun** and **Mars** <note>original: "Bhauma"</note> possess an **Upward Aspect**, while **Mercury** <note>original: "Budha"</note> and **Venus** <note>original: "Bhargava"</note> look downward. **43**

**Jupiter** <note>original: "Jiva"</note> and **Saturn** <note>original: "Shani"</note> are seen as having an even or horizontal aspect. The **Moon**, the **Sun**, and **Jupiter** are friends... Venus and the Sun... while Venus and Mercury are considered gentle/benefic... <note>The final lines provide a technical list of planetary friendships and enmities used to calculate the strength of a fort's defense</note>.

Those who reside in the night are the enemies; the **Moon** <note>original: "Chandra"</note> is 44. The natural enemies are thus declared: the distance of their internal anger is 10, 11, **Mercury** <note>original: "Budha"</note> 5, **Venus** <note>original: "Shri/Shukra"</note> 2, the **Sun** <note>original: "Ayana/Surya"</note> 1, and **Saturn** <note>original: "Sva/Shani"</note> 12. These have gone to their positions. At that moment, the perfection of mutual success is achieved. In the twin signs of exaltation, the pot-bearer <note>referring to Aquarius</note> is the trine. **Venus** and **Saturn** are the ones who delight. The **Sun** and **Moon** are the rays of that planet. **Mars** <note>original: "Mrityu/Yama/Kuja"</note> and the Sun are at 48. In the trines of **Leo** <note>original: "Simha"</note>, there are a hundred exaltations. In the sign of the **Fish** <note>original: "Mina"</note>, **Venus** and **Saturn** are of various kinds; there, **Mars**, the **Sun**, and the **Moon** are of seventeen measures. **Jupiter** <note>original: "Jiva"</note> and the Moon are at sixty measures. **48-49** 

In one’s own field, with the strength of all quarters, even if they are semi-equal, those who move in the quarters should be known as strong in the triple-check method. **49** When <term>Malefic Planets</term> <note>original: "Krura"; literally "cruel" planets like Mars or Saturn</note> are in the "womb" <note>the interior or center of a formation</note>, in three types of transformations, those situated outside should be considered. **50** 

Where the mouth of the enemy is, one should always avoid being trapped by them. **51** If one is situated outside, it is equal to the "womb" of the city; one should always avoid being trapped by the gods and demons above. **52** But if one is outside, equal to the womb of the city, and bathed by the gods, then a battle will occur where the stronger side prevails. **53** 

If <term>Malefic Planets</term> are in the interior "womb" and have emerged from the city, while <term>Benefic Planets</term> <note>original: "Saumya"; literally "gentle" or "moon-like" planets like Jupiter or Venus</note> are in the middle and the Malefics are outside, the fort is considered difficult to conquer. **54** 

When two Malefics are within the <term>Fort Walls</term> <note>original: "Prakara"</note> and a Benefic moves in the middle, pride is broken there; the city is taken without a fight. **55** If Malefics are stationed on the wall and Benefics are also in the middle, when a breach occurs in the fort, those wishing to enter will find it broken. **56** 

If Benefics are situated in the "middle channel" <note>original: "madhya-nadi"</note> and Malefics are stayed outside, the army turns back from the external enemy, and victory happens without a battle. **57** When Malefics stay both on the wall and inside the city, and the Benefics are outside, then the fort remains in a state of destruction. **58** If Benefics are in the middle and in the corner of the wall, while Malefics are outside, even the gods like **Brahma** and others [cannot take] the fort in battle...

If that retrograde motion ceases, then the residents of the fortress flee and the fort is abandoned at that time. **75** Just as it is for the Malefics, so should the Benefics be understood through the reversal of these effects. For mixed planetary configurations, one should know the results are mixed.

### The Seven-Spiked Diagram

In the <term>Seven-Spiked Diagram</term> <note>original: "Sapta Śalākā Cakra"; a grid of seven horizontal and seven vertical lines used to map stars and predict which direction or gate of a fort will fall</note>, one finds the position of Saturn. In whichever direction Saturn is situated, the "breaking" <note>original: "bhanga"; the breach or destruction of the wall</note> will occur in that specific direction. **54** If the army enters the fort-city and the King's power is established there, the enemy is broken. **55** 

If <term>Malefic Planets</term> <note>original: "krura"; literally "cruel" planets</note> are situated inside the fort and are joined by the King's own family, then the destruction of the stronghold is seen. If a Malefic planet looks toward the direction in which the army entered, that direction of the fort will fall. **56** If a retrograde planet <note>original: "vakra-ga"; a planet appearing to move backward</note> is present, the "Chakravarti" <note>original: "Cakravartī"; a universal monarch or high king</note> performs his actions, and the city is breached by various means. One should know that the city is won when the King enters the place. **57** 

Success is attained on a **Tuesday** <note>original: "Bhaumavāra"; the day of Mars, often associated with war</note>. If the siege occurs on this day, or if Mars is positioned in the "two hands" <note>referring to a specific Nakshatra or degree</note>, the entry will be successful. If the planets enter that very place, the city is seized. If a planet leaves the city and goes into a retrograde motion on a particular day, then there will be a breach in the eastern direction and a terrible fear will strike both armies. ...regarding the destruction of the fortress—if at that time an omen occurs while Saturn is positioned with the Sun, or if Jupiter is in a <unclear>favorable</unclear> position relative to the Sun and the day is Sunday, and even if Venus is retrograde, it signifies the destruction of the <term>citadel</term> <note>original: "koṭa"; a fortified stronghold or inner castle</note>. When food is scarce during a siege, and the omens regarding <unclear>Shiva</unclear> are unfavorable, a most terrible war ensues. If the planets are moving swiftly, the fort can be conquered; it will be protected if nine crores <note>original: "nava-koṭi"; ninety million, here likely signifying an immense or insurmountable number of defenses</note> of protections are in place. Having understood this thoroughly, one should begin the battle for the citadel, whether from the middle or from the outside. Whether the planets are "cruel" <note>original: "krūra"; malefic planets like Mars or Saturn</note> or "gentle" <note>original: "śubhā"; benefic planets like Jupiter or Venus</note>, one must consider where the planetary "gaze" <note>original: "dṛṣṭi"; the astrological aspect or influence a planet casts on a specific house</note> falls.

One should investigate the <term>varga</term> <note>original: "varga"; a system of grouping the Sanskrit alphabet into five-letter sets (Ka-varga, Ca-varga, etc.) used to determine compatibility between a commander and a location</note> of the enemy. If the attacker and the fort belong to the same group, and if they are in their proper places, a breaking of the defenses and defeat will occur. Men who belong to the same group as the fort’s category should not be used as commanders in that battle. However, if the fort and the commander are of the same group but possess a unified spirit, then the man will go forth and surely achieve the fort. The vowels, starting with 'A' <note>original: "akārādi svarā"; the phonetic series beginning with the letter A</note>, should be written in the East and other directions, and in the middle as well. In the place of the village or town, a defeat is indicated if the vowel of the fort's name matches certain criteria. On the day when the vowel of the fort's name is <gloss>strong</gloss>, one should begin the war; if it is otherwise, the fort will remain standing.

### Effects of Planets within the Fort

When the **Sun** enters the middle of the fort, there arises a sorrow concerning water.
When the **Moon** enters, there is a "breaking" or a great heat/destruction.
When **Mercury** enters, the men will possess great wisdom and strength.
When **Jupiter** is in the middle of the fort, there will be an abundance of food and plenty of water.
When **Venus** enters, death occurs; and when **Saturn** enters, disease follows.
When the King is in the middle of the fort and a conjunction of planets occurs, the defenses are pierced.
When **Ketu** <note>original: "ketu"; the descending lunar node, often associated with sudden upheaval or comets</note> enters the middle, there is a dispute among the commanders.

These results are spoken of when a single planet enters the middle; however, with the conjunction of "minor planets" <note>original: "upagraha"</note>, there is an extreme danger to life. Entry into the fort should be made when the Moon is in a certain phase and Jupiter is supportive. At midnight, the fort's activities should be signaled by the sounding of instruments. If the King is within, the wise should perform their duties at night with water. Both entry and exit are linked to the <term>shalyas</term> <note>original: "śalya"; literally "thorns" or "splinters," referring to hidden defects or astrological obstacles in the ground</note> of both parties. Who will achieve victory at the citadel, and who will suffer a contrary defeat? Such results are calculated by the wise through the yoga of planets and the sounds of the vowels. This applies to **water forts**, **hill forts**, and **land forts**, even when the planets are harsh or uneven.

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for military operations, shifting from purely astrological calculations (Vargas and planetary gazes) to the practical application of battle formations and the physical methods used to breach a fortress. One should understand the <term>Vyūha</term> <note>original: "vyūha"; a tactical military formation or array of troops</note> here, moving within the circle of the directions. When the second <unclear>Brahma-node</unclear> is in the <unclear>intelligence-giving</unclear> position, it is auspicious. Those who move in the fourth and fifth positions are like a heavy mass in the thick of battle at the great gate. The formation is thus proclaimed regarding the <term>breaking of a fortress</term> <note>original: "durga-bheda"; the specialized art of siegecraft and breaching fortifications</note> as it stands. When one has correctly reached that point, the "cruel" planets <note>original: "krūra"; malefic planets like Mars or Saturn that signify violence or destruction</note> wander there. Then, one must consider the sum of merit and sin at the moment of the <unclear>final fall</unclear>.

In the <term>Krittika</term> <note>original: "kṛttikā"; a lunar mansion or Nakshatra associated with fire and the god of war, Kartikeya</note> mansion, it is said that the mind is set on moving toward the wall. Some say the position is fixed by the <unclear>outer banner</unclear>. In whatever direction the Moon is located, that direction is "seen" by both sin and merit <note>astrologically influenced by benefic and malefic aspects</note>.

One should perform the **Test of the Sword** and the **Test in Battle**. Let one fill the space there with mango wood and other materials. If the scent of a lotus <note>original: "padma-gandha"</note> arises, it indicates success and fruitfulness. For such a battle, one should encircle the outer gate. Even if the fortress is difficult to reach and entangled, one must not let the mind waver from the position of the fort. Elsewhere, even through the methods of piercing and splitting, the King should conquer such a place of sin. 

One should bring it down using **stones, bricks, and various weapons**. In accordance with the <term>Mantra</term> <note>here likely referring to a technical formula or secret instruction for siegecraft</note>, one should strike with stones and rocks. From the pavilions, one should strike with noise and with <term>machines</term> <note>original: "yantrite"; refers to mechanical siege engines like catapults or ballistae</note> that cause the walls to tremble. Situated there, the attackers should strike from all sides with:

*   **Fire** <note>original: "agni"</note>
*   **Boiling oil** <note>original: "taila"</note>
*   **Molasses** <note>original: "guḍa"; used in incendiary mixtures to make them stick to surfaces</note>
*   **Water-spraying instruments**

The army should be visible, climbing with swords drawn, like gods. With the firm striking of the **elephant divisions** <note>original: "danti-dala"; elite war-elephant units used as ancient tanks to batter gates</note> and the shower of arrows released by the warriors, one should seize the fortress through the prescribed methods of counter-fortification. Even if it is a hundred thousand <term>Kroshas</term> <note>original: "krośa"; a traditional unit of distance, roughly 2 miles</note> away in a distant land, or if it is a "water-fortress," the King should conquer it. 

With jars full of water and well-prepared supplies, and with buffaloes and bulls who possess the strength of a **thunderbolt** <note>original: "vajra-dhāri"</note>, one should bring them from one's own region to the four directions. From all sides, the mercy of the King shall prevail. Then, one should commence the throwing of stones from both sides. One should stir the air with a self-made breeze. Fire and oil-based flames should be spread everywhere. Even in the absence of water, one should accomplish the task and master the difficult fortresses. By crushing, by blocking the path, and by burning—the King, joined with the **strength of elephants and horses**, should conquer the "human-fortress" <note>original: "nara-durga"; a metaphor for a dense, disciplined infantry formation that acts as a living wall</note>.

<margin>
Royal
Science
<note>original: "rā. vi."; likely abbreviations for Rājavidyā (Royal Science) or Rakṣā Vidhi (Procedure for Protection)</note>
</margin>

By various efforts, one may conquer an unbroken fortress, even when the means of doing so appear impossible. Likewise, the protection of what is feasible is achieved through the power of **mantras** <note>sacred chants or formulas</note> and **yantras** <note>mystical geometric diagrams used as tools for focus or power</note>. As it was said in ancient times in the *Adi Yamala* <note>a foundational text in the Tantric tradition</note>, fortresses are proclaimed to be of three types: superior, inferior, and middling, as well as those that are "unconquerable."

The Goddess of Virtue spoke regarding these: In inaccessible great fortresses situated in difficult or uneven terrain, one should perform rituals involving **yantras** and **mantras**. These include **immobilization** <term>stambhana</term> <note>the ritual act of paralyzing an enemy's movements or speech</note>, **sowing discord** <term>vidveṣa</term>, **lethal magic** <term>māraṇa</term>, **expulsion** <term>uccāṭana</term>, **fire sacrifices** <term>homa</term>, and **enchantment** <term>mohana</term> <note>the act of deluding or mesmerizing the inhabitants of the fort</note>.

Within the center of the fort, the "Sixteen-fold Great Power" <note>original: "ṣoḍaśāṅga... mahāvallā"</note> resides. For the purpose of destroying everything, one should carry out the rituals of conflict. One can achieve success in the fort in an instant through efforts that bring about the destruction of one's own enemies. In this very way, to reverse an enemy's advantage, one should perform the **Sixteen Moments** ritual.

Draw a **circle** <term>cakra</term> with twelve spokes, adorned with three outer rings. Place the **Eight-Syllable Mantra** within it. In the center of this, meditate upon the King of Death, **Yamaraja** <note>the god of death and justice, often invoked in rituals involving life and death</note>. To overcome the two-fold terrors of death and to remain free from disease, one should recite this **Peace Mantra** <term>śānti mantra</term> ten thousand times. One should perform ten thousand fire offerings with clarified butter, honey, and red flowers.

Similarly, in the fire of conquest, one should recite the thirty-two-syllable mantra ten thousand times. For the purpose of killing or creating division among the guards, one should proceed according to the prescribed rules. At that moment, one should offer worship to **Yamaraja**.

In the *Bahya Jamala*, it is said: for the peace of the fortress, draw a square **Fortress Circle** <term>koṭacakra</term> with four gates and four paths. It should be joined with the powers of the Sun, Indra, and other deities. Place the lunar mansions starting from **Krittika** <note>the Pleiades</note> in groups of seven in a four-fold sequence.

Write these seven-by-seven sequences according to the path. In the East gate, place the syllable *Da*; in the South, *Pa*. <unclear>Seven and seven</unclear> are placed in the North gate joined with the Moon. If "cruel" planets <note>malefic planets like Mars or Saturn</note> wander there, then the city is seized and success in entering is not achieved. 

On the right side of the gate, "gentle" planets <note>benefic planets like Jupiter or Venus</note> should be placed, and "cruel" planets on the left. To enter the city, one should worship the town. If the cruel planets are on the right and the gentle on the left, it provides protection. If the Moon is situated at the gate, it causes the destruction of diseases. When cruel planets are on the left, it is always auspicious. If the gentle planets are on the right, it bestows the **breaking of the city** <note>original: "purabhaṅga"; the successful breaching or conquest of the fortification</note>. This is determined by the lunar day, the mansion, and the day of the week.

...provided with these, one should divide by eight <note>original: "vasu," a symbolic word for the number eight</note>. Knowing this, O beautiful one, be attentive.

**Shri Devi spoke:**
Listen, O Lord! I have heard all this as it was told by you—the essence of the waves of wisdom. O Lord of the remnants, tell me more.

**Shri Bhairava spoke:**
Listen, O Goddess, to the expanded and pure teaching of the rhythmic pulse. It is the essence of essences, the most excellent secret. Merely by knowing this, a man attains the status of a god. He should recite the mantras in the lunar mandala <note>a mystical circle or sphere of influence</note>. He should be worshipped by the common people through gifts and lineage. This must be meditated upon in the assembly, O Goddess, and not otherwise. By this, one should ward off enemies in all matters concerning the gods. For the sake of nourishment and protection, one should perform the recitation of mantras.

The banners and flags should be raised, accompanied by the sounding of the kettle-drums <term>damaru</term> and trumpets <term>kahala</term>. In the deep of the night, for the purpose of warding off the enemy army and breaking their ranks, one must perform:
*   **Immobilization** <term>stambhana</term>
*   **Expulsion** <term>uccāṭana</term>
*   **Delusion** <term>mohana</term>
*   **Sowing Discord** <term>vidveṣa</term>
*   **Lethal Magic** <term>māraṇa</term>

These peaceful and fierce acts should be done for one's own service. After the preparation of the "Hero’s Cloth" <note>a ritual banner or garment</note>, one should place the **Sarvatobhadra Chakra** <note>the "All-Auspicious Wheel," a complex grid used for divination and ensuring military success</note>. To ensure it is not struck by obstacles, one should envision the Hero there. In the Great Sound <term>maharava</term>, the Hero whose name is greater [than the enemy's] prevails. The Hero associated with the enemy's name is overcome. One should give to him...

...the Victory-Increasing treatise <note>original: "Jayavardhani"</note>, [produced by] the one who rests at the lotus feet of the illustrious King of Heroes. Informed by the wisdom declared in various texts, and endowed with the ornaments of success and elevation, one should proceed. 

One should offer milk, consecrated water, and other ritual items with a focused mind. Amidst the sounds of the five instruments <note>the *pancha-shabda*, a set of five traditional musical instruments used in royal or religious processions</note> and shouts of "Victory!", all the petitioners should be provided with gifts. One must offer salutations to the Teacher <term>Acharya</term>, show loyalty to the Lord, and then worship the assembly of Yoginis and the god Bhairava.

O Auspicious One, the land is classified as high, middle, or low. High ground is superior for the King; level ground is middling; and low ground is considered inferior. These three types of terrain have presiding deities: the Ruler <note>the King or a high-ranking official</note> governs the high ground, the Sun <term>Divakara</term> governs the level ground, and the Moon governs the low ground, presiding over fame and strength.

O Goddess, one should station their own army <term>Sainya</term> at the front of the field. These should be deployed by one who is diligent and follows the prescribed methods. The guardian of the field and the Yoginis should be honored. One should gratify the chosen deity with offerings of various substances. By contemplating the form of Bhairava, one gains power; he grants fearlessness to one's own side while terrifying the enemy army. One should offer the blood of horses and elephants, or even human sacrifice <note>original: "narashvade," likely referring to symbolic or archaic fierce tantric offerings</note>, to satisfy the bow-wielding Bhairava. 

When a King is thus prepared and established on the earth, the Great Goddess Kali, the mistress of the gods, stands ready for the time of war <term>Yuddha</term>. One should then observe the omens <note>original: "chihna," signs or marks</note> of both one's own army and the enemy's. Between the two armies, wherever a divine brilliance is seen, victory is certain; if it is absent, defeat follows. If signs resembling white cranes or swans appear over the forces, it is an omen of success. However, if the army takes on the dark hue of crows or cuckoos, the situation for that army will become extremely dire.

In the eight types of conflict <term>Vigraha</term>, for the destruction of the enemy's forces, even eighteen enemy kings will be routed, fleeing on their own like prey before a lion. These instructions from the teacher <term>Deshika</term> should be followed. O Goddess, within these garments [of secret knowledge], after knowing this, one should commence the battle <term>Sangrama</term>. Especially on the power of the earth, as stated in the *Brahma Yamala*. This is the supreme essence, O Goddess, of the guidance provided by the teacher. In a battle with a king, or in a struggle against the laws of one's own kind, victory comes in order through the grace of the Guru's feet. He who understands these steps shall be granted victory. For the sake of slaying the demon Taraka, this was revealed to Uma...

In this *Yamala* <note>a category of Tantric texts, specifically the *Gurutithechadi Yamala* mentioned here</note> tradition, this four-fold knowledge <note>original: "chaturanga-jnanam", referring to the four divisions of an army: elephants, chariots, cavalry, and infantry</note> should be given. It should always be granted to a devotee of Shiva, as it was spoken by Shiva himself in ancient times. ### The Seven-Lath Diagram
### (Sapta-Shalaka Chakra)

The diagram consists of seven horizontal and seven vertical lines. One should plot the lunar mansions <term>Nakshatras</term> starting from the star Krittika. Including the star Abhijit <note>an intercalary lunar mansion often used in specialized astrological calculations</note>, there are twenty-eight stars to be drawn. 

From Krittika to Dhanishta, they are placed in sequence. In the center is the star Ardra <note>original: "Raudra"</note>. When planets enter into mutual "piercing" <term>Vedha</term> <note>an astrological aspect where planets block or strike one another's influence</note>, it affects the pillars of the army, the horses, and the seven lords of the chariots. 

The Sun <term>Ravi</term> is associated with fire <note>original: "Vahni"</note> and the time of destruction. From the Moon <term>Soma</term>, the eighth position indicates strife, serpents, or thieves. 

The secondary planets <term>Upagraha</term> are calculated in sequence:
* From the Moon: the Salty or Wrathful one.
* From Jupiter <term>Jiva</term>: the cycle of movement.
* From Venus <term>Bhargava</term>: the secondary influence.
* Saturn <term>Shanaishchara</term>, the North Node <term>Rahu</term>, and the South Node <term>Ketu</term> are also plotted. 

The Lord of Death <term>Yama</term> and the seven forms of Rahu are considered in the separation and union of the stars. Ketu is seen as the light-bearer.

### Planetary Strengths in Battle

Saturn is the lord of the earth (9). The Moon <term>Soma</term> and Ketu govern the twelfth positions. Jupiter <term>Guru</term> and the son of the Moon (Mercury) <term>Budha</term> are associated with the lords of the directions. When Saturn resides in its own region, it becomes the lord of the host. Venus <term>Bhargava</term> is the lord of the masters, while Mercury <term>Budha</term> is the ruler of the outcome. 

In a moveable ascendant <term>Chara-lagna</term> or sign, a planet with fast motion possesses great strength. When the Sun <term>Savitar</term> is in the host's sign, one should seek the path of the enemy. 

If a planet enters a retrograde division <term>Vakra-bheda</term>, the hero does not ascend to victory; rather, he stays put. If a planet is combust <note>rendered invisible by the Sun's proximity</note>, the enemy will experience joy, as this state brings about death and decline. When Rahu is joined with enemies, it surely results in death. 

Even if planets are in fixed signs <term>Sthira-rashi</term> or fixed divisions, they may still lose their established strength. If they are in dual-natured signs <note>signs like Gemini or Pisces that share qualities of both fixed and moveable signs</note>...

<meta>This page continues the technical rules of military astrology, transitioning from the construction of diagrams to the interpretation of specific planetary positions (Lagnas) and subdivisions (Drekkanas).
