<margin>Ra-<br/>Vi-</margin> <note>original: "ra-vi-"; likely shorthand for Rashi (Zodiac Sign) and Vidha (Method) or the planets Rahu and the Sun (Ravi).</note>

If a battle occurs, there will be prosperity and victory in that place. In a **fixed ascendant** <term>Sthira Lagna</term> <note>zodiac signs that represent stability and endurance: Taurus, Leo, Scorpio, and Aquarius</note>, or if the zodiac sign is fixed, the situation remains stable. In a **dual-natured sign** <term>Dvisvabhava</term> <note>signs that alternate between movement and stability: Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, and Pisces</note>, that which is fixed remains so. In a **movable ascendant** <term>Chara Lagna</term> <note>signs representing change and initiative: Aries, Cancer, Libra, and Capricorn</note>, there is a change of state; thus say the wise. 

In a fixed sign, if the dual-nature is obstructed, then **death** <term>Mrityu</term> occurs. In the **decan** <term>Drekkana</term> <note>a subdivision where each zodiac sign is divided into three ten-degree sections</note>, if the "fighting" decan is active, one carries the threat of death. Weakness or impotence follows from the influence of the decan. 

The relationships of "friend" and "great friend" <note>original: "mitradhimitra", referring to a complex Sanskrit system of planetary relationships</note> should be observed at the time of the rising sign. When the **Lord of the Ascendant** is strong and in its highest placement <term>Exaltation</term>, one should declare **victory**. However, if the decans are unfavorable or the planets have fallen into their lowest placement <term>Debilitation</term> <note>original: "Neecha"</note>, or if they are weak and situated in an enemy’s house, then death in battle is indicated.

When malefic planets <term>Papa-graha</term> <note>planets considered naturally harsh, such as Mars or Saturn</note> afflict the stars of one’s birth or royal consecration, then even the **King of the Earth** <note>original: "Mahipati"</note> meets his death. The Sun <term>Surya</term>, also called the Maker of Light <term>Bhaskara</term>, and the Moon <term>Soma</term> are the lords of the two primary classes <term>Varna</term> in order. If the lord of one's class is gazed upon by an enemy planet or situated in an enemy’s house, death will be encountered.

Mars <term>Bhauma</term>, Rahu, and Ketu are the lords of the lower classes, such as the Shudras, in their respective order. The Sun is the jewel of the day. Jupiter <term>Guru</term> and Venus <term>Kavi</term> are the priests <term>Brahmins</term>, especially when joined with the star Rohini. The Sun's son <note>Saturn</note> is associated with Leo... <note>the manuscript here correlates specific animal/class traits with planetary origins</note>.

Mercury <term>Somaja</term> represents the merchant class <term>Vaishya</term>. Mars <term>Bhauma</term> is the lord of those who live by fire and weapons. Jupiter <term>Brihaspati</term> and Venus <term>Bhrigu</term> are the planetary poets. Ketu is born of <unclear>nirahara</unclear>. Saturn <term>Shani</term> is born of the outcastes. If the lords of death are in the house of "piercing" <term>Vedha</term> <note>an astrological aspect where planets block or strike each other's influence</note> and are moving rapidly, life does not persist.

If Saturn or the Sun are in their lowest state <term>Debilitation</term>, and there is a conjunction with an enemy during a "piercing" aspect, death is seen when the enemy planet gazes upon them. If combined with death in the **seventh house** <note>the house of the opponent in war astrology</note>, yet the planets move fast and are joined with Venus, then a **peace treaty** <term>Sandhi</term> should be understood between the two opposing forces. 

When the Moon is joined with Mercury and is seen by a warrior-planet, or if it is in the eighth house and strong but gazed upon by an enemy, the hero at the head of the army will be destroyed. If seen by a friend while in **retrograde motion** <term>Vakra</term>, the army turns back from the left-hand path...

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for military astrology, moving from the basic interpretations of the ascendant to the specific physical and moral qualities required of a military commander (Senapati) and how planetary movements—specifically speed and retrogradation—affect the outcome of a siege or battle.</meta>

<page-num>51</page-num>

If the rising sign is viewed by a **friendly planet** <note>original: "mitradrishte"</note>, the person returns safely from the journey; even if coming from the midst of enemies or grief, they arrive without fear. 39. If viewed by a **malefic planet** <term>Papa-drishta</term> <note>harmful influences from planets like Mars or Saturn</note>, the man goes forth but is not called back; he wanders off the proper path. However, if he is strengthened by the gaze of a **benefic friend** <note>original: "maitreya-lokitah"</note>, he remains vigorous. 39.

Regarding physical ailments or humors during the expedition: From **Mars** <term>Bhauma</term> and others, the "six winds" <note>likely referring to a classification of nervous or digestive disorders</note> arise. From **Venus** <term>Shukra</term> and **Jupiter** <term>Ijya</term>, conditions born of good deeds or balanced humors occur. 40. From the **Sun** <term>Surya</term> and **Moon** <term>Soma</term>, ailments arise from food-faults or wind. From **Rahu**, difficulties or poison-like conditions emerge. From **Ketu** and similar nodes, ailments come from bindings or injuries. From **Mercury** <term>Budha</term>, all types of "wind" disorders <term>Vata</term> <note>in Ayurvedic medicine, Vata represents the air and ether elements, governing movement and the nervous system</note> are known by the wise. In the zodiac sign of Jupiter, wherever the target is, there will be a defeat of that position. 41.

Therefore, the results of the **sounds and vowels** <term>Svara</term> <note>the Sarvatobhadra Chakra uses a grid of vowels and consonants to determine fate based on a person's name</note> and the diagrams such as the **All-Auspicious Grid** <term>Sarvatobhadra Chakra</term> mentioned previously should be analyzed. These are the causes for the breaking of forts and the fate of men. In the center of the grid, one finds the truth of the army's strength. The commander <term>Senapati</term> who seeks refuge in the strength of numbers or the "strength of ten" in the eastern lands becomes powerful. 

The appointment of the **Vanguard** <term>Senamukha</term> must be performed; upon their strength depends the entire army. In whichever birth sign the Moon and Sun are placed, the commander's valor is determined by the presence of certain vowels and planetary positions. 

A king should **not** appoint a man as an **Army Commander** <term>Senapati</term> if he is:
*   Quickly overcome by disease.
*   A burden to others or born of a timid family.
*   One who slanders teachers <term>Guru</term>, gods <term>Deva</term>, or priests <term>Dvija</term>.

Instead, kings should appoint a commander who:
*   Takes delight in worshipping teachers, priests, and gods.
*   Is devoted to **Righteousness** <term>Dharma</term> and is pure of heart.
*   Is loyal to his master and born of a noble family.
*   Is brave, daring, and a master of the **Scriptures of Weaponry** <term>Shastra</term>.
*   Is respected by his peers and skilled in leading **war-elephants** <term>Ranahastina</term>.

Regarding planetary motion: If **Jupiter, Venus, the Sun, Saturn, Mercury, and the Moon** are situated in certain "hours" or divisions, an **astrological piercing** <term>Vedha</term> <note>a technical term where one planet's position "strikes" or obstructs another's influence</note> occurs. Even for a conqueror, if this "piercing" is present, victory may not come. 

If all the planets moving in the sky are in **Direct/Fast Motion** <term>Sheeghra</term>, a conflict or war will break out among the kings situated in those specific directions. When all planets are in fast motion, there will be opposition to the king. When all planets enter **Retrograde Motion** <term>Vakra</term> <note>appearing to move backward in the sky from the perspective of Earth</note>, the war becomes terrible and violent. Even if the victors are usually those with fast-moving planets, if planets are retrograde, victory may not be achieved as expected.

If the **defenders** <note>original: "sthapinashcha," referring to the stationary or besieged party</note> are positioned in the night, and the **invaders** <note>original: "jayine," the attacking or victorious party</note> are situated to the south, the victory belongs to the travelers. In that place, the residents are the ones who grant victory; the invaders are at the end of the King's reach. Then, at the end of the battle, sin arises. When **malefic planets** <note>original: "papa"</note> are **retrograde** <note>original: "vakra," appearing to move backward in the sky</note>, even with auspicious blessings, the battle is always fierce. The path of the **Moon** <note>original: "chandra-rekha"</note> going retrograde provides victory to the defenders. If **Venus** <note>original: "shukra"</note> is retrograde and **Jupiter** <note>original: "guru"</note> is moving fast, it is said to provide destruction for the defenders. If the King’s star is obscured or struck, one should speak of a gap in peace for ten leagues <note>original: "yojana," a traditional unit of distance roughly equal to 8-9 miles</note>.

When the lines of the mystical diagram reach the enemy, the stationary ones will slay them. If the army is situated on the enemy’s side, it creates a blockage of the path. When a single line reaches the enemy, the flaw of death follows. When the diagram’s line reaches a friend's path, there is a desired meeting. Through the crossing of friends, even an enemy may become a friend. If one succeeds in entering the midst of the enemy, enmity arises. Whether it be the color of the enemy or the speed of the tide, the King’s nation may suffer a fracture or fall from the sky. The first man killed in the place of human effort, falling with his face toward the summoner, signifies victory; otherwise, it is a defeat. If the first to fall are those of high rank, the battle should be abandoned with effort, for the feet will tremble.

A man who is wealthy, an expert in weapons, or a King's horseman—there the army of the King shall obtain victory on the earth. This is the **Four-Limbed Army** <term>Chaturanga sena</term> <note>the traditional division of an ancient Indian army: elephants, chariots, cavalry, and infantry</note> as described by the generals. Its lord is like the heart; the **elephants** <note>original: "gaja"</note> are the shoulders; the **horses** <note>original: "vaji"</note> are the two arms; the **infantry** <note>original: "dhana/padati"</note> are the wrists and hands. The **Minister** <note>original: "mantri"</note> is the tongue; the twice-born <note>original: "dvija," here referring to the teeth or perhaps the scholarly class</note> are the teeth; the **Deities** <note>original: "devata"</note> are the eyes. The breath and hearing are the **spies** <note>original: "charaka"</note>. The banners, the umbrella, the fly-whisk, and the flag are the locks of hair. All the military equipment is the body hair. 

Thus, remembering this, the rise of the gift is given. At death, one eye is in the heart, one in the tongue, one in the directions, and one on the back of the ocean. In the "Serpent Formation" <note>original: "bhujanga-vyo," a zigzag military formation</note>, the heart is in the horse, the heart is in the heart—each one is pure in the belly. Four at the end... <unclear>dmugamashimutamatapuna</unclear>... again retreating. One should not perform these actions when impure. Then, as if churned by a mountain, he becomes overwhelmed. In the interval of the plantain tree, there joined the brownish-red... worshipped by white servants.

...even if standing at the edge of the stone, one who bears a weapon should be considered. When the lord of the weapon is mentioned, whether the action is hostile or auspicious depends on the specific signs.

Now, the description of the **Sword Diagram** <note>original: "Khadga-chakram"</note> is given. One should draw the sword-shaped diagram endowed with nine divisions. Starting from the constellation where the warrior is stationed, the wise should write three stars in each section. If **malefic planets** <note>original: "krura," literally 'cruel' ones like Mars or Saturn</note> are positioned there, destruction and the fear of death arise in battle. If **benefic planets** <note>original: "somya," 'gentle' ones like Jupiter or Venus</note> are present, victory and profit are achieved. 

Regarding the edge of the sword: if malefic planets are there, the battle is won through harshness. If benefic planets are present, there will be "Ranga" <note>likely referring to a colorful display or a theater of success</note>. With mixed planets, mixed results follow.

**Thus ends the Sword Diagram.**

In the three-hand measurement <note>original: "hasta," a cubit or the length from the elbow to the fingertip</note>, the commencement of the practice of the **Science of Archery** <note>original: "Dhanurveda"</note> is praised. A bowstring that is well-prepared, thin, long, smooth, well-bound, and producing a good sound is considered to have the best qualities. 

The five stances of an archer should be known:
1. **Samapada** <note>feet together</note>
2. **Vaishakha** <note>feet apart</note>
3. **Mandala** <note>circular stance</note>
4. **Alidha** <note>right knee bent, left leg extended forward</note>
5. **Pratyalidha** <note>left knee bent, right leg extended forward</note>

The **Sharanga** bow <note>The celestial bow of Lord Vishnu</note> is always his own, the king of all bows. One should avoid bows that have a broken back, are crooked, or are overly long or thick at the end. Such is the nature of the weapon. 

Lord **Maheshvara** <note>Shiva</note> is the presiding deity of the bow. By merely remembering these instructions, one is released from all sins. 

In the **Bow Diagram** <note>original: "Chapa-chakram"</note>, which is drawn with the bow, the string, and the arrow, one should write three lunar mansions <note>original: "Nakshatras"</note> in each section. Place three at the base of the arrow, three in the middle, and three at the tip. Likewise, place two or three in the base and middle of the bow, and three on the string. 

The results are as follows:
- At the **base of the arrow**: death.
- In the **middle of the arrow**: disease.
- At the **tip of the arrow**: victory.
- In the **middle of the string**: disease.
- On the **bow body**: fear and loss of wealth.
- At the **ends of the bow**: conflict and death.

If malefic planets are joined here, it is particularly undesirable. Conversely, if there is a "Somya" <note>benefic</note> conjunction, one should predict victory and profit in the Bow Diagram. When there is a mixture of auspicious and inauspicious planets, the result follows the stronger planet.

**Thus ends the Bow Diagram.**

The various types of swords include the **Taravari**, the **Parisha**, the long sword, the **Narasimhaka**, the **Kapalaya**, and the **Vanaka**. One should also know the **Dagger** <note>original: "Chhurika"</note>, which measures nine finger-widths <note>original: "angula"</note>... <unclear>Chhurika</unclear>...

**Chamunda** <note>original: "Rivamunda," a variant spelling of the fierce goddess</note>, **Kalika**, **Meru**, **Lakshmika**, **Bhairavi**, **Yogini**, and **Tripura** are the peaceful agents <note>or 'pacifiers'</note>. These should be fourteen *angulas* <note>a traditional unit of measurement roughly equal to a finger's width</note> in size, while the girls <note>subsidiary figures</note> are two. The **Moon-born** <note>the planet Mercury</note> and the pure ones are famously measured at five *angulas*. The staff <note>dandika</note> is said to be twenty-one *angulas*. From the navel, the swing <note>dolaka</note> and the half-circle diagram are known. The warriors should be placed like a sword.

All these implements have been described. Having reached the position of the sound itself, if **malefic planets** <note>original: "krura," refers to harsh planets like Mars or Saturn</note> are situated on the cloth <note>the diagram surface</note>, and **benefic planets** <note>original: "somya," gentle planets like Jupiter or Venus</note> are in the region of the heart, one obtains the following results:

1. When the King is in his station, and the Sun is in the place of the fire, chariots, and so on, and the planets are in the strength-position, there are two elephants in front, and the **four-fold army** <note>the *chaturanga*, consisting of elephants, chariots, cavalry, and infantry</note> achieves worship <note>success</note>. In the place of the banner <note>Ketu</note>, success is born. 

If all the malefic planets are in the limbs, death is predicted. If the planets are mixed, the results are mixed. If the benefics are weak, they cause a "break" <note>defeat</note>. If **Saturn** <note>original: "Manda"</note> is in the head, the fire of the intellect and the earth are destroyed. Here, **Rahu** <note>the shadow planet of the ascending node</note> provides an obstacle to the King's success. If **Mars** <note>original: "Bhauma"</note> is in the belly, it causes disease or pain. Below the navel, if the benefics are placed, they bring victory. If the positions are reversed, the army is destroyed. If the planets are mixed, there is a break <note>failure</note> in battle, unless the power of the stones/weapons is greater. Victory belongs to the King if the benefics are more powerful.

13. **Thus ends the Army-Form Diagram.** <note>original: "iti senarupa-chakram"</note>

When elephants, horses, and commanders are large and cold-hearted <note>undeterred</note>, they cause the destruction of the enemy even without weapons. I shall now describe those diagrams with their characteristics. 

A measurement of **fifty angulas** is considered the best; **twenty-five** is the lowest. Between these two is the medium. Regarding the **Sword** <note>khadga</note>, it is said to be seven *angulas* wide at the front. It may be marked with the shapes of a banner, the **Shrivatsa** <note>an auspicious curl of hair or mark</note>, the **Vardhamana** <note>a symbol of prosperity</note>, or the **Swastika**. With these marks, even if it is struck or dented by a weapon, it remains auspicious. Otherwise, if it is notched or broken, it foretells death and the loss of the treasury.

It concludes with the ideal physical measurements and auspicious markings (like the Swastika) for a warrior's sword.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the omens regarding planetary positions. The previous page ended mid-sentence discussing the influence of malefic planets on the King’s fortunes. This section transitions from general military omens to specific diagrams (chakras) used for divination, specifically the Ankurika (Sprout) and Atikrata (Overcoming) diagrams, which utilize iconometric measurements.</meta>

...[If all the malefic planets are] in a position of defeat, then victory is weighed. Who can say what follows when the [auspicious] power has departed? Without cause, one produces a region of death <note>original: "mṛtyudeśaṃ," a specific sector of a divinatory map indicating fatal outcomes</note> that was not previously known. If one even touches the river or the Eagle-deity <note>original: "Tārṣya," referring to Garuda</note> with the sacrificial gift, a pure man remains.

In the region of death, the shape is like the leaf of the **Sun-plant** <note>original: "arka," Calotropis gigantea</note> or the **Oleander** <note>original: "karavīra"</note>. If an eight-legged figure arises there, and **Takshaka** <note>the King of Serpents in Hindu mythology</note> falls upon it, he causes great change at the root. The memory of the Mother <note>the goddess</note> is the only purification. Whatever limb she touches, she draws out the essence of the poison. Even the hair is transformed. One should specify that his son is endowed with the strength of war.

The following omens are seen regarding the women and the leaders:
If one is smeared with the filth of a wound, or if the King of Oleanders is broken, or the Jasmine-maker is struck. In the sacrifice for the prosperity of the cattle, if the signs are like the urine or wings of a bird, or the fat of clarified butter, it indicates a desire for silk.

**Omens of Consumption:**
*   From **drinking blood**, one obtains a gain of mountains and auspicious things.
*   From **drinking water**, one attains the perfection of wealth.

When the plowman's yoke-cloud or the bull-tail cloud is struck, the farmer’s trees are destroyed.

### The Sprout Diagram
<note>original: "Aṅkurika Chakra," a divinatory grid used to predict growth or the success of an undertaking</note>

In the field of the **Sun’s** <note>original: "Arka"</note> moisture, there are twenty-eight positions for the Sun's light. One is in the fire, and others are within the water-vessels. In the groups of three, a **malefic planet** <note>original: "pāpagraho," such as Mars or Saturn</note> or perhaps **Mercury** <note>original: "Budha"</note> combined with the Moon’s node <note>Indra-pāya, likely referring to Rahu or Ketu</note> may be situated. Where those three are placed, there is a pair of planets. 

The **Sprout Diagram** should be eleven cubits <note>original: "kara," a measurement from elbow to fingertip</note> in size, or perhaps nine, seven, or five. Strength is achieved through these measurements. On the three-fold surface, the **Three-Roots** <note>trimūlam</note> are in the hand, and the **Hundred-Roots** <note>satāmūlam</note> are in three cubits. Two and a half cubits are for the stake <note>śaṅko</note>, and one cubit for the pestle. In the region of the blacksmiths, the measurement is three cubits. In the form thus created, the "Root-Staff" is established. 

One must draw the lines starting from the day-star <note>the Sun</note> in groups of three. One should announce the "fear of the Lord" in those groups of three where the fruit is harsh. If the root is born in the region of death, a sea of endings shall occur. The wise man who considers all these applications [will succeed].

### The Overcoming Diagram 
<note>original: "Atikrata-cakram," a diagram for surpassing obstacles or enemies</note>

The area is fifty **finger-widths** <note>original: "angula"</note>. At the base of the petals is the mace. There are sixteen petals. The measurement is a single grain. The hearing of the vessel's sound is the internal mechanism of the instrument.

Three semi-circles <note>original: *ardhavalayatrayam*</note> should be placed on the petals. Or, a diagram <note>original: *yantra*</note> with six points or three circles should be contemplated on the two staffs and the fist-handles <note>the portion of the weapon gripped by the hand</note>. One should purify it and then place a pot <note>a decorative finial or *kalasa*</note> on the eight-part staff. Within the circles of the enemy, the soldiers are crushed in the heart. On the auspicious handle, one should measure a pot of eight finger-breadths <note>original: *angula*; a traditional unit of measurement roughly equal to 1.75 cm</note>. The iron rod should be sixty finger-breadths in height.

The joints and the "ears" <note>projections on the weapon</note> are determined by the birth star <note>original: *janmarksha*; the constellation the moon was in at the time of the owner's birth</note>. In those circles, place five and five units. In the middle of the upper pot, the arrangement of the circles is auspicious. Thus, for the iron rod, there should be six petals of four finger-breadths. In the triangle of the half-petal, place two. At the highest point of the "eldest" <note>the primary point of the weapon</note>, there is one pot. The seal <note>original: *mudra*</note> is placed on the petals. In the triangle of the "pleasure of women," the Master <note>the deity or owner</note> is with the pot; five seals are desired. In the staff, the enemy is situated within the heart of the petal.

**The Thunderbolt** <note>original: *vajram*</note> is six finger-breadths in height and circumference. The two points are curved and six finger-breadths long. The staff... in the weapon known as "Control" <note>original: *vasha*</note>, place three "fire-bearers" <note>likely a specific mark or etched symbol</note>. In the middle, there are five. Seven houses <note>divisions of the diagram</note> are auspicious. On the polished staff, there are three. What is given by the enemy in the middle... situated there. For the plow-weapon, the staff height is eight cubits <note>original: *hasta*; the length from the elbow to the tip of the middle finger</note>. What is given is six finger-breadths. The two points are curved. It is said to be five-faced. The staff is eight finger-breadths; the petal-point is six finger-breadths. In the middle, there are three in the staff and two in the fist-grip.

At the root of the middle is the portion for the **Pushya** constellation. One should place the decoration at the root of the sides. In the middle of the three, starting from the "End," there are three. At the middle root, the birth star is bathed. These should be made every seven days for beauty and the desire for victory. One should consider the auspicious and inauspicious signs there. The manufacture of weapons should be done by skilled craftsmen on **Saturdays, Tuesdays, or Sundays**, especially during the "half-junction" <note>original: *ardhayoga*; a specific astronomical alignment</note>. Beholding the King, bearing weapons <note>original: *shastradharana*</note>, and gathering attendants are auspicious in the golden circle.

<meta>This page continues the technical specifications for military equipment, transitioning from the construction of the iron staff to the specific dimensions and colors of banners (flags) for different divisions of the army. It then details various complex battle formations (vyūhas) and how they counter one another on the field.</meta>

<meta>Watermark: © Dharmartha Trust J&K. Digitized by eGangotri</meta>

A triangular banner <note>original: *trikoṇā*</note> for the army should be nine cubits <note>original: *hasta*; a unit of measurement from the elbow to the tip of the middle finger, approx. 18 inches</note> in length. The banner for an elephant <note>original: *gaja*</note> should be eleven cubits. For a chariot <note>original: *ratha*</note>, it is seven cubits. For the infantry <note>original: *padāti*</note>, the banner is five cubits. A banner of sixty units <note>perhaps referring to a supreme royal banner</note> is a standard that is honored even by the gods <note>original: *tridaśairapi*</note>.

The colors of the banners should follow this sequence: red, white, and black. A white banner is intended to bring rain <note>or general prosperity</note>. Blue and variegated <note>original: *citra*</note> colors are also used. The first three colors mentioned are considered the most auspicious. In the directions of the West <note>governed by Varuna</note> and the Southeast <note>governed by Agni</note>, a single "head" or finial is desired. 

There are five or six banner-holders <note>original: *dhāraketava*</note> for the cloth. In the "womb" or center of the flag <note>original: *dhvajagarbha*</note>, three marks should be placed. Among the six types, the last is the most significant. 

### Planetary Associations with Banners
The choice of color and fabric is determined by the presiding planets:
*   **Red** for the Sun <note>original: *Sūrya*</note>.
*   **White** for Mars <note>original: *Bhauma*; though Mars is usually red, the text specifies white/clear here</note>.
*   **Yellow** for Jupiter <note>original: *Jīva*</note>.
*   **Best White** for Venus <note>original: *Sita*</note>.
*   **Silk and Linen** <note>original: *kośeya kṣauma*</note> for the variegated banners of the Son of Earth <note>Mars</note>.
*   **Black/Sesame-colored** <note>original: *tila*</note> for Saturn <note>original: *Manda*</note>.

The lord of the banner's cloth should be placed according to the astrological house. If the banner remains in the remaining directions, it is auspicious for those who wander in righteousness. It is said that these banners can grant victory even to the sinful, but if they are displayed in reverse, they bring disease and misfortune. Mixed colors produce mixed results. 

If the banner staff <note>original: *yaṣṭi*</note> of the Master of Sound <note>perhaps a specific official or deity</note> or of Venus is damaged, it destroys the kingdom. Fruitless staffs, broken banners, crooked staffs, or damaged fly-whisks <note>original: *cāmara*</note> are to be avoided as described in previous years.

### Military Formations (Vyūhas)
In the labor of strength, one must employ battle formations <note>original: *vyūha*</note>. 
*   **The Crane Formation** <note>original: *valākā-vyūha*</note> is created for the purpose of war.
*   **The Crow Formation** <note>original: *kāka-vyūha*</note> is the destroyer <note>original: *bhañjaka*</note> of the Crane formation.

One must certainly divide the army into these two types of formations. In the line of battle, chariot must face chariot. Five to seven elephants should be stationed. Thirty cubits and a <unclear>...</unclear> hundred should be on the flanks. Just as a gem is placed in a setting, the King remains in the middle. 

With thirty cubits on the flanks and two elephants, followed by the rest of the troops, this is called the **Total Army Formation**. When two are in the front and the rear is white, it is called the **Heron Formation** <note>original: *krauñca-vyūha*</note>. 

The Crane is broken by the Heron. In this Heron formation:
*   Two chariots are in the front.
*   Seven elephants are positioned behind them.
*   Behind those are twenty pillars <note>or specialized units</note> and five hundred horse-riders.
*   Seven chariots are on each side, with two elephants stationed there.

This arrangement resembles an altar <note>original: *vedi*</note>. In the center are the infantry, and on the flanks are the horses <note>original: *turaṅgama*</note>. This is known as the **Wagon Formation** <note>original: *śakaṭa-vyūha*</note>; it is said to be unbreakable even by the gods. In the front are three chariots, arranged in the shape of an elephant... five chariots each... sixty bows...

...at the rear of the elephants, the entire army is positioned for the **Lion Formation** <note>original: *simha vyūha*; a formation designed to mimic the strength and frontal assault of a lion</note>. The **Wagon Formation** <note>original: *śakaṭa vyūha*</note> is used at certain times, but it can be broken by the **Sati Formation**. The **Lotus Formation** <note>original: *padmavyūha*</note> is countered by the Lion, and the **Needle Formation** <note>original: *sūcikā*</note> can also pierce it. In the center of the elephants and horses, one should arrange a "deadly shape" or a sacrificial altar shape <note>original: *vedyā*</note> with chariots and banner-bearers. Behind them, place the archers and those bearing swords and shields. The army should be led to encircle the enemy in a fixed order. This should be done repeatedly until the enemy host is routed.

### The Wheel and Lotus Formations

The **Wheel Formation** <note>original: *cakravyūha*; a legendary circular formation famously mentioned in the Mahabharata</note> is known to be impenetrable, even by the gods. In this arrangement, one must place a single chariot in each of the eight primary directions. Between them, nine elephants should be stationed. Then, the infantry should be positioned in ten "petals" or outer sections. In the very center, place seven chariots and thirteen elephants. There should be nineteen [units] and twenty-eight infantry. 

When the center is filled with elephants and chariots, it is called the "pericarp" or seed-head <note>original: *karṇikā*</note> of the lotus. The King <note>or Commander</note> should be mounted upon an elephant in that center. In the intervals, place "troughs" or columns <note>original: *droṇikā*</note> consisting of three chariots, three horses, and three of everything else, along with twenty-three infantrymen. This is known as the **Lotus Formation**, because it is constructed in the shape of a lotus. 

### The Garland and Sun Formations

When chariots, elephants, and horses are arranged in a continuous string or row, it is called the **Garland Formation** <note>original: *mālā vyūha*</note>. 

In another variation, place two chariots in each of the four directions. Behind them, place ten elephants, twenty-four horses, and twenty sword-bearers. Archers and those carrying brilliant weapons follow. Behind them are the banner-bearers and those guarding the "engines" <note>siege engines or mechanical weapons</note> at the gates. This arrangement of elephants, chariots, and infantry is known as the **Vyuha Prakarini** <note>The Arranger Formation</note>.

When the army is filled with many units and moves in a serpentine or "snake-like" manner <note>original: *sarpākāreṇa*</note>, it is known as the **Sun Formation** <note>original: *sūryavyūha*</note>, which is highly celebrated in the science of warfare. It is seven-fold, consisting of seven chariots, elephants, horses, and infantry. With three chariots and others arranged in groups of seven in sequence, one below the other, it is called the **Matra Formation**. This is the most powerful of all formations, acting like a consumer of all things, similar to fire. 

The army should be arranged in a knowledgeable shape <note>or "Vidhya" shape</note>, with the King standing in the middle. The junction or union of these parts is known as the **Valvaka Formation**, which stands as a firm circular defense.

The **Eagle Formation** <note>original: *Soparṇa*; a formation shaped like the mythical bird Garuda</note> should be executed according to its own specific qualities. Next is the **Serpent Cycle** <note>original: *Sarpiṇī-cakra*</note>, which determines the strength and movement of the host. The **Staff Formation** <note>original: *Daṇḍaka*</note> must be shaped like a straight staff, while the **Crocodile Formation** <note>original: *Makara*</note> takes the form of a crocodile <note>a wedge-shaped formation with a narrow front and wide center</note>.

These formations are equal in importance. In the place of Mercury <note>original: *Budha*</note>, one should know the Staff; in the place of Jupiter <note>original: *Guru*</note>, the Staff or the Serpent. In times of crisis or narrow passages, the **Circular Formation** <note>original: *Vṛtta*</note> is considered best by the wise. A formation should be chosen for battle only when the planets are favorable. If the planets are unfavorable, one must employ a "Heavy" <note>original: *Guru*; meaning dense or highly fortified</note> formation to break through the enemy's lines.

### Thus Ends the Arrangement of Formations

The complete cycle of formations, including the Serpent and others, should be measured in units of twenty-four hands <note>original: *hastatrayastayam*; a 'hasta' or cubit is the length from the elbow to the fingertip</note>. Following the daily movement of the constellations, one should observe the "Half-Day Circle." Upon entering or exiting the formation, three stars should be passed clockwise. Eight moons should be in the center, with the Sun positioned continuously.

Through this day and night continuity, the "Staff" is measured. On the day of one's birth star <note>original: *janma-bha*; the nakshatra or lunar mansion under which the commander was born</note>, the results of the formation will always be successful.

### Thus Ends the Formation Cycle

At the base of the **Standard** <note>original: *Nisāna*; refers to the military drums or royal standards carried into battle</note>, the measurement is one and a half hands. The height should be three hands. Four stones are placed at the peg...

Regarding the **Drum of the Camel** <note>original: *Uṣṭrasya vāda*; refers to the kettle-drums mounted on war camels</note>: the yoke is positioned separately. On the camel's head and shoulders, place two pegs. On the curves of the instrument, five and five. On the horses or other mounts, they are placed one by one. At the base, there should be eight "feet" and four at the center.

The musician should place his birth star within the "Heart-Position" of the cycle; this grants great success. If the star is positioned in the "Mouth" of the instrument, it results in the paralysis or destruction of the enemy. However, if a malefic planet <note>original: *pāpagrahā*</note> resides at the peg of the instrument during the playing, it is ill-omened. If the star is positioned at the camel's foot, it signifies a "break" or defeat; if at the base, it brings death to the musician. Malefic influences on the head or throat are considered highly inauspicious.

### Thus Ends the Standard Cycle

For the **Elephant** <note>original: *Gaja*</note>, the standard is three hands long and one and a half hands thick. On the two tusks, two marks; on the head and shoulders, the various rites are performed. On the back, two; and on the feet, five marks. Thus it is known by the wise.

<meta>This page continues the technical manual on military strategy and divination. <margin>
Sha-Vi- <note>Abbreviation for *Śatru-Vināśana*, meaning "Destruction of Enemies"</note>
36
</margin>

If three malefic influences <note>original: *pāpa*; technically referring to "cruel" planets like Mars or Saturn</note> are situated in the **Drum Mouth** <note>original: *Bherī-vaktra*; the striking surface of the war drum</note>, it signifies the destruction of the enemy’s own diseases and strengths. If placed on the elephant’s shoulder or its trunk, it brings about the **Slaying of Elephants** <note>original: *Gaja-māraṇā*</note> and grants beauty and success. For those stationed on the human form of the diagram, it provides invincibility. In the **Drum Cycle** <note>original: *Bherī-cakra*</note>, one should worship the deity through the work of war; this ensures that no wealth is lost. 

Next, regarding the **Camel Cycle** <note>original: *Gandharā-cakra*</note>: place two units on the head, ten on the face, five on the feet, and two on the tail. If a malefic influence is situated on the head of the **Camel** <note>original: *Gandharā*; likely referring to a camel-shaped diagram used for divination</note>, it is said to cause one’s own destruction. However, a malefic influence situated on the body of the Camel results in the complete destruction of the enemy’s host. 

### The Camel Cycle

The **Camel** should be visualized as being enveloped in iron armor. In this auspicious cycle, the placement is as follows: 
- One on the head
- Two on each of the eyes
- Ten on the face
- Five on the feet
- Four on the tail 

Within this **Twin Cycle** <note>original: *Yamala-cakra*; a diagram involving paired elements</note>, if malefic influences are at the mouth, one should expect conflict. If benefic influences <note>original: *saumya*; "gentle" planets like Jupiter or Venus</note> are present, there is enjoyment. If malefics are at the feet of the musician/striker, or at the head, they grant the musician success. This is the work of the **Lord of Twins**. 

If the Sun <note>original: *Ravi*</note> is situated at the mouth, it indicates a friend. In the **Madanikā Cycle** <note>term: *Madanikā* | A specific divinatory diagram, literally "The Intoxicating One"</note>, a malefic situated at the top provides victory. If a malefic is at the mouth or the two eyes, it grants great power. The Son of the Earth <note>Mars</note> is unfavorable if in the middle. 

Two units are placed on the hands, eight on the body, and one on the tail. Four are in the belly, and one above the throat. One each on the throat and head. Two on the shoulders, and eight on the feet. In this **Madanikā Cycle**, a malefic influence grants victory. However, it causes a "breaking" or faltering of the pace for the mount or the rider. 

The tip of the nose, the elephant’s shoulder, and the tail—if benefic influences are situated here, it leads to wealth. In the **Warrior’s Instrument** <note>original: *Vīra-vādya*</note> cycle, the **Camel** and others bring victory when correctly aligned. One should place two units on the head, two on the thighs, and two on the face of the instrument. If three malefics are at the throat or the head, or two on the hands, it leads to a great victory. If one is on the feet, and malefics are stationed on the **Warrior’s Instrument**...

<summary>This page details several divinatory diagrams (Chakras) shaped like war drums, camels, and "intoxicating" figures, used to predict military outcomes based on the placement of malefic and benefic planets on specific body parts of the diagram.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for military divination. Following the discussion on the Camel Cycle, the text shifts to the "Victory-Impulse" (Jayacheta) and various "Cycles" (Chakras) modeled after musical instruments used in war. It focuses on the placement of "cruel" (malefic) and "gentle" (benefic) planetary influences upon these diagrams to predict the outcome of battle and the safety of the king's forces.</meta>

If the **Victory-Impulse** <note>original: *Jayacheta*; a technical term in war-divination referring to the metaphysical "momentum" or spirit of victory</note> is situated in an inauspicious position, it portends the victory of the enemy over one’s own army. If it is situated in the opposite manner during an equal battle, it acts as a consumer of the enemy, like a parching wind. When seven "cruel" influences reach the Victory-Impulse, the carrier of the king is affected. When the Great Warrior <note>referring to the King or the commander</note> is situated in the Wind-element, and gentle or cruel planets are placed there, one should recognize the breaking of the vanguard of the attacking forces.

**Thus ends the section on the Warrior’s Instruments.** 

Regarding the **Large Drum** <note>original: *Pataha*; a traditional barrel-shaped war drum</note>, its foundation and its rhythmic "din-din" sound should be understood to yield the same fruits as the Warrior’s Instruments. For the Large Drum, the **Small Drum** <note>original: *Dindima*</note>, and the **Double-Headed Drum** <note>original: *Mardala*</note>, the results are as previously stated. One must apply the Victory-Impulse to these instruments of war. Just as in the previous nine-fold division, the Victory-Impulse should be properly arranged. 

In the four-fold arrangement, if one sees marks of ash or lines on the banners and cloth that emit a sound of their own accord, it indicates the presence of spirits. These include **Vampiric Spirits** <note>original: *Vetala*</note>, **Ghosts** <note>original: *Bhuta*</note>, **Departed Souls** <note>original: *Preta*</note>, **Flesh-Eating Demons** <note>original: *Pishacha*</note>, and **Gourd-Shaped Spirits** <note>original: *Kushmanda*</note>. 

Where the <term>Pellet-Drum</term> <note>original: *Damaru*; a small two-headed drum often associated with Lord Shiva, used here as a mystical diagram</note> is situated, one should use red sandalwood and the "life-breath" of the instrument, which is its skin. It should be placed toward the North. In the **Fire Circle** <note>original: *Agneya-mandala*</note>, it should be enveloped by the Wind-seed syllable, or it may be enveloped by the Earth-seed syllable during focused meditation. If the deity—identified by names such as Devadatta—has "eaten" or "is eating" <note>likely referring to ritual offerings made to the diagram</note>, one should perform the **Vajapeya** <note>a high-ranking Vedic sacrifice, here adapted for military success</note> ritual for the protection of one’s own army. This mystical diagram is a breaker of the enemy's army; it destroys the enemy’s waves and horses and manifests light for the awakening of the many worlds. 

### The Pellet-Drum Cycle

This diagram is used to interpret the movement of "cruel" weapons and winds. In the <term>Damaru Cycle</term>:
- Three units on the head
- Three on the face
- Ten on the hands
- Four on the feet

If a malefic influence is situated in these positions, it causes the breaking of limbs and the shattering of weapons. 

**Thus ends the Corpse Cycle.** <note>original: *Shauva-cakram*; likely a diagram shaped like a body or associated with Shiva in his fierce form</note>

[The placement for this cycle follows:] Five and four in the center, nine on the head, and on the arms and shoulders. If malefic planets cause a "breach" here, there is fear from the fire of the enemy. However, if they are situated on the feet or the assembly of the body, then it is a giver of total success.

**Thus ends the Lute Cycle.** <note>original: *Vivana-cakram*; a diagram shaped like the Vina, a stringed instrument</note>

[The text concludes with a transition back to the classification of drums:] The Small Drum, the Great Kettle-drum, the Lute-drum, the Large Drum, and the Double-Headed Drum...

Multiply this by fifty-five (55)... 

<note>The following section contains dense technical calculations for astrological timing (Muhurta).</note>

The results are obtained from the **Nadis** <note>a unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note>. For the excellent blessing, the calculation involves twenty-six (26) **Angulas** <note>widths of a thumb</note>. Then, from the navel of the lord, the values of the **Vedas** <note>the number 4</note> and the **Anila** <note>the number 49 or wind-values</note> are subtracted. This gives the "body-total" of twenty-five (25). By adding the **Sangha** <note>the number 6</note> and the **Jinas** <note>the number 24</note>, and then subtracting the **Saptas** <note>the number 7</note> and the **Vedas** <note>the number 4</note>, the measurement is fixed. This should be calculated month by month. 

On the day of the **Vara** <note>weekday</note>, in the twenty-second (22) **Ghati** <note>a unit of 24 minutes</note>, the total should be two (2). When the sum of the **Tithi** <note>lunar day</note> and the **Vara** <note>weekday</note> equals seven (7), the "Cloud-Day" occurs here. Subtracting the **Manus** <note>the number 14</note> from the total of the **Tithi**, the remainder determines the "foot" of the calculation. In the case of wealth, if there is an odd number, it is called a "Continuous Foot," otherwise, it is known as the "Moon-Foot."

When the **Tithi** is in the second, fifth, or twelfth position, or in the tenth or third, one should apply the "Veda-calculation." In the ninth, fifth, or sixth signs, such as **Simha** <note>Leo</note>, or in the eighth sign, the power is diminished. In the seventh sign, the **Vara** <note>weekday</note> is not considered. 

The **Nadis** <note>time units</note> of the Sun are explained when the Sun enters the "Throat of the **Makara**" <note>the sign of Capricorn</note>. The remainder of the explanation follows: in the twelfth part, or the seventh part, or in the sign of **Mesha** <note>Aries</note>, the calculation is joined with the "Sky-Pair" <note>the number 20</note>. When the Sun is in **Karka** <note>Cancer</note>, the calculation is slow. In **Simha** <note>Leo</note>, there are three or six units; in the seventh sign, they are elevated. These are also proclaimed for **Kanya** <note>Virgo</note> and **Meena** <note>Pisces</note>. In **Dhanu** <note>Sagittarius</note>, the calculation is always performed for the sake of the color of the theater/battleground. When the auspicious part of the **Tithi** is not mentioned, the calculation of the **Tithi-bhukti** <note>the portion of the lunar day already passed</note> is used. This is combined with the **Nadis** of the Sun to determine the outcome...

If the duration is less than a **Nadika** <note>a unit of time equivalent to 24 minutes; 60 Nadikas make a full day</note>, then it becomes a given **Yoga** <note>the 'union,' one of the five limbs of the almanac representing the combined longitudinal motion of the Sun and Moon</note>. Half of a **Tithi** <note>a lunar day</note> is declared to be a **Karana** <note>the 'action,' another limb of the almanac</note> in these matters. Starting with Aries for Mars, whether in the preceding or following positions, these are fraught with difficulty. Multiply by four <note>original: *Veda*; the number four, representing the four Vedas</note>, then add or subtract units of time <term>Ghatis</term> by threes. The **Tithi** is determined by the motion of the Sun <note>original: *Ravi-bhukti*</note> combined with the **Nadikas** of the previous lunar mansion. Add sixty to the fixed star, then determine the next **Nadika** from the Sun’s position.

From the Sun's motion, one should calculate the Moon's motion <note>original: *Chandra-bhukti*</note> and the duration of the **Nadikas**. When sixteen units are reached, the result is known. The duration of the **Yoga** and half a **Tithi** constitute the **Karana**. The motion of the Sun and Moon combined is defined as the **Yoga**. When multiplied by two and adjusted, it results in the active **Karana** associated with that **Tithi**.

In the calculation of the "Five Limbs" <term>Panchanga-sadhana</term>, one should apply the rules in the latter and former halves of the day. Following the prescribed order for the Sun and Moon, the **Yogas** are measured. In the calculation of months, use the "Moon-Goad" <note>a symbolic numerical system where 'Moon' signifies 1 and 'Goad' signifies certain factors</note> method. By this establishment, the **Chart of Coming and Going** <term>Yat-ayat Chakra</term> is formed, combined with the birth-lunar-mansion <term>Tithi-bhava</term>. When the Sun transits <term>Sankramana</term> in a given year, the calculation of the hours <term>Ghatikas</term> and minutes <term>Vighatikas</term> should be adjusted by the factors of the earth and the moon... 2...

This is the method for determining the day of the week <term>Vara</term> and other elements in the **Panchanga**, following the sequence of the elapsed units of time. Using the numbers twenty-eight and thirty, the **Tithi** is divided into six parts, and the results are recorded according to one's own knowledge. Thus, one should consider the timing in the **Panchanga** for all types of work. By this general standard, one can determine if a day will result in auspicious or inauspicious outcomes. All tasks should always be performed according to these precise rules.

### The Calculation of the Journey

Here ends the calculation of the **Panchanga** for a specific moment. Now the **Tithi**, the day of the week, and the elapsed **Nakshatra** <note>lunar mansion</note> are discussed. Divide the total by seven; by the remainder, one should plan the **Journey** <term>Yatra</term> <note>refers here specifically to a military expedition or an important march</note>.

1. If the remainder is **One**: The journey will be beautiful and auspicious.
2. If the remainder is **Two**: The journey will bring wealth and profit.
3. If the remainder is **Three**: There will be a loss of wealth or a failed mission.
4. If the remainder is **Four**: The journey will result in supreme success.
5. If the remainder is **Five**: There will be obstacles and hindrances on the path.
6. If the remainder is **Six**: Death is indicated.

<note>The final lines return to technical planetary alignments.</note>
This is for the purification of the "All-Facing" grid <term>Sarvatomukhi</term>. Take three from the Sun, excluding the Moon; when the Sun and Moon are equal, or when in the fierce cycles of the Sun...

# The Calculation of Planetary Hours and Time Divisions

The Sun <note>original: *Surya*</note> is determined through a sequence of three, starting from the released positions of the liberation. The latter half is governed by the Sun’s lunar day <note>original: *Tithibhanu*</note>. In the divisions known as the Moon and the Sun <note>original: *Chandra-arka*</note>, the position is declared first in the sequence of the days of the week. In the half-positions, when the planets strike one another, the Sun is directed at the lotus-like feet <note>a poetic reference to a specific point in a diagram or calculation</note>. This is calculated on the days of **Victory and Triumph** <note>original: *Jaya-Vijaya*; these are specific names for the 3rd, 8th, and 13th lunar days, considered favorable for success</note>, during the gathering of the military arrays <note>original: *vyuha*</note>. On the days of arrival and departure, the resulting calculation is produced. Divide the desired units of time <term>Nadikas</term> by the parts of the Sun. When the resulting sum is combined, it becomes a complete division.

One <term>Ghati</term> <note>a unit of 24 minutes</note> remaining to be experienced is multiplied by the local rising time of the sign <term>Lagna</term> and divided by the light-points. The results of the time to be experienced are thus obtained. These are experienced in the sequence of their respective risings. In the calculation of the shadows <note>a method for telling time</note>, the corrections <note>original: *chara*</note> are considered. From the cooling rays <note>the Moon</note> and the Sun, the calculation is reversed. Four <note>original: *Tamas*; also referring to Rahu</note> is the part to be experienced. 

The duration of the ascendant <term>Lagna-mana</term> is divided by the "Knowledge-Vedas" <note>the number 4</note>. For the North Node <note>original: *Rahu*</note>, the motion is retrograde <note>original: *vamaga*</note>. In determining the duration of the ascendant, one calculates from the ocean-divisions <note>original: *sagara*; the number 4</note>. In the circle of planetary piercing <term>Vedha-chakra</term>, when the lord of the sign is pierced in the planetary hour <term>Hora</term>, one should abandon even that which was prepared with great energy. In the "piercings of war," the sword, the water-carrier, the arrow, and the <term>Nadika</term> <note>time-measure</note> are traditionally remembered. The sequence begins from Aries <note>original: *Meshadi*</note> and moves to Libra <note>original: *Tuladi*</note> in the calculation of wealth.

### The Nature of the Planetary Hours

In this system, the **Planetary Hour** <term>Hora</term> can be extremely cruel. If a cruel planet occupies the **Hora**, the result will be cruel. The number of **Horas** is proclaimed to be twenty-four, spanning the day and night. The duration of the movement of an ascendant-hour <note>original: *Lagna-hora*</note> is two <term>Ghatikas</term> and twenty <term>Palas</term> <note>Approximately 56 minutes; usually a Hora is 2.5 Ghatis (60 mins), suggesting a specific local or systemic adjustment here</note>. 

The first **Hora** is defined as belonging to the Lord of the day <note>e.g., the Sun for Sunday</note>. The others follow in the sequence of the six-fold planetary relationships. These **Horas** are known as:
1. The Sun <note>original: *Aditya*</note>
2. Venus <note>original: *Shukra*</note>
3. The Moon <note>original: *Soma*</note>
4. Mercury <note>original: *Indra/Budha*</note>
5. Saturn <note>original: *Sauri*</note>
6. Jupiter <note>original: *Vagisha*</note>
7. Mars <note>original: *Bhumaja*</note>

These are proclaimed to be six-fold in their count. The specific names for the divisions of time are: 
**Obstruction** <note>Rodha</note>, **Gentle** <note>Saumya</note>, **Terrible** <note>Karala</note>, **Steady** <note>Sthira</note>, **Swift** <note>Vega</note>, **Extensive** <note>Vishalika</note>, **Death-bringer** <note>Mrityumari</note>, **Auspicious** <note>Shubha</note>, **Cruel** <note>Krura</note>, **The Fierce Outcaste** <note>Chandali</note>, and **The Cutter** <note>Kruttika</note>. 

Then follow:
**The Night of Time** <note>Kalaratri</note>, **The Obstructor** <note>Rodhani</note>, **Confusion** <note>Vibhrama</note>, **The Lunar** <note>Shashini</note>, **The Trident-bearer** <note>Shulini</note>, **The Great Illusion** <note>Mahamaya</note>, **The Intoxicating** <note>Madani</note>, **The Life-stealer** <note>Jivaharini</note>, and **The Withering** <note>Soshani</note>...

<summary>This page details the mathematical calculation of the 24 Horas (planetary hours) and their specific names, ranging from auspicious to "cruel." It provides the technical basis for military divination, determining whether an hour is favorable for battle or should be "abandoned."</summary>

...The renowned and celebrated divisions burn with power. These four divisions, known as the lords of the directions, provide incomparable results. When the face is turned upwards and the hair is disheveled at the moment of the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term>, it signifies the destruction of troubles and the wearing away of the bonds of suffering. This "Turning of the Wheel" <note>original: *Chakraruti*</note> acts as a horizontal face for the one who is departing. For an army, this creates a state of confusion; however, it brings success through great effort if the goal is far away. All efforts will bear fruit if the lord of the house is not occupied by a **malefic planet** <note>original: *papagraha*; referring to planets like Mars or Saturn which were thought to cause hardship</note>.

Thus ends the section on the **Twenty-Four Horas** <term>Chaturvimshati Hora</term>. 

Now, one must understand the current positions of the **Moon and the Sun** <note>original: *Chandra-Suryau*</note> in the world. In the cycle known as the "Awe-Inspiring," one should begin the battle under the heat of the sun. Calculate the sum of the Moon’s duration and the remaining units of time <term>Ghatikas</term>. From this, the portion of the constellations to be experienced is divided. Then, in the second stage of valor, the signs of victory appear. 

In this context, the following omens should be observed regarding the stream of urine: If the Sun is red and the Moon is dark, and a stream of urine is released, look for these signs. If the urine is **red** <note>original: *rakta*; likely indicating blood</note>, there will be a loss of the objective. If the urine is **white** <note>original: *shveta*</note>, the work will be accomplished. Success is not achieved if the stream is excessively thick. However, if it flows steadily like a stream of oil, success in the task is granted. If only half is released, then only half the profit is gained, and the rest of the task will be destroyed.

If, at the start of a journey, **urine** is seen first, it is a sign previously observed by the wise. If **urine and dung** <note>original: *mutra-vishtha*</note> fall upon the ground simultaneously, it portends **death** <note>original: *mriti*</note>. One must consider these signs carefully for sickness, victory in war, or profit in all undertakings. If the stream follows the path of the wind, it indicates a pillar of strength. 

The following sequences of omens are critical:
*   If **urine** is released first, followed by **dung**: This is auspicious for war and other actions.
*   If **dung** is released first, followed by **urine**: This signifies **defeat in war** <note>original: *yuddhe bhango*</note> and fear during the journey.
*   If **urine** is followed by **dung**, and then **urine** again: Victory will be achieved, but only with great difficulty, and there will be small profits in trade.
*   If **dung** is followed by **urine**, then **dung**, and then **urine** repeatedly: This foretells total defeat in battle, great loss, and death or chronic illness for the merchant.

Victory is proclaimed when the urine is of excellent quality and cool. If it is hot and released from a high position, it indicates a fall or a gathering of enemies after a long time. In cases of war or disease, these signs indicate whether there will be gain or an auspicious end.

<margin>Ra-<br/>Vi-</margin> <note>Abbreviation for the *Rajavijaya* text</note>
Just as in the *King’s Victory* <note>original: *Rajavijaya*</note>, the text titled **The War Elephant** <term>Ranahasti</term> describes the protection of the path. Now, I shall explain the realms in brief. In all four types of battle, the one who possesses this victorious **Wheel** <term>Chakra</term> shall be the winner.

The wheel has **twelve petals** <note>A geometric diagram used for mapping time and direction</note>. Wherever the Sun is positioned, it consumes everything in a period of two and a half **Ghatikas** <term>Ghatika</term> <note>A unit of time equal to 24 minutes; 2.5 Ghatikas equals one hour</note>. This is known as the **Great Pestilence** <term>Mahamari</term>. At that time, the land should not be observed. If used correctly in battle, it is said to grant victory, provided one understands the laughing sign <note>original: *hasula lakshana*; a specific auspicious mark or omen</note>.

Thus ends the section on the **Great Pestilence** <term>Mahamari</term>.

This force is named "Left-Handed" in the East. It moves through the four directions and the four intermediate quarters in a clockwise direction every month. For an army of four divisions <note>Cavalry, infantry, elephants, and chariots</note>, it grants victory if it stays in the "insect" position <note>likely referring to a specific astrological subdivision</note>. This is the **Guardian of the Territory** <term>Kshetrapala</term> in the South, the best strength of the land. When this land-strength is present, others are defeated. Without this strength, all efforts are surely in vain.

Thus ends the section on the **Guardian of the Territory** <term>Kshetrapala</term>.

For the destruction of demons, Rudra <note>The fierce form of the god Shiva</note> contemplated this in his heart. I shall now describe the ritual of "Victory" for the worship of the Victorious One. He has ten arms and holds a staff; he has three eyes and a beautiful lip. He is seated upon a **Great Corpse** <term>Mahapreta</term> and holds a firm, sharp weapon. 

The directions are governed by:
*   **Indra** (East)
*   **Vayu** (Northwest)
*   **Yama** (South)
*   **Rudra** (Northeast)
*   **Varuna** (West)
*   **Agni** (Southeast)
*   **Ishwara** (North)

The months begin with Chaitra and follow in sequence. This is calculated by the day, the half-day, the watch <note>original: *yama*; a 3-hour period</note>, and the half-watch. According to this measure, the sacrifice is given.

Thus ends the section on the **Ground of Rudra** <term>Rudrabhumi</term>.

In a wheel marked with **seven lines** <term>Saptarekha</term>, the influence of Rudra is balanced. Twenty-eight positions <note>referring to the 28 Nakshatras or lunar mansions</note> should be served in a clockwise direction. The destruction of the day occurs through the passage of time. In this line, the "consumption" <note>astrological influence</note> lasts for two and a quarter **Ghatikas**. At the end of the month, the Moon is primary; on a bad day, the Sun is primary. Two units of time, two hours—the Sun is always present there.

When the desired sacrifice is offered, and one moves by the left-front path, there is no doubt that victory shall be obtained, even when the flesh of the fallen is scattered in battle. This is linked to the **Wheel of the Fire of Time for the Low-Born** <note>original: *Shudrakalanala-chakra*; a specific astrological diagram used for predicting the fate of common soldiers or specific castes</note>; whatever results are seen in this wheel should be understood as manifesting on the earth.

In the south, she moves through the entire wheel during the day and night <note>original: *ahoratra*</note>. Regarding the doubt of the **Mistress of Yoga**, she is gentle toward you. Based on the measure of the time-units <term>Ghatika</term> from the time of the Fierce Lord <note>original: *Rudra*</note>, the rotation occurs through the half-day sequentially. Whether in a single hour <term>Muhurta</term> <note>A unit of roughly 48 minutes</note> or in the unseen times, the goddess **Chandi** <note>A fierce, warrior form of the Divine Mother</note> is always rotating. For the one whom she lifts to the right, no enjoyment <note>or "consumption," meaning life or success</note> remains. Thus ends the section on **Chandika**.

From the **Southeast** <note>original: *Agneya*</note> corner to the left, following the lunar days <term>Tithi</term> from the full moon, the period of **Bhadra** <note>A specific astrological period considered inauspicious for new beginnings but powerful for certain actions</note> lasts three and a half days. In the **Northwest** <note>original: *Vayavya*</note>, it lasts five and a half days. When she is facing forward or moving to the left, she brings victory in travel and likewise in war. For those desiring a favorable entry for planets like Jupiter <note>original: *Pushya*, referring to the lunar mansion or the planet Brihaspati</note>, this is the desired path.

Thus ends the section on **Bhadra**.

Furthermore, when the strength of the time is held within a portion of the earth, it quickly becomes a destroyer of the three worlds. For those whose bodies are stained with blood, the Moon has moved to the right. Fire moves to the north. All things held within the center face the eclipse, or the wind. He who stands in the front-left should be worshiped by all people. When positioned with the serpent <note>original: *Ahi*; likely referring to the lunar node Rahu</note>, the given desire grants victory. When in the form of **Life** <term>Jiva-rupa</term>, it remains steady. In the form of **Sin** <term>Papa-rupa</term>, it is always terrifying, yet the desire grants victory.

The position in the body or the vehicle is called "The Destruction." The movement of the Moon is indeed the movement within all living beings. When the Moon is auspicious and strong, the living beings are strong in their movements and play. Whatever inauspicious act one performs when the Moon is favorable, O Beloved, it is said to result in an inauspicious army and internal storms. If the Moon is positioned in the **Life Form** <term>Jiva-rupa</term> within the container, the enemies survive. When it moves to the left or the back, **Parvati** <note>The consort of Shiva, here representing the goddess's path</note> marks the way of the Moon.

<summary>This page describes various mystical "Wheels" (Chakras) and "Time-Lines" used to determine the favor of goddesses like Bhadrakali and Chandika in battle, emphasizing that the Moon's position determines whether a soldier acts in a "Life Form" or "Sin Form."</summary>
<keywords>Bhadrakali, Chandika, Kalarekha, Kalanala-chakra, Yogishvari, lunar astrology, military divination, Jiva-rupa, Papa-rupa, Bhadra</keywords>

Victory occurs when positioned toward the front-left; if one acts from the right, the result is never the same on this earth. If the alignment is toward the back, one should give way; if to the right, there is "water" <note>This may refer to a metaphorical fluidity or a specific ritual requirement</note>. However, a defeat <term>bhanga</term> <note>literally a "breaking" or rout</note> occurs at the front-left even for the gods and demons if the **Moon** <note>original: *Chandrama*</note> is not correctly placed. Victory is granted when the Moon is positioned at the front-left; otherwise, a defeat surely arises, O Fair-Faced One <note>A traditional poetic address to a goddess or female student</note>. When the **Sun** <note>original: *Arka*</note> is in the "lotus position" and the **Moon** <note>original: *Nishakara*, the "night-maker"</note> is in the "container" <note>original: *samputa*; a technical term for a specific layered arrangement of astrological data</note>, and the Sun is situated to the right, one should not station the army there. 

By relying on the **Sun** <note>original: *Bhanu*</note> at the back, it becomes a maker of victory. In the "container" arrangement, one should always triumph on the left; otherwise, it is the opposite. From the beginning of the movement, when the planets are situated at the front-left, victory is declared. If they are situated in a "broken" or unfavorable state, or if the **Moon** is behind the **Sun**, it creates victory; otherwise, it causes a defeat, even if acting from the right. When the "Snowy-Rayed One" <note>original: *Himagu*; the Moon</note> and the "Guru" <note>Jupiter</note> are in the hand <note>a specific calculation method</note>, and the Moon is in the right-side houses, one should avoid the enemy army after achieving victory. Those who are dear to the Lotus <note>The Sun and Moon</note> are the doers of this; when positioned to the right, victory is made if one is a devotee of **Janardana** <note>A name for Vishnu, the Preserver</note>. 419

By making this the "Tone of Dharma" <term>Dharmasvara</term>, and if the **Wind** <note>original: *Samira*; likely referring to the breath or the "Vital Air" (Prana)</note> moves on the left, one conquers the enemy host. If the movement is lacking or behind, and **Rahu** <note>The shadow planet or "Dragon's Head" associated with eclipses and chaos</note> and the **Moon** have gone to the right, while the **Sun** <note>original: *Bhaskara*</note> has moved to the left, and if the Sun and Moon are both powerful, then like a **Lion** attacking from the back, one conquers the enemy host. When the "Lord of Life" <note>Jupiter</note> is well-positioned, and both are at the right-back relative to **Rahu** in battle, one shall crush the enemy armies.

When the gentle planets are at the front-left and **Rahu** is situated on the right, I say that victory over the enemy is immediate. When **Rahu** has gone to the back and the gentle planets are well-situated there, victory over the enemy host arises—this is no other way, O Hero. If on a particular day cruel <note>malefic</note> planets are situated in the front, and **Rahu** is on the right, the army may be struck, but victory is sometimes possible. Malefic planets gone to the back are makers of victory over the enemy. **Rahu** situated in those same positions brings victory without loss. When the gentle planets are at the front-left and others are at the back, it causes the destruction of the enemies <note>original: *daitya*, literally "demons"</note>. When **Rahu** is at the back or above the head, it is not considered a "Rahu-battle"; rather, being at the back grants victory. When this calculation is joined with the strength of the armies, and the **Moon** and **Sun** are united in this alignment, victory over the enemy host arises.

In the **Great Yoga** <term>Mahayoga</term> <note>original: *Mahayoga*; refers here to a supreme system of astrological or tantric divination</note>, specifically regarding all this movement and travel, it is like a world-conqueror. This has not been explained by me to anyone else; it is revealed only here. (53) Likewise, this boon remains: it should not be given to just anyone. It must not be given to another’s disciple out of fear or a greed for wealth. It should be given to one of affectionate character, and not as a mere fee, O **Mountain Princess** <note>original: *Parvati*; Shiva is addressing his consort, the goddess Parvati</note>. Only when a long time has passed and a disciple is found to be long-devoted and humble, should one bestow the perfection of this knowledge after first testing them.

Thus, in times of distress: <insert>The Land of the Lord of Hosts</insert> <note>original: *Ganarajabhumi*</note>. This is the "Desire" calculation. From the month of **Chaitra** <note>the first month of the Hindu calendar, occurring in March-April</note> onwards, in the twelve-spoked wheel, where the "horn" is located, the sequence of the womb follows. It is said that being at the back gives defeat <term>bhanga</term>, while being situated at the left gives earth <note>stability</note>. It grants victory when positioned in the front; one stays firm in the middle of battle. This is the **Tigress** <note>original: *Sharduli*</note> position.

In the directions of Indra (East), the Fire (South-East), and the Ancestors (South-West)... reaching the Northwest... in the Southwest, the Wind (Northwest), and the Lunar (North), the Eleven-Roarers <note>original: *Rudra*; fierce forms of Shiva associated here with directions</note> preside over the two paths. If one moves in these steps, it gives victory when at the back or the right. The **Land of Victory over the Three Worlds** <note>original: *Trailokya Vijaya Bhumi*</note> is not perceived by the common world. This concludes the **Victory over the Three Worlds**.

The best positions are in the Eastern quarter; from there, the movement is slow for the servant. Whatever the time or month may be, seven by seven, there is happiness. When the **Noose of Time** <note>original: *Kala-pasha*</note> takes refuge in the left direction, it is best for travel and for battle, and is auspicious there. (63) This concludes the **Time of the Noose** <term>Pashakala</term>.

On the eight-spoked wheel, following the left-hand path... by the movement of the sixth path, it occurs in the southern half at sunrise. The warrior is delighted by this category... when done from the back... the **Land of the Fire of Time** <note>original: *Kalanali bhumi*</note> brings results through the "face of the pair." This is the **Vargi Land**.

In the **Wheel of Rahu and the Fire of Time** <term>Rahu-Kalanala Chakra</term> <note>a complex divinatory chart using the position of the shadow planet Rahu, the 'swallower' of the sun/moon</note>, regarding the **Moon** <note>original: *Chandra*</note> and the **Sun** <note>original: *Divakara*</note>, I will speak of the differences in their aspects on the earth. If situated in the **Mouth of Rahu** <note>original: *Rahu-mukha*</note>, but the Moon is in the back portion, it grants victory. In the "container" arrangement <note>original: *samputa*</note>, in battle, it brings victory when at the front-left. If the Moon is in the "Skull of Man" position on the left side, it grants victory. The **Sun** <note>original: *Bhanu*</note> is everywhere and all-going; it gives victory at the back-right. This concludes the **Rahu-Fire of Time Wheel**.

In the lordship of the donkey and the lunar days <term>tithi</term> in order, the sunrise stays in the right-hand path...

Always from that point, starting from the lunar day <term>tithi</term>, each movement <term>bhukti</term> consists of five *nadis* <note>a unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note>. When positioned behind one's back, it is **Victorious** <note>original: *jayada*</note>. On the left side, it brings death. If positioned in the middle, it brings a mixture of death and life. In both cases, a small portion should be avoided during battle according to the theory of the "Land of Sound" <term>Svarabhumi</term>. **Thus ends the Land of Sound.**

Indra (East), Vayu (Northwest), Meru, the Watery (West), the Auspicious, the Moon (North), the Night-stalker (Southwest), and Yama (South)—Rahu is said to wander through these eight directions. **Thus we demonstrate the positions of Rahu.**

Ishana (Northeast), Yama (South), Vata (Northwest), Soma (North), Agni (Southeast), Varuna (West), and the East—he wanders through these in a sequence during the night. The *muhurtas* <note>auspicious 48-minute periods</note> for the day and the directions are thus proclaimed. These *muhurtas* of Rahu must be known by those who understand the "Vedic sounds" <term>Svaravedis</term>.

In each *muhurta*, Rahu, the Sun, the Moon, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn wander from the East or Northeast. Then, the rising sign <term>udaya</term> is determined. During the day of the week, the sequence should be known starting from the direction of the Sun. Rahu is known to be born of the water-element; where Saturn wanders, he stays. **Thus ends the Kalika.**

One should observe [the positions] in trees and other objects. When Rahu is at one's right or back, there is success; others lead to defeat. According to the doctrine of Rahu, he resides in the breath <term>prana</term>. There are three types of Rahu. In a diagram <term>chakra</term> made of seven lines, the Sun is situated on both sides. Just as the Sun rules the day, the Moon is situated likewise for the night. Those desiring victory must act when [Rahu] is positioned at their back. **Thus ends the Explanation.**

On the second and tenth lunar days, and on the eleventh and third, the Maiden <note>original: *Kaumari*, one of the Yoginis</note> resides in the Southeast. On the fourth and twelfth, she is said to be in the Southwest. In the southern part, on the fifth and thirteenth. On the sixth and fourteenth, the Indra-energy is situated in the West. On the Full Moon and the seventh day, the rise of the energy occurs in the Northwest. If the day does not rain, the Time-Goddess <term>Kala Yogini</term> follows a path through the great past. In the positions of the directions, if she is seen directly in front, she is considered to be in the West. If she is in a corner, she remains in that corner. This is the sequence of the rising [Yoginis] according to the lunar days.

To the right, it is not **Victorious** <note>original: *jayada*</note>; to the left or facing forward, the result varies. The **Circle of the Yoginis** <term>Yogini-chakra; a diagram used to track the movement of eight female spirits across the compass</term> is described in the *Brahmayamala* <note>a major early Tantric text dedicated to the cult of the Bhairavas and Yoginis</note>. Thus is the Circle of the Yoginis proclaimed. 

The distinction of the middle and left cycles exists on the left side during the current lunar days <term>tithi</term> starting from the first day of the fortnight. One should avoid the time [of the Yoginis] when it is in the Southeast <note>Agni</note> or the West <note>Varuna</note>. In the "Time of the Sword" <term>Khangi-kala</term>, one should know the Lord of Loss when it is arrived at. In the remaining house, it becomes the time. When positioned behind or to the right, it is **Victorious**. Such is the Time of the Sword. 

In the *Shatyaka* <note>possibly a reference to a specific school or text of divination</note>, the time between Mars and Jupiter is proclaimed. In the residence of Venus, the eldest region is to be avoided during the breaking of the ranks.
