This is proclaimed for all wars at all times. Regarding the **Strength of the Wind**: in the time of battle, when the Moon is stable, the decision is certain. When the Sun-breath is flowing <note>referring to the right nostril being dominant</note>, victory is always achieved, O Arjuna <note>original: *Partha*, a traditional address for a warrior-prince</note>. In battle, a treaty occurs if the influence is in the West. Victory is ordained by that; thus the path of the wind is described. (95)

When a full *nadi* <note>a unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note> is completed, death or defeat lies ahead in the place of the elements. There is no doubt about this. (56) As previously stated in the *Veda-bhanu*, the Moon is situated there, and the positions follow a sequence. (57) 

When the Moon-breath rises <note>the left nostril</note>, the Moon should be kept to the left or facing forward. When the Sun-breath flows, victory is won if the Sun is kept behind or to the right. (58) 

This is the **Land of the Body** <term>Shariraka-bhumi</term>. These "Powers of the Land" are known through the sequence of the rising breath. Especially at the fall of night, the union of these lands is powerful. In the month, the fortnight, and the day, the movements should be known by all embodied beings during the day and the night. (60) 

When seeking victory in battle, if one does not attain the correct "Sound" <term>Svara; the flow of breath</term>, one should chant the **Great Mantra of the Sun** <note>original: *Bhaskara-mahamantra*</note>. In a triangle of twelve inches, facing oneself, with the energies of the Sun: (102)

**Om Hrim, salutations to the Teacher.** <note>original: *Om Hrim Shastre Namah*</note>

This mantra, ending with the word *Svaha*, is used when performing the fire offering <term>homa</term>. At the start of battle, mounted on a horse, one should be focused in mind. Again, facing the Sun, even if the mantra is under a curse, one should look at the Sun while Venus resides in the direction. It is auspicious in the direction of the wind <note>Northwest</note>. Endowed with full radiance when Mercury and Jupiter are nearby. (4) 

When the diagram is positioned, one carries the knowledge of the Sun.

...when combined with the name, it becomes [auspicious] 5. In this way, when the Sun is positioned in battle, it remains stable forever on the surface of the earth; this has been proclaimed by the Snowy One <note>original: *Himaya*; likely referring to Shiva as the Lord of the Himalayas</note> as the very essence of truth 6.

When, by the strength of the name, the illustrious maker of the world... in this work, **The Victory of the Royal Battle-Elephant** <term>Ranahastiraja Vijaya; a medieval Sanskrit manual on military magic and omens</term>, composed by the venerable **Ranahastin**, the chapter concerning the composition of **Mantras and Yantras** <term>Mantra-yantra; sacred sounds and geometric diagrams used as tools for spiritual or worldly power</term> and the forces of beings has been proclaimed. A **Mandala** <term>Mandala; a sacred circular diagram used as a focal point for rituals</term> measuring three cubits should be drawn, containing the fall of the Sun <note>original: *ravipata*; perhaps referring to specific astrological alignments within the diagram</note> and the four heroes. In the pericarp <note>the center of the lotus-shaped diagram</note>, one should place the Goddess and Shiva himself. One should worship them with the seven "immortal" name-mantras, beginning with the sacred syllable *Om* and ending with *Namah*. Before the Mandala, one should write the name of the great hero and place him there. He should be bathed with pots filled with water from holy pilgrimage sites 8. 

Then, the hero should be consecrated by being sprinkled one hundred times while reciting the **Sanjayana Mantra** <term>Sanjayana Mantra; a "victory-producing" or "conquering" chant</term>. The hero is then consecrated with the following sounds: **Om Hrum Hma Sah**. This is the **Death-conquering Mantra** <note>original: *mrityu maya mantra*</note>. Once consecrated, the hero becomes **Victorious in Battle** <term>Sangrame Vijayi</term>. His body cannot be pierced by the weapons of the enemy. A man who stands in the center of the Mandala and is thus consecrated always defeats the enemy hosts. Even one who has been stripped of his kingdom shall regain his kingdom.

### **The Ritual of Consecration** <term>Abhisheka Vidhi</term>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for military success. Following the previous page's focus on divination and the "Circle of the Yoginis," we now move into the consecration of weapons and the creation of protective talismans. The text shifts from general omens to specific Tantric "seed-syllables" (Bijamantras) used to empower the tools of war.</meta>

The Mantra for the Weapon of Saturn: **Om Hram Hrim Hum Haim Hraum Hrah.** Salutations to the shield, the jewel-weapon of the fierce Rudra-Bhairava. <note>original: *Bhairavarudraya-aparatnayudhaya phalakaya namah*</note> Thus ends the Mantra for the Shield. 

The Mantra for the Armlet: **Om Lolillailolaim.** Salutations to the armlet-weapon, the weapon of the path of light. 

The Mantra for the Arrow: **Om Sam Sim Sum Saim Saum Srah.** Salutations to the Five-Arrowed One, the embodiment of the five deities of the circle. <note>original: *valayapanchadevata-murttaye-pancanaya namah*</note> Thus ends the Mantra for the Arrow. 

The Mantra for the Spear: **Om Kram Krim Krum Kraim Kraum Krah.** Salutations to the spear, the weapon of Bhairava in the form of the Trident-bearer. Thus ends the Mantra for the Spear. 

The Mantra for the Dagger: **Om Mrogeshvari Chamunda Hram Hrim Hum Haim Hraum Hrah.** Salutations to the goddess Katyayani, the embodiment of the Divine Lady. <note>original: *Bhagavatimurttaye Katyayani*</note> Thus ends the Mantra for the Dagger. 

### **The Procedure for Battle Worship**

In a portion of cow urine, take a string and tie one hundred knots while reciting the "Victory Mantra" <term>Sanjaya mantra; a formula intended to ensure triumph</term>. By performing this ritual, the **Battle Bracelet** <term>Ranakankana; a consecrated wristband worn by warriors for protection and power</term> is created. By binding it to the body, victory shall be achieved. 

Regarding the **Warrior’s Plate** <term>Virapatta; a headband or metallic plate inscribed with protective symbols and worn on the forehead</term>: combine the dust of camphor and *Gorochana* <term>Gorochana; a bright yellow pigment obtained from cattle, considered highly auspicious in Tantric rites</term>. In a beautiful spot like the "Cuckoo’s Abode," sitting on a fine white cloth, one should write the name of the seeker in the middle of two "Sa" sounds. It should be surrounded by the "Hum" sound of fire. 

The name of the seeker should be written in the center of a "Earth-Envelope" <note>original: *Prithvi-samputa*; likely a specific geometric square in a Yantra representing the element of Earth</note>. With the Breath of Life, [ending in] *Phat*, and the directions as garments, one should recite the mantra and seal it upon the cloth. This ensures victory in the thick of battle. 

Thus is the **Warrior’s Plate**. Following the previously mentioned method, the **Battle Plate** <term>Ranapatta</term> is inscribed. When the sacrifice is made, write this mantra: 

**Om Hram Hrim Hum Haim Hraum Hrah.** 

By writing this mantra, the birth of great victory occurs. By the power of this [talisman] held in the hand or on the head, one achieves supreme valor. Thus ends the **Battle Plate**.

One should draw the terrifying form of Bhairava <term>Bhairava; the "Terrible" or "Frightful" manifestation of Shiva, often associated with destruction and protection in battle</term> using all the ritual substances, either on the right hand or the forehead. Surround it with the **Tripura Mantra** <term>Tripura Mantra; a powerful formula dedicated to the goddess Tripura Sundari, the "Beauty of the Three Cities"</term> and seal it. Through this, one clearly conquers the three worlds. 

**Om Yem Yem Hlim Hraum Hrim.** 

This is the **Tripura Mantra**. It should be recited while tying a thread of eight-fold thickness around the waist or head. By this connection, one becomes invincible. Using the Victory Mantra, tie one hundred knots; when bound to the waist, it grants the strength of a hero.

Thus ends the protective girdle of the goddess Brahmani. <note>original: *mekhala brahmani kavacha*</note> Whosoever enters the battlefield empowered by this shall become a lord. In this month, one should use the "Red-Faced" gesture by placing the tongue against the palate. One should also write on beautiful **Birch Bark** <term>bhūrjapatra; the bark of the Himalayan birch tree, traditionally used in India for writing sacred mantras and protective amulets</term>. While writing, chant the mantra and place the name of the specific warrior in the center. [28]

The mantra should be chanted, and the name should be woven into the recitation. By the power of this act, whether in a village or in battle, victory is assured. This ritual of the "Hand-Holder" is performed on the ground in the forest under the sun. One should take a thread spun by a maiden during the month of Kartika <note>October–November</note> and tie it with one hundred recitations of the **Aghora Mantra** <term>Aghora; a fierce form of the god Shiva; the mantra is used for protection and the removal of obstacles</term>. [31] Now, for these mantras which are to be recited, each should be repeated separately one hundred and eight times. By this, the "Hero’s Mark" is produced on the ear. [32]

> **Om. O Virgin Goddess, carrier of the arrows that strike the enemy, dweller in the forest, subduer of the three cities: shatter, shatter all my foes! Svaha!** <note>original: *Om kumari tava tarahara vana yavasini tripurdhasatena sarva mu yam bhanjaya bhanjaya svaha*</note>

This is the mantra.

> **Om. Shrum. By this, the invincible are conquered.**

Even a ruler shall be filled with fear [of the practitioner]. One becomes endowed with all powers and energy. Thus, by the power of the "Moon-Discus," the "Vattri," and the "Triple-Folded Blossom," the man’s body in battle becomes a **Restrainer of All Weapons** <note>original: *sarvayudha nivarini*</note>. [25] Under an auspicious star, take the root of the **Apamarga plant** <term>Apamarga; *Achyranthes aspera*, a plant held sacred in the Vedas for its protective and cleansing properties</term>. By merely applying this as a paste, a warrior wards off all living beings. [15] By applying an ointment or paste to the body, a man becomes victorious in the assembly and attains a **Diamond Body** <term>Vajra-deha; a body as hard and indestructible as a diamond or a thunderbolt</term>.

Worn in the middle of the arm, or bound as a talisman, it wards off all creatures. [1] Held on the right arm or the left side of the heart, the man goes into battle like the god Rudra himself, possessing a diamond-hard body. [16] Encased in "Auspicious Iron" or combined with mica and sacred minerals, it acts as a "Discus of Death" to the enemy and a restrainer of all weapons. [36] Take the white **Girikarṇī** <term>Girikarṇī; the Butterfly Pea plant (*Clitoria ternatea*), often used in Tantric rituals</term>, the Shami tree, and white Gunja berries. When ground with sandalwood and applied as a mark, it ensures victory. [4] The "Downward-Facing Flame" and the white Girikarṇī, when combined with **Gorochana** <term>Gorochana; a bright yellow pigment found in the gallbladders of cattle, highly prized in ritual magic for attraction and subjugation</term> and applied as a forehead mark, will utterly **confuse the enemy** <note>original: *shatru mohane*</note>. [4] Gold, fire, and the five-fold...

Whosoever applies the **forehead mark** <term>tilaka; a ritual mark applied to the forehead, often using powdered minerals or botanical pastes</term> is seen with favor among all beings. [41] One should take the skull of a black cobra <note>original: *krishnasarpakapala*</note> and, in the presence of a cluster of white *gunja* plants, collect its root. [42] When this mark is applied, even if one is surrounded by enemies from his own clan, he shall move without fear on this earth. [43]

Through this mark, a woman may perceive hidden wells and ponds. <note>This suggests a form of dowsing or clairvoyance granted by the ritual mark.</note> The goddesses to be invoked are: Brahmadandi, Kaumari, Ishvari, the All-Encompassing, Varahi, Chakrani, Chandi, and Mahalakshmi. [44] These are known as the Divine Mothers who grant strength.

...

Gold, silver, and other fine metals should be prepared by the practitioners along with **Mercury** <term>parada/rasa; in Indian alchemy (Rasashastra), mercury is considered a potent substance capable of transforming both metals and the human body</term>. [48] To pierce and bind the mercury, it should be placed in the mouth of a vessel and joined with iron. Place it in the middle of a forest, without external support, in a water-vessel with **Peepal** <term>pippala; the Sacred Fig (Ficus religiosa), considered holy in Indian traditions</term> for seven cycles. [50] When the bronze is refined and bound within the center of the bulb, it shall not break. [51]

Using iron powder and borax <note>original: *tanka*</note>, one should heat it briefly. There is no doubt that even a small amount will be effective. [52] When all these are placed within a crucible, a beautiful pill is born, known by the name **Jau-Sendari**. It is said to grant all perfections and destroy the suffering of the world. [53]

One who possesses this becomes a hero victorious in battle, possessing a **Diamond Body** <term>vajra-deha; a body as indestructible and hard as a diamond or a thunderbolt</term> and great strength. [54] He is forever dear to all people and always fortunate among women. This pill has been described by me as told in the **Brahmayamala** <term>Brahmayamala; an ancient and influential tantric scripture dedicated to the cult of Bhairava</term>. [55]

The categories of these pills or herbs are: the Lioness, the Tigress, the Deer, and the Swan. These are their effects in sequence. [56] The "Lioness" <note>original: *Simhi*</note> is the color of gold; the "Tigress" <note>original: *Vyaghri*</note> is like a bull; the "Deer" <note>original: *Mrigi*</note> should be known by its yellow flowers and white stems. [57] The "Swan" <note>original: *Hansi*</note> is as white as water. Knowing these distinctions, one should begin the work. [58]

The herb named Lioness is a great essence. Within the "Lioness Cowrie," the mercury is processed. [59] By placing it in the mouth and combining it with lead, a man becomes transformed. [60]

### The Circle of Air

One should visualize the **Circle of Air** <note>original: *vayumandalam*; a mystical diagram or visualization of the element of air</note> marked with the **Seed-Syllable of Wind** <note>original: *vayu-bije*; the syllable 'Yam' which governs the air element</note>... in the **twelve-spoked** <term>dvadashara; a twelve-spoked wheel or chakra used in visualization</term>... by the path of sound. ### The Trumpet of Victory

Using seven parts of lead and iron <note>original: *trapah-mayasai*</note>, and the medicinal fruit, one should fashion a **Trumpet of Victory** <note>original: *jaya-kahalah*</note>. By the sound produced from this instrument, even the strongest armies are tormented as if by the play of the "Great Night" <note>original: *atira-tra*; a reference to a specific powerful Vedic ritual or a night of destruction</note>. Thus concludes the Procedure for the Trumpet of Victory. 

### Sustenance by Air

One should perform this likewise: using the fruit of the **Licorice plant** <note>original: *madhuka*; Madhuca longifolia</note>... the ground... the goddess... accompanied by the essence of the **Prickly Chaff** <note>original: *apamarga*</note>... and the juice of the **Aloe Vera** <note>original: *kumari*</note>. By grinding these together in a mortar, one achieves **Sustenance by Air** <term>vayu-bhojanam; a yogic or alchemical state where one is nourished by the vital breath or air rather than solid food</term>. 

With the plants **Brahmi** <note>original: *brahmi*; Bacopa monnieri, known for mental clarity</note> and **Aloe** <note>original: *kumari*</note>... this is the [ritual] of **Wind-Eating** <note>original: *vayu-bhojane*</note>.

### The Ritual of Ash-Pounding

It shall appear in **four colors** <note>original: *chatur-varna*</note>, following the sixty-four measurements. [91]

### The Song of Rama

Joined with forty-four verses of the **Song of Rama** <note>original: *ramagite*</note>, I shall now declare the Essence <note>original: *rasa*</note> which consists of all auspicious mantras. At the proper time:

#### The Mantra of the Fanged Deity
**Om Hrim Hrim!** To the one with distorted fangs and [three] eyes, **Phat!** <note>original: *vikritadamshtrin netre phat*; a fierce invocation typical of tantric destructive rites</note>. Having offered oblations, one should chant this mantra. Perform a fire sacrifice (*homa*) with one thousand red flowers and honey. ->**The Verse of the Yantra**<-
"He who is the Lord of Death, the protector, always fearless... the source of the womb... the dweller in the forest." <note>original: *yama-rama-sada-bheya...*; this verse appears to be a protective or power-bestowing shloka associated with Yama</note>

One should chant this verse of the Lord of Death thirty-two thousand times. By following these rules, one attains the fruits of the fire sacrifice and becomes a master of the rite, accomplishing whatever task the mind desires.

### The Ritual of Destruction
<note>original: *Marana*; the magic act of killing or causing terminal ruin to an enemy</note>

In the sanctuary of the funeral pyre, where the red and black fluids reside, one should write upon a **shroud** <term>pretapata; a cloth taken from a corpse or used in a funeral</term> using a pen made from the bone of a dead man. In the center, write the name of the target <term>sadhya-nama; the person the ritual is intended to affect</term>. 

One should visualize the form of **Yama** <note>the God of Death</note> seated upon a buffalo, holding a staff. The practitioner, having become like Yama himself—naked, with hair loose and disheveled—should focus their intent. Placing the writing in an earthen bowl used by a priest, wrap it with a white thread. By burying this in the center of a **cemetery** <note>original: *smashana-bhumi*</note>, the enemy will be destroyed within seven days.

**Thus concludes the Ritual of Destruction.**

### The Ritual of Enchantment
<note>original: *Mohana*; the magic act of deluding, attracting, or hypnotizing others</note>

Using a mixture of yellow pigment <term>gorochana; a bright yellow pigment found in a cow's gall bladder, used in tantra</term>, saffron, sandalwood, and red sap, one should write this yantra. Write the name of the target at the "door" or entrance of the diagram. Surround it with a circle of fire and wrap it with a **red thread**. Once the diagram is completed by the practitioner, it should be placed within a pillar and worshiped with red flowers. Within seven days, the target will surely be enchanted.

**Thus concludes the Ritual of Enchantment.**

### The Ritual of Paralysis
<note>original: *Stambhana*; the magic act of "stilling" or paralyzing an opponent's speech, mind, or body</note>

Using a mixture of realgar <note>red arsenic</note>, turmeric, and black mustard, one should write upon a cloth of blue and red. Write the name of the target in the center and surround it with yellow mustard seeds. Create a shape like a mountain and place the diagram inside. Worship it with **yellow Dhattura** flowers <note>a poisonous plant associated with Shiva and paralyzing rites</note> and a red pot. Through this, the body and mind of the enemy will be paralyzed within seven days.

**Thus concludes the Ritual of Paralysis.**

In a windless place, using the blood of a buffalo... <note>The text cuts off here, beginning a new ritual instruction</note>

### The Garland Mantra of Hanuman
<note>A 'Garland Mantra' (Mala Mantra) is a long-form incantation, often exceeding forty syllables, believed to wrap around the practitioner like a protective garland or to encompass the totality of a deity’s power.</note>

[Salutation] to the one of adamantine body, who holds the adamantine staff, who is tawny and reddish-brown, with hair like [unclear], possessed of great strength, with a red face and a tongue like lightning, with great and terrible fangs. [To the one who] strikes with his fangs, the great Lord who stands firm, who moves mountains, the Lord of the skies. 

Come, O Lord of great strength and prowess! Bhairava <note>A fierce manifestation of Shiva who commands the spirits and lower deities</note> commands you! Come, O fiercely terrible one! Encircle the sun <note>original: *arkene*; possibly referring to the enemy or the solar disc</note> with your long tail! Encircle! Encircle! Break! Break! Scatter! **Hum Phat Svaha!**

***

### Instructions for the Ritual
Thus is the Garland Mantra. One should illuminate the mantra... <unclear>e di payedeke shva</unclear>. The letters should be inscribed on the tongue three times. 25. 

For the acts of paralyzing <term>stambhana; the magical power to freeze an enemy or a natural force</term>, deluding <term>mohana; the power to confuse or hypnotize</term>, obstructing, and breaking, the wise practitioner should apply the "Seed" syllables of the Goddess, beginning with the sacred sound **Om** <note>original: *pranavadi*</note> and ending with "Salutation to the Lord." 26.

One should place the syllable **La** <note>The seed syllable for the Earth element</note> throughout. The wise practitioner should place the root mantra on the face, the head, and the private parts. On the tail, one should place the Mother Goddesses <term>matrika; the personified energies of the Sanskrit alphabet</term> starting with **Om**. 28. 

### The Consecration Ritual
<note>original: *Pratishtha*; the ritual process of 'breathing life' into a statue or diagram so it becomes a living vessel for the deity.</note>

This should be performed upon a small vessel <note>original: *mallaka*</note> accompanied by the laughter of the Mother Goddesses. One should carry out the consecration on the eighth or the fourteenth day of the lunar fortnight. 29. 

It should be done during any auspicious festival, provided the stars and the moon are in a position of strength. The practitioner must wear red garments and be smeared with red sandalwood paste. 30. 

Adorned with red flowers, and holding a weapon, the practitioner should draw an elegant eight-petaled lotus. For the purposes of paralyzing, deluding, purifying, and breaking, one should place the vowels in the cardinal directions in order. 32. 

The sacred seats <term>pitha; centers of spiritual power</term> such as **Uddiyana** should be established in the northeast corner. Success in the rites of the Goddess Bhairavi and Lord Bhairava is attained here. A fee <note>original: *dakshina*</note> should be given to the teacher. This ritual applies to images made of wood, stone, or to mystical diagrams (*yantras*). 35. 

### The Benefits of the Ritual
In whatever region, village, or city this consecration is performed:
- No famine shall occur there.
- No fear of death or calamities shall dwell there.
- No foreign armies shall invade.
- No one shall be afflicted by diseases.
- No fear of opposition or enmity shall exist there. 36.

Through the power of Hanuman, the influence of malevolent spirits—such as the Shakinis, Bhutas, Vetalas, and Pishachas <note>various classes of ghosts, vampires, and meat-eating demons in Hindu lore</note>—and even poison will not manifest. 38. 

When the banner of Hanuman is placed upon elephants or horses, the enemy's army will flee from the battlefield when facing it. 38. Just by the sight of it, they become agitated...

### The Benefits of the Sovereign Mantra

[Just as] deer [flee] at the sight of a lion, so too do the enemies of one who possesses this [mantra]. Now I shall declare the procedure that grants all spiritual and worldly accomplishments <term>siddhi; supernatural powers or perfections attained through yoga or ritual</term>.

For the destruction of enemies, [one should prepare the materials]. Using yellow pigment <term>gorochana; a sacred gallstone pigment from cattle</term> and saffron <term>kunkuma</term>, one should draw [the diagram] on a beautiful four-petaled lotus or upon a plate of gold. It should be inscribed with a stylus using [consecrated] earth. 

In the center of this perfected [diagram], place the "Root" mantra. On the eight petals, place the Garland Mantra. It should be encircled by the sacred sound **Hrim** <note>original: *Hrim-kara*; a 'seed' syllable representing the power of the Goddess and the energy of manifestation</note> and further encircled by the three worlds <note>This likely refers to three concentric circles drawn around the yantra to bind its power</note>. 

If one wears this on the head or the arm, no obstacles will ever arise. Whether in battle, in the presence of the royal court, or during a legal dispute <note>original: *vyavahare*; refers to litigation or business dealings</note>, one will never be defeated. This "adamantine body" <note>original: *vajra-deha*; a body as indestructible as a diamond or a thunderbolt</note> provides great strength against the faults caused by demonic possession, and against all poisons, whether from stationary sources [plants] or moving creatures [snakes]. 50.

***

### The Protective Power of the Amulet

There is no fear for such a person. He becomes beloved even by those who would do him harm <note>original: *ghati*; executioners or strikers</note>, and by women. The malevolent planets <term>grahas; celestial influencers like Saturn or Mars</term> and minor astral spirits are held in check by his very presence <note>original: *pindena*; refers to his physical person or the 'lump' of the amulet</note>. There is no fear of water, fire, tigers, or thieves. All fevers are pacified through the specific power of this King of Mantras, which destroys all calamities. 

***

### Construction and Consecration

The letters [of the mantra] are to be arranged in groups of twenty-six and four hundred... <unclear>hyetarena na</unclear>... in groups of eight and seven. 51.

During the period of an eclipse, until its release <note>original: *grahana-mukti*; the moment the sun or moon is freed from the shadow</note>, the practitioner should bind [the amulet] with a red thread. Having recited the chosen mantra, it becomes the destroyer of diseases and foes. **[Mantra Fragment:]** *Departure, joy, do not go, go, go elsewhere...* <note>original: *visarga-muda-mudapati...*; these are likely command words to direct the energy of the ritual</note>. In all conditions, this brings hidden strength and prevents misfortune. 52.

***

### The Fire Sacrifice

Having inscribed this during an eclipse of the moon or the sun, one should perform the recitation. One must perform a fire sacrifice <term>homa; the ritual of pouring offerings into a consecrated fire</term> using one-tenth the number of recitations, offering sesame seeds and mustard seeds. 53.

When placed upon... <unclear>gudakalivata</unclear>... the destruction of the enemy’s strength is certain. 54. 

This should be prepared with musk <note>original: *madena*</note> and colored red, according to the previously described characteristics. One should then empower the elephants, horses, and chariots, or the vessels used for drinking. 55.

### The Rite of Piercing the Enemy

With these syllables, one should strive with great effort. For the purpose of "piercing" <term>vedha; a tantric act of spiritually striking or paralyzing an opponent</term>, and to instill affection or power, one should place the [talisman] upon the head. Once it is known that one has seen the face of the enemy's army <note>original: *parachakrasya mukha darshat*; referring to the vanguard or the literal sight of an opposing force</note>, the practitioner attains the fruit of the rite in that very moment.

#### The Ritual of the Fire Sacrifice

One should perform the fire sacrifice <term>homa; a ritual where offerings are made into a consecrated fire</term> using the following materials:
*   The power of the Tail-Mantra <note>original: *langulila*; likely referring to the *Hanuman-langula-astra* or the "Tail-Weapon" of Hanuman</note>
*   Poison
*   Black mustard seeds <note>original: *rajika*</note>
*   Neem leaves <note>original: *nimba*</note>
*   Ghee <note>original: *ghrita*; clarified butter</note>

One should kindle the fire using wood from the *Chitrak* or *Kodrava* plants within the sacrificial pit. Facing the direction of the Fish <note>The text mentions *matsyabhimukha*, which may refer to a specific ritual orientation or a symbolic posture</note>, one must recite the mantra eight thousand times every single day. If this is performed for seven days, the enemy will be overcome with intense suffering.

From this fire sacrifice <term>angada homa</term>, great pain, blisters, and boils will afflict the enemy. There is no doubt that the opponent will perish if this is performed during battle.

### The Adamantine Body and the Secret Ritual

For the one who recites this "Garland of Fire-Sacrifice" mantras <note>original: *homa-mala-mantra*</note>, the body becomes like a "Thunderbolt of Wind" <term>vajra-vayu-deha; a body possessing the indestructible hardness of a diamond and the unstoppable speed/power of the wind-god, Vayu, who is Hanuman's father</term>, possessing incomparable valor.

Now, another method of "piercing" <note>original: *vedha*</note> is described, which is exceedingly rare and kept secret in all the scriptures. It is known by the name *Mabuka* <note>likely a variation of *Manduka* or "Frog-ritual"</note>. 

1.  One should obtain a beautiful leaf of birch bark <term>bhujapatra; traditionally used for writing sacred diagrams</term>.
2.  Upon it, using gold or specific pigments, draw the figure.
3.  Following the prescribed rules, the practitioner—with hair unbound and naked—should face the fish-direction.
4.  Where the battle is fought, one should perform the rite of the "Fierce One" <note>original: *aghori*; referring to a terrifying form of Shiva or a fierce tantric path</note>.

By identifying the soul [of the enemy] and entering it through the power of the practitioner <note>original: *atma vishtam tu sadhaka*</note>, one should prepare an effigy <note>original: *pinda*</note>. One should then pierce it with an iron stake or a thorn made of *Shalya* wood. 

Bring this to the enemy’s house and bury it. By the power of the "Frog’s Breath" <note>original: *dardura-ghrata*; a technical term in subduing rituals</note>, and by performing this during the time of war over a fire of anxiety-inducing wood, the enemy’s mind and body will be tormented by fever and pain. They will surely die; there is no doubt in any direction.

This "Frog-Face" ritual <note>original: *mandukasya*</note> acts like a lion's roar, causing the enemy to tremble like a cuckoo <note>original: *kokila*</note>. Even in a royal city, these faults and strikes will fall upon the enemy like a thunderbolt. One should encircle the diagram with the "Seed of Illusion" <note>original: *maya-bija*; the sacred syllable **Hrim**</note> and bind it with the "Goad" <note>original: *ankusha*; a mantra used to hook or pull energy</note>.

### The Rite of Immobilization

Then, one should pin [the ritual object] with thorns <note>original: *kaṇṭakaiḥ kīlayet*; a common practice in "binding" magic where thorns from specific trees are used to pierce an image or diagram</note>. One should perform the worship in the middle of a chosen site using a bundle of grass. Afterward, one may begin the work for success in battle, in disputes, or in legal matters. In this way, there occurs a "stopping of the measure" <note>original: *mātra-stambha*; likely referring to the rhythm or flow of an opponent's speech or actions</note>, as well as the immobilization of the mind.

In disputes, the immobilization of the tongue <term>jihvā-stambha; a specific tantric power used to strike an opponent dumb or make them unable to testify against the practitioner</term> occurs, by which one commands authority over others.

#### The Solar Yantra for Psychological Breaking

Through the pair of Sun-Yantras <note>original: *arka-yantra-yuge*; Arka refers to the Sun or the Calotropis plant</note>, one should smear the names [of the targets] with the "Mind-Destroying" paste <note>original: *cintāmāreṇa*; possibly a specific herbal preparation</note>. As before, one should pin it and then cover it firmly with a cloth. By the power of this Yantra, a "breaking of the mind" <note>original: *mano-bhaṅgo*; psychological collapse or loss of will</note> occurs in the enemy.

### The Yantra for Breaking the Enemy

Furthermore, should the enemy enter into battle, they will perish without a doubt. This is the **Yantra for Breaking the Enemy** <note>original: *shatru-bhaṅga-yantra*</note>.

It is composed of:
*   The syllable *Ha* <note>original: *ha-kāro*</note> adorned with six vowels.
*   The nasal sound and the divine point <term>nāda-bindu; the semi-circle and dot representing the vibration of the cosmos</term>.
*   The syllable *Om* <note>original: *o-kāro*</note> placed in a protective "casket" <note>original: *sampuṭa*; a method where two mantras or symbols enclose a name</note>.
*   Eight thunderbolts <term>vajra</term> pointing in the cardinal directions.
*   Sixteen vowels placed in the corners.

When written according to the rules, this Yantra acts as a destroyer of obstacles and a means of bringing others under one's influence <term>vashya; the rite of attraction or subjugation</term>. One who is devoted to benevolent meditation should face the North while writing this auspicious Yantra.

### Protective and Healing Applications

To achieve success, one should write the Yantra within a bronze vessel <note>original: *kāṃsya-bhājana*</note> using fragrant substances and red sandalwood. By worshipping it with one hundred and eight white flowers, the following effects are realized:

1.  **Destruction of Malignant Knowledge:** The Yantra destroys the "higher knowledge" or spells used by an opponent.
2.  **Protection from Spirits:** Ghosts, vampires <term>vetāla</term>, <unclear>ikkanībhūta</unclear>, spirits <term>pishācha</term>, and demons <term>rākṣasa</term> are subdued by the brilliance of this Yantra.
3.  **Curing Ailments:** It is effective against:
    *   Cholera <term>visūchikā</term>
    *   Chronic wasting diseases
    *   Boils and blisters <term>sphoṭaka</term>
    *   Illness caused by malevolent sorcery <term>abhichāra</term>
    *   Poisons, whether from stationary (plant) or moving (animal) sources.

#### Support for Childbirth and Fever

For the pains of pregnancy, it ensures the womb is well-protected. In this way, diseases that would normally be fatal are neutralized, and one may even obtain a virtuous son who lives a long life.

I shall explain in brief [the rite of] the **Conqueror of Death** <note>original: *mṛtyuñjaya*; a powerful form of Shiva invoked for health and longevity</note> situated in the center of gold. When the sun is made divine, with gems joined to its low sound, one should take the third letter of the dental class <note>The letter 'Da'</note> and adorn it with vowels. With the flood of mantras beginning with the Sacred Syllable <term>praṇava; the syllable "Om"</term>, placed upon an eight-petaled lotus, the syllable *vighna* <note>meaning "obstacle"</note> is stationed on the petals. There, where the breath and the fire-vowel are always present, one should place the twenty-two syllables in all directions. One should place them in order, joined with the "ten-million" power in a golden vessel. 

There, one finds the seats of success of **Bhairava and Bhairavi** <note>The fierce aspects of Shiva and the Goddess</note>, the lords of the house of attainment, accompanied by the eight-fold groups of the Eight Protectors of the Realm <term>kṣetrapāla; guardian deities of a specific physical or ritual space</term>. Through these syllables, one brings about the beautiful destruction of death. Taking the syllables in the proper sequence—beginning with the Brahmin class and so on—the letter *Va* is transformed into the form of *Ge*. 

The practitioner, possessing the means of accomplishment, should sit facing North and write with a golden pen the names of the Conqueror of Death. Through this, a beautiful symbolic form <term>liṅga; the iconic representation of Shiva</term> is created. One should bathe it with the **Five Divine Nectars** <term>pañcāmṛta; a ritual mixture of milk, curd, ghee, honey, and sugar</term>. For seven nights, one should worship it with white flowers, camphor, and sandalwood. With foods of like quality, the practitioner becomes sharp and keen.

One should perform the fire sacrifice <term>homa; a ritual where offerings are made into a consecrated fire</term> in a lotus-shaped fire-pit using honey, clarified butter, guggulu resin, and white mustard seeds. Facing the direction of the Lord Rudra <note>the North-East</note>, using Shami wood and Durva grass, and understanding the gestures of the sky-goers <term>khecarī-mudrā; a specialized tantric physical/energetic seal</term>, one should offer the oblation.

By this, one is released from disease. The sinful soul is purified of sin. One is liberated from suffering and the [malice of] the planets; they attain a state of gentleness and no other. The Dakinis, ghosts, vampires, and flesh-eating demons <note>original: *śākinī-bhūta-vetāla-piśāca*</note>, along with his fevers, shall perish. Whether it be stationary or moving poison within the body, all of it becomes extinguished. Whatever malevolent magic <term>abhicāra; sorcery or black magic intended to harm others</term> has been performed here on earth, it is consumed just as wood is consumed by fire. Even an enemy becomes a friend through the grace of the Lord.

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of the Sarvatobhadra Chakra (The Great Auspicious Chart), a complex astrological grid used for divination. It shifts from the general ritual preparations described on the previous page to specific planetary "piercings" (aspects) and the influence of the mythological serpent deities (Nagas) on human destiny.</meta>

->81<-

### The Effects of Planetary Piercings

It is auspicious when the Sun <note>original: *ravi*</note> is free from the piercing aspect <term>vedha; a technical term in Indian astrology where a planet "pierces" or influences a specific point or letter in a chart</term> of Mars <note>original: *bhauma*</note>. In conjunction with the shadow planet Rahu <term>Rāhu; the North Node of the moon, traditionally viewed as a "shadow planet" that causes eclipses</term>, it is always beneficial for all people. Through the harsh, sharp, and mixed planets, various combinations <term>yoga; a specific alignment of celestial bodies</term> are prescribed. One determines the results—whether death, disease, or pain—by analyzing the energy channels <term>nāḍī; subtle pathways of time or energy in the astrological grid</term> and the secondary planets <term>upagraha; mathematical points in the sky considered to have planetary influence</term>.

During these difficult times and malicious planetary combinations, the moon may be afflicted. In the chart known as the **Sarvatobhadra** <note>literally "Auspicious from all directions"</note>, if the days of Saturn, Mars, or the Sun coincide with the presence of Rahu, certain signs appear. If a person eats or enjoys food while these cruel aspects are active, they may suffer the consequences of these "piercings."

### Alignments and Serpent Deities

When there is a parity of declination <term>krānti-sāmya; when the Sun and Moon are at the same distance from the celestial equator</term> between the Sun and Moon, or when they enter the "Spear" combination <note>original: *shūla-yoga*; a difficult astrological alignment</term>, it is considered a time of great distress for living beings. However, this may be mitigated by a favorable aspect from Mercury <note>original: *budha*</note> when it is positioned nearby.

The great serpent deities <term>nāga; semi-divine serpent beings in Hindu mythology who guard the directions</term> are assigned to the various divisions of the chart:
*   **Ananta** and **Shesha**
*   **Padma** and **Mahapadma**
*   **Takshaka**, **Karkotaka**, and **Pundarika**

These deities arise in sequence starting from the Northeast <note>original: *īshānya*</note>. If a planet is retrograde <term>vakra; the apparent backward motion of a planet as seen from Earth</term> or moving quickly, the effects are felt more intensely. If a planet is joined with "malefic" or cruel planets in the West or the Center while in retrograde, the scholar should know that the results will be specifically challenging.

### The Consequences of the Piercing Aspect (Vedha)

The chart is arranged from the first lunar mansion <term>nakshatra; one of the 27 or 28 divisions of the sky through which the moon passes</term> known as **Ashvini**. One should carefully examine the positions of the planets within these divisions. The specific results of being "pierced" by different planets are as follows:

*   **Sun's piercing:** Fear of the King or government <note>original: *rāja-bhaya*</note>.
*   **Mars's piercing:** Fear of fire.
*   **Mercury's piercing:** Fear or trouble from one's master or employer.
*   **Jupiter's piercing:** Fear of disease or issues related to phlegm <note>original: *kapha*</note>.
*   **Saturn's piercing:** Fear of injury from iron or weapons.
*   **Rahu's piercing:** Great distress, or a state comparable to hell.

When the Moon is full and bright, it brings success and fame; however, if it is afflicted or in a difficult house <term>rāshi; a sign of the zodiac</term>, the traveler or the person acting under that sign will find their desires frustrated.

When the cruel planets <note>original: *pāpa-graha*</note> are positioned in the astrological hour <term>horā; an astrological unit of time roughly equal to one hour</term> of the Sun, even if one is accompanied by helpful influences, if they are devoid of auspiciousness, it is called a **Thunderbolt Strike** <term>vajraghāta; a sudden and destructive astrological affliction</term>. The first is known as the **Fire Strike** <note>original: *agnighātana*</note>; the second is likewise described; the third is the **Life Strike** <term>jīvaghāta; a strike that targets the vital breath or soul</term>; and the **Universal Strike** <note>original: *sarvaghāta*</note> brings all things to rest. One should cause the destruction of iron and wood 
<margin>50</margin>
through these fiery strikes. If there is no gain, and the "thorn" <term>śalya; a hidden defect or ritual impurity</term> remains in the body, a person may suffer. Especially in humans, the Life Strike <note>original: *vainejīvaghātaye*</note> acts with particular force. In the human body, it affects the bones, the heart, the vessels, the nerves, and the very life-essence. Even when one has performed cleansing with ash, if the strike is inauspicious, it causes distress. From these regions, instability arises in the body, and the wise man observes the trembling of the limbs. When this reaches the hands, or when the time of death resides in the channels, one should perform the rituals there to cause the destruction of the enemy.

### The Rituals of Destruction

In the fifth house, if the head is bound and reversed, one should visualize the armor, the skull, and the sword. After worshipping the **Yoginis** <term>yoginī; powerful female deities or semi-divine spirits in Tantric tradition</term>, one should begin the ritual work again. The spirits known as **Dakinis** <term>ḍākinī; fierce female spirits often associated with the transition between life and death</term> bring about these strikes. One should look upon the lamp-garland of the Dakini and offer worship in the auspicious direction. By fixing the ritual stake <term>kīlaka; a mantra or physical peg used to "pin" energy in place</term> and using the bones of the dead, one takes the head of the enemy and places it within the enemy’s army. 

There, one should perform the strike which causes great destruction, including the **Seizing of the Womb** <note>original: *garbhasya haraṇaṃ*; likely referring to causing a miscarriage or ending a family lineage</note> and the total slaughter of the enemy. With blood and through the breaking of urine <note>original: *mūtrabhaṅga*; likely a reference to physical bladder failure or a specific ritual curse</note>, the breaking of the mind, and the breaking of the army, the goddess causes destruction. By the **Breaking of the Place** <note>original: *sthānabhaṅga*</note>, she rules.

### The Enumeration of the Strikes

The first is the strike of seventy, which breaks twelve parts. The second is the strike of the enemy, residing in nine parts. In the third "noose" <note>original: *pāśe*</note>, there are three parts, and in the fourth, there are various types. There are many hundreds of strikes:
1. The first is of the **Lineage** <note>original: *kuliyātmakaḥ*</note>, known as the strike of the ranks.
2. The second is the **Burnt Strike** <note>original: *dagdha-ghāte*</note>.
3. The third is the **Conjoined Strike**.
4. The fourth is the **Keta Strike**.
5. The fifth is the **Falling Thunderbolt** <note>original: *vajrapāta*</note>, which brings death.

In the hundred-and-one strikes, the first is of the soul. When these coincide with the time of a king’s journey or the meeting of armies, they destroy the strength of the three powers. At that time...

On a cloth from the cremation ground, one should draw a diagram <term>yantra; a mystical geometric design used in worship or magic</term> using many seeds. Without the use of ash, the enemy's house is brought to ruin. This becomes a deed of destruction. On a Tuesday, the practitioner should use this cloth to cause the destruction of the enemy.

Encircled by the seed-syllable of Truth <note>original: *satya-bīja*</note>, one should collect clay from an anthill <note>original: *vālmīka-mṛd*</note>. From this clay, a figure of the target <term>sādhya; the person intended to be affected by the rite</term> should be fashioned. Having bound the figure with the appropriate seed-syllables, the practitioner should write the enemy's name upon it. Then, drive stakes <term>kīla; ritual nails or pegs used to "pin" an enemy’s power or life</term> known as "The Cuckoo" into the figure with focused intent. Using the remains of crows, vultures, and other such birds of prey—found near or within the ritual space—one should proceed. The following is the Great Incantation:

**The Great Weapon of the Lord of Beasts** <term>Mahāpāshupatāstra; a supreme and terrifying weapon of the god Shiva, invoked here for its destructive power</term>: 
Burn! Burn! <note>original: *dhaga dhaga*</note> 
Shatter! Shatter! <note>original: *photaya photaya*</note> 
O Great Wave, Break them! Break them! <note>original: *mahodarmā bhajaya bhajaya*</note>

Do it! Do it! Hail! <note>original: *kuru kuru svāhā*</note>

One should then empower the dust from the earth of an un-worshipped Shiva temple. For the target <note>original: *devadatta; a placeholder name similar to "John Doe"</note>, this will bring about a state of total exhaustion. 

By taking the bone from the left foot of a deceased man on a Tuesday, one should perform the ritual of death according to the prescribed methods. When the spirit of the deceased <note>original: *bhūta*</note> is placed with its face downward, and a stake of eight fingers in length is driven into it on a Tuesday, the enemy will undoubtedly perish through the affliction of a deadly fever <note>original: *jvara-dosha*</note>. 

Furthermore, if one takes the left part of a "village lion" <term>grāma-siṃha; a traditional poetic euphemism for a dog</term> and writes the enemy's name upon it, the enemy will be tormented by disease. Within seven days, they will suffer from the affliction of eruptive sores or pox <term>visphoṭaka; infectious skin diseases like smallpox or heavy boils</term>. 

Whether at the place of excrement or any other base location, the clay should be empowered. On the fourteenth day of the lunar cycle, one should create the figure of the man in a temple of Shiva or a cremation ground, using the enemy's name and the wood of a funeral pyre. This sends the power of the "Crow's Wing" <note>an idiomatic expression for a curse or ill omen</note> directly to the enemy to ensure their end.

Now, the signs <term>lakshana; physical or situational omens</term> of a man who has been struck by a curse and finds pleasure in sin: 
He is always devoid of righteousness and becomes a seeker of strife. He places trust in enemies and is constantly suspicious of friends. He gives away his wealth to those who follow the wrong path. These are the signs of an enemy's ruin. 

He becomes a man who is prone to weeping and speaks with a trembling voice. He becomes lustful and is easily frightened. He speaks uselessly and repeatedly about his wife and sons. He is a mortal who is fond of constant quarrels. He fears his own wife, children, and kinsmen. These are all the signs of one who has been struck.

He becomes confused and agitated, suffering from useless defeats. This is the sign of a man whose life is collapsing. He finds no sleep during the nights. He is terrified by groundless fears. These are the signs of a fatal strike. The King becomes weak, and the entire populace becomes agitated and distressed. This is a great sorrow for the ruler of the people.

<meta>This page transitions from the physical ritual of the previous page—which involved making effigies and using toxic plants—to the astrological and temporal requirements for a successful military expedition (Yatra). It focuses on the Science of the Rising Breath (Swarodaya) and the specific lunar days (Tithis) that dictate victory or certain defeat in war.</meta>

Whether daily or at a specific time, when one is joined with the proper power, one should avoid the strike of a weapon. In a dream, a strike at the root... for the fruit of the earth, for the horses and the divine. Both of these are found in the **Science of the Rising Breath** <term>Swarodaya; an ancient practice correlating the flow of breath through the nostrils with astrological timing and success in worldly actions</term>. It is said that for the destruction of enemies—their chariots, elephants, horses, and footmen—this certain path should be followed. When confused about when a strike should be performed, one must look closely. 

One should begin a campaign without the risk of a "fall" <note>original: *paten*; likely referring to an astrological descent or an ambush</note>, for without this knowledge, victory is never possible. Therefore, I shall declare the nature of the "strike" <term>ghata; an inauspicious astrological timing that leads to being "struck" or defeated</term>. Whether in one's own territory or while invading another's, the destruction of the enemy is certain if the timing is aligned. If not, the destruction of one's own three-fold army <note>original: *tri-shansainya*; the traditional divisions of an army</note> will surely follow. Whether it be a city, a gateway, a house, or a forest, the gods and demons alike are constrained by these rules of war. He who sets out at the wrong time will perish, even if he is as strong as Rama. For the destruction of the enemy, this timing has been established. Without a firm root in this knowledge, there is no success in battle, just as there is no righteousness without intention, and no god higher than the father. 

Therefore, a man should always worship the stars before departing. He should satisfy his own priests and well-wishers with garments, flowers, and various wealth, so that his mind remains bright. 

Now, hear the rules of the journey <term>Yatra-vidhi</term> for one's own benefit and victory, according to the lunar days:

*   The **First Day** <term>Pratipada; the first day of the lunar fortnight</term> is said to strike at the soul. 
*   The **Second Day** <term>Dwitiya</term> causes affliction and distress.
*   The **Third Day** <term>Tritiya</term> brings loss, even at night.
*   The **Fourth Day** <term>Chaturthi</term> is the giver of death.
*   On the **Fifth Day** <term>Panchami</term>, one should never set out.
*   The **Sixth Day** <term>Shashthi</term> brings strikes from weapons and loss of garments. 
*   ...The **Ninth Day** <term>Navami</term> leads to the destruction of sons.
*   The **Tenth Day** <term>Dashami</term> is the giver of sorrow.
*   The **Eleventh Day** <term>Ekadashi</term> provides fever.
*   On the **Twelfth Day** <term>Dwadashi</term>, if one goes out, there is a "strike on the elephants" <note>original: *gaja-ghata*; interpreted here as a loss of the heavy cavalry/power</note>.
*   The **Full Moon** <term>Purnima</term> is declared to be inauspicious for warriors.
*   The **New Moon** <term>Darsha/Amavasya</term> causes the destruction of all aims and wealth.

The period of **Bhadra** <note>an inauspicious astrological period occurring on specific lunar days</note> is said to be a "trap," and cruel astrological combinations act the same way. When the Moon's path is unfavorable, no victory is produced. Loss occurs when departing during the **Yamyas** <note>periods associated with Yama, the god of death</note>. Even if the sun is in a favorable position, if the timing of the "strike" <term>ghata</term> is active, the result is sorrow. 

In the **Chitra** constellation, sickness is removed, but in **Svati**, it gives pain. In the **Uttara** constellations, success is born. When the lunar day, the day of the week, and the constellation <term>nakshatra</term> are all corrupted, the journey results in disaster. Even if the gods themselves were to go out during these evil times, they would face defeat.

# 2. The Ritual of the Lineage

<margin>
Na-
Vai-
</margin>
If one sets out through negligence at the moment of an inauspicious strike <note>original: *ghata*; referring to the astrological "blow" mentioned on the previous page</note>, destruction will arise from those faults. With great effort, one should guard against the loss of wealth, the destruction of servants, and the ruin of elephants and sons. According to the prescribed order of the physicians <note>original: *vaidya*; here likely meaning experts or learned masters of ritual</note>, one should establish the ritual space in the four directions. Using six types of wood, such as the Sun-tree and the Khadira <note>original: *Arka* (Calotropis gigantea) and *Khadira* (Acacia catechu); both used in sacred fires for their purifying and protective properties</note>, along with the sacrificial sticks for the **Nine Planets** <term>Navagraha; the celestial bodies that influence human destiny</term>, one should perform the rite with a steady mind.

Prepare a mixture of milk-rice <term>payasa; a sweet ritual porridge</term> and sesame seeds, and perform the ritual of expansion. With the **Mantra that Conquers Death** <term>Mrityunjaya Mantra; a powerful chant dedicated to Shiva to ward off physical and spiritual death</term>, one should invoke Brahma, the Goddess Gauri, and the sun. These should be honored separately, along with the horses and the enemies' weapons. Offerings should be made to the Goddess Durga with the prescribed hymns and to the Guardians of the World <term>Lokapala; the deities ruling the eight directions</term>. One should arrange the ritual for victory at the gateway using the hymns to Durga.

In the ritual fire, one should make separate offerings using the **Mantra that Conquers Death** one hundred and eight times, using sesame seeds and clarified butter <note>original: *ajya*; ghee</note>. The following items should be prepared: oranges, betel nuts, black pepper, ginger, and round citron fruits; also bananas, coconuts, musk, and curd, along with barley and grain. In the presence of the assembly, offer sweets: **Modakas** <note>sweet dumplings</note>, fried cakes, and bread filled with ghee. For the satisfaction of the Planets and the fierce form of the Great Destroyer <note>original: *Kalarudra*; Shiva in his role as the ender of time</note>, these various milk-based offerings should be made. 

To please the deity and for the success of the death-conquering chant, one must offer <term>land and grain</term> and <term>fresh grass</term>. When the ritual fire has been properly tended and the red cow has been honored, one should use the ritual ladle to offer the final, complete sacrifice <note>original: *purnahuti*; the "full offering" that concludes a Vedic ritual</note>. A wise man should then distribute food and clothing to all beings present. After this, one must perform the **Ritual Anointing** <term>Abhisheka; a ceremonial bathing of the king or an object to instill divine power</term> with the water from the consecrated pot. Peace will then prevail.

Even a man standing in the very mouth of death can be transformed. However, a ritual performed without the proper steps, without the correct mantras, or without the **ritual fee** <term>dakshina; the payment given to priests that validates the sacrifice</term> is one's own greatest enemy. There is no foe equal to a ritual performed poorly. Thus ends the section on the "strike." In this way, the king achieves victory in battle over elephants and enemies. If one is surrounded by the "noose of the strike," this is the **Joy of the Consecrated Pot.**

This particular genre, "Victory of the King," is a manual used to determine <term>Muhurta: auspicious moments</term> for a ruler’s success in governance, coronations, and warfare.</note> <-

<meta>Following the identification label (No. 5458 — Gh), this page marks the beginning of the actual manuscript text. It opens with traditional Sanskrit invocations to deities of wisdom and success, followed by the introductory verses establishing the theological and practical purpose of the work.</meta>

->**Salutation to the auspicious Ganesha**<- <note>original: "॥ श्री गणेशाय नमः ॥" (Śrī Gaṇeśāya Namaḥ). It is a standard practice in Hindu manuscripts to begin with a prayer to Ganesha, the elephant-headed deity who is the remover of obstacles and the patron of letters.</note>
->**Salutation to the auspicious Goddess Sharada**<- <note>original: "॥ श्री सारदा देव्यै नमः ॥" (Śrī Sāradā Devyai Namaḥ). Sharada is another name for Saraswati, the goddess of learning, music, and the arts, invoked here to bless the author's intellect.</note>

Victorious is the Lord <note>original: "ईशानः" (Īśānaḥ), an epithet of the god Shiva meaning 'The Ruler' or 'The Master'.</note>, who is the cause of the birth, preservation, and dissolution of the <term>three worlds: the traditional Vedic cosmology consisting of Earth (Bhumi), the Atmosphere (Antariksha), and the Heavens (Svarga)</term>. Victorious is that Supreme Lord, as well as primordial Nature and the Great Intellect, and the rest. || 1 || <note>original: "प्रकृतिर्महदादिकं" (Prakṛtir-mahad-ādikaṃ). These are technical terms from Samkhya philosophy referring to the primary components of the universe: <term>Prakriti: the primal creative matter</term> and <term>Mahat: the cosmic intelligence</term>.</note>

Therefore, victory should be secured for kings by the wise through the zodiacal signs. Having referred to the <term>science of auspicious timing: Muhurta-shastra, the branch of astrology focused on selecting the most favorable moment to begin an activity</term>, I shall now describe the characteristics of victory. || 2 ||

By knowing this, a mortal becomes free from blemish and a destroyer of enemies. That king, desiring victory, shall always attain success. || 3 ||

<summary>The opening page of the manuscript "The Victory of the King," featuring traditional invocations to Ganesha and Sharada and an introductory verse framing the astrological work as a guide for monarchs to achieve military and political success through the science of auspicious timing.</summary>

<keywords>Ganesha, Sharada, Shiva, Samkhya philosophy, Muhurta, Royal Success, Astrology, Sanskrit Literature</keywords>

## **The Victory of Kings** (**Astrology**)
<note>original: "Rājavijayaḥ" (The Victory of Kings) and "Jyotiṣa" (Astrology). This title refers to a work within the field of <term>Jyotisha: the traditional Indian science of astronomy and astrology</term>. The "Victory of Kings" is a specialized branch of this science, often focusing on calculating the most auspicious times for a ruler to wage war, sign treaties, or undergo coronation ceremonies to ensure success.</note>

## 52 Folios
<note>original: "patrāṇi." This indicates the count of individual leaves. In traditional Indian bookmaking, these were often unbound sheets of paper or palm leaf kept together between wooden boards or within a cloth wrap.</note>

## **The Victory of Kings** (**Astrology**)

# The Jewel Garland (**<term>Jyotisha: the traditional Indian system of astrology and astronomy</term>**)
<note>original: "श्रीरत्नमाला (ज्योतिष)" (Śrīratnamālā — Jyotiṣa). The *Ratnamala* (Garland of Jewels) is a famous 11th-century treatise on electional astrology written by the scholar Sripati. It is widely used to determine auspicious times for various life events and rituals.</note>

-> 14 Folios <-
<note>original: "पत्राणि १४" (patrāṇi 14). This indicates the manuscript consists of 14 individual leaves of paper or palm leaf.</note>

-> **Incomplete** <-
<note>original: "अपूर्णम्" (apūrṇam). Unlike the previous manuscript, this copy is missing sections and does not contain the full text of the *Ratnamala*.</note>

## Salutations to the Illustrious Ganesha
<note>original: "श्रीगणेशाय नमः" (Śrīgaṇeśāya namaḥ). This is a traditional invocation found at the beginning of Hindu manuscripts, seeking the blessing of Lord Ganesha, the remover of obstacles, to ensure the successful completion of the work.</note>

Now begins the Jewel Garland.

<margin>The First Chapter</margin>

Having bowed to the Sun, the Moon, and Mercury,
And then to those named Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn;
To Rahu and Ketu, who grant the expansion of the intellect,
I shall speak the **<term>Jewel Garland</term>**, the essence of the sacred sciences. || 1 ||
<note>original: "प्रणिपत्य रविं शशिनं च बुधं / गुरुशुक्रशनैश्वरसंज्ञमथो । / राहुं च केतुं मतिविस्तृतिदं / वक्ष्ये रत्नमाला शास्त्रसारम् ॥ १ ॥" This opening verse is a salutation to the nine planets (Navagraha). In Indian astrology, these celestial bodies are believed to influence human destiny, and the author pays homage to them before distilling the "essence" of astrological knowledge into this text.</note>

->**Now begins the writing of *The Victory of Kings***<- <note>original: "॥ अथ राजविजयो लिख्यते ॥" (Atha Rājavijayo likhyate). This is the formal opening statement of the text, signaling the start of the specific treatise after the initial blessing.</note>

This science of <term>astrology</term> <note>original: "ज्योति:शास्त्र" (Jyotiḥ-śāstra). Literally "The Science of Light," this field encompasses astronomy, mathematics, and the interpretation of celestial movements to predict human and political outcomes.</note> was proclaimed by **Brahma**, the seer of the fundamental truths of reality, out of a desire for the welfare of the whole world and the longing for the victory of kings. (1)

Having bowed to the imperishable **Sun**, who is the cause of the origin of the universe, I shall speak the established truths of astrology for the purpose of the victory of kings. (2) <note>original: "मार्तण्ड" (Mārtaṇḍa) refers to the Sun. In Indian astrology, the Sun represents the soul, authority, and the king; therefore, invoking the Sun is particularly appropriate for a text focused on royal success.</note>

->**Auspiciousness**<- <note>original: "मङ्गलम्" (Maṅgalam). A marker indicating a benediction or a successful start to the endeavor.</note>

## Folios: 52 <note>original: "पत्राणि ५२" (Patrāṇi 52). In manuscript studies, a 'folio' refers to a single leaf of paper or palm leaf; this indicates the physical length of the document.</note>

# The Victory of Kings (<term>Astrology</term>)
<note>The title *Rājavijayaḥ* translates to "The Victory of Kings." This work belongs to the genre of <term>Jyotiṣa</term>—the traditional Indian system of astrology and astronomy. In a royal context, this refers to "mundane astrology," used by court advisors to predict the outcomes of military campaigns and identify the most auspicious moments for state ceremonies and battles.</note>

# The Victory of Kings (<term>Astrology</term>)
<note>original: "Rājavijayaḥ (Jyotiṣa)". The title literally translates to "The Victory of Kings." It belongs to the branch of <term>Jyotiṣa</term> (traditional Indian astrology and astronomy) known as mundane or political astrology. Such texts were vital to royal courts, used to calculate the most auspicious moments for military campaigns, coronations, and significant state decisions to ensure success and stability.</note>

# 1. The Victory of Kings

->**Salutations to the Illustrious Lord Ganesha**<-
<note>original: "śrī-gaṇeśāya namaḥ". This is a traditional opening invocation to Ganesha, the elephant-headed deity of wisdom and the remover of obstacles, meant to ensure the work is completed without hindrance.</note>

Having bowed before the Support of the Universe—the God who is saluted even by the gods—I shall speak of the charming science known as the **Victory of Kings**. (1)
<note>original: "Rājavijaya-saṃjñaṃ". This refers to the title of the treatise, indicating its focus on the success and triumph of a monarch.</note>

By the mere examination of this work, the certain outcome of all tasks is determined; it ensures the victory of kings and the total destruction of all enemies. (2)

Without the **science of light** (<term>Astrology</term>), a king can never be a conqueror of his enemies; for this reason, the science of light is declared by the wise to be the most excellent of all sciences. (3)
<note>original: "Jyotiḥ-śāstra". This term encompasses the study of celestial bodies and their influence on human affairs. In this context, it is presented as a vital strategic tool for a ruler, equivalent to military intelligence.</note>

<summary>This opening page of the "Rājavijaya" invokes divine blessings and asserts that astrology is an essential science for any ruler seeking to defeat enemies and ensure the success of their kingdom.</summary>

<keywords>Ganesha, Rājavijaya, Jyotiṣa, astrology, statecraft, royal victory, Sanskrit literature, Mangalacharana</keywords>

## The Victory of Kings (Astrology)
<note>original: "Rājavijayaḥ (Jyotiṣa)." The title *Rājavijaya* translates literally as "The Victory of Kings." It is categorized under <term>Jyotiṣa</term>—the traditional Indian science of time-keeping, astronomy, and astrology. This specific work would have served as a manual for court astrologers to calculate the most favorable moments for royal activities, such as declarations of war, coronations, or diplomatic journeys.</note>

-> 52 Folios <-
<note>original: "Patrāṇi." This refers to the physical leaves of the manuscript. In the context of North Indian collections like this one, these are typically loose-leaf paper folios held together between protective boards.</note>

-> Complete <-
<note>original: "Sampūrṇam." A standard archival notation signifying that the manuscript contains the full text from beginning to end, rather than being a fragment or a partial copy.</note>

# **The Victory of Kings**
## (<term>Astrology</term>)
<note>original: "राजविजयः (ज्योतिष)" (Rājavijayaḥ [Jyotiṣa]). The title *Rajavijaya* translates to "The Victory of Kings," suggesting this text focuses on the use of celestial omens and timing to ensure success in governance and warfare. It is classified under <term>Jyotiṣa</term>—the traditional Indian science of light which encompasses astronomy and the calculation of auspicious moments.</note>

-> 52 Folios <-

# **Salutations to the venerable Lord Ganesha**
<note>original: "श्रीगणेशाय नमः" (*Śrī-Gaṇeśāya namaḥ*). It is customary in Hindu manuscripts to begin a work with a salute to Ganesha, the elephant-headed deity who is the remover of obstacles and the patron of letters and learning.</note>

**Now begins the writing of the book "The Victory of Kings."**
<note>original: "अथ राजविजयग्रन्थो लिख्यते" (*atha rājavijaya-grantho likhyate*). The word "Atha" signifies a formal beginning, often used in sacred or scientific texts to transition from the introductory rituals to the core subject matter.</note>

<margin>Auspicious Invocation</margin>
<note>original: "मङ्गलाचरणम्" (*Maṅgalācaraṇam*). A marginal note indicating that these opening verses serve as a prayer to ensure the successful completion of the work.</note>

Having bowed before the Lord who is the foundation of the world and who is saluted by all the gods, I shall now expound upon the **Astrology** (<term>Jyotiṣa</term>—the science of celestial light and influence) regarding **Royal Victory**, which serves to increase the power and prosperity of kings. || 1 ||
<note>original: "प्रणम्य जगदाधारं देवं देवैर्नमस्कृतम् । वक्ष्ये विजयराजस्य ज्योतिषं राजवर्धनम् ॥ १ ॥"</note>

This treatise, which describes the characteristics and signs of victory, is concise and easy to understand. It is intended to increase the joy of kings and to bestow success in all their undertakings. || 2 ||
<note>original: "अल्पग्रन्थं सुबोधं च राज्ञां प्रीतिविवर्धनम् । सर्वकार्येषु जयदं शास्त्रं विजयलक्षणम् ॥ २ ॥"</note>

<summary>This page marks the formal opening of the "Victory of Kings" manuscript, featuring a devotional salutation to Ganesha and two introductory verses that describe the text as a concise, easy-to-understand astrological guide for ensuring royal success.</summary>

<keywords>Ganesha, invocation, Royal Victory, Rājavijaya, Jyotiṣa, astrology, kingship, statecraft, Sanskrit verse</keywords>

## The Victory of the King <gloss>Astrology</gloss>

In a royal context, these texts were essential for calculating the most auspicious moments for coronations, military campaigns, and political alliances.</note>
